Remedy: Wi-Fi Calling ($0); fallback: Extender ($175)'. 0:55 the call connects. 1:10 audit scoreboard: 'X of Y channels offered a handset first' (corporate vs franchise vs AI chat). 1:30 CFO tile: 10,000 converted saves = $6.7M, and bundles churn 40% less. 1:50 the ask: a 30-day pilot at one save desk. Poster table: judges run Save Scan on an Android phone, with a map of the 50 rooms and the channel audit table.
- **Why unique:** It puts the CEO's own Apr 2026 anecdote into operation and checks in the field whether the new policy reached the counter after the Aug 2026 franchise handoff and the move to AI care. It is a tool for Verizon's save desk, not another student app. Other teams cannot copy the evidence (honest channel audits plus RF in rooms students live in). It differs from our explored Glass Ceiling: the unit is the save decision and its cost, not a building's attenuation.
- **Biggest risk:** Verizon may already route saves this way: Schulman cites 'hundreds of micro initiatives' and micro-segmentation. If the audit shows the channels already offer place fixes, the pitch shrinks to the Save Scan tool. iOS hides RF metrics, so the full scan is Android-only (iPhones get Field Test readings or a guided checklist). The sample is small. Channel audits must stay honest and unrecorded, and they could irritate Verizon retail. There is partial overlap with the explored Glass Ceiling (indoor loss).

## I12: Paid-Off: the subsidy-free switch (lens cost_lines, pillar Pillar 2: Cost & Sustainability Transformation (credit losses and equipment loss). Pillar 3 is secondary (onboarding experience)., self-score 56)

**One-liner:** Find the students whose phones are already paid off and unlocked, and make bringing that phone to Verizon a 10-minute eSIM switch instead of a new financed handset. The new youth line then arrives with no device receivable, no handset loss and no store visit.

- **Problem evidence:** (1) FY2025 10-K: customers with tenure under 210 days caused $884M of $1,356M in device-payment-plan gross write-offs (about 65%) while holding $6,020M of $32,951M in receivables (about 18%). Each 0.25% change in the loss rate moves bad debt by $167M. Total credit-loss provisions were $2,114M (https://www.sec.gov/Archives/edgar/data/732712/000073271226000007/vz-20251231.htm). (2) Equipment was sold at about -$3.5B in FY2025 (cost was 113.8% of equipment revenue; derived from https://www.sec.gov/Archives/edgar/data/732712/000073271226000003/a2025q4exhibit99.htm). (3) Q2 2026 call (Jul 24 2026): 'every new account onto Simplicity is basically subsidy-free', 'a lot more of the youth market', and net new accounts '31% greater than we forecasted' (https://www.verizon.com/about/sites/default/files/2026-08/vz_2q2026_transcript_072426rm.pdf). (4) Simplicity costs $45, or $30 per line for switchers (Jun 16 2026, https://www.verizon.com/about/news/verizon-puts-customers-first). (5) Schulman's churn cause #2 is 'friction in the process, whether it's onboarding, the billing, when they call our customer service' (Jan 30 2026, https://www.verizon.com/about/sites/default/files/2026-02/vz_4q25_transcript_013026r.pdf). Apr 27 2026: 'We will encourage more volume into digital sales and service channels, which lowers costs' (Q1 transcript). (6) Instant eSIM activation would make 69% of Gen Z more likely to switch, and Gen Z leaves family plans at ages 19-22 (OXIO, Mar 2026, https://www.telecompetitor.com/most-consumers-consider-changing-mobile-plans-once-per-year-survey/). (7) Carrier locks gate BYOD: under DA 26-43 (Jan 12 2026) Verizon now follows the CTIA code and unlocks postpaid phones 'after it is paid in full' (https://docs.fcc.gov/public/attachments/DA-26-43A1.pdf).
- **Verizon tech:** eSIM remote SIM provisioning (GSMA SGP.22, SM-DP+). Verizon's BYOD compatibility check (IMEI/TAC: VoLTE and bands including C-band n77). Number port-in through NPAC with a Number Transfer PIN. eSIM transfer and entitlement (GSMA TS.43 ODSA). Wi-Fi Calling E911 address provisioning. Visible and Total Wireless, which run on Verizon's network and provisioning stack, serve as cheap test lines.
- **Verizon money:** Money lines: credit-loss provision (new-customer write-offs) and equipment loss. Derived, rough and labeled: (a) equipment loss averaged about 13.8% of equipment revenue in FY2025, about $110-140 on an $800-1,000 phone (Simplicity may already have lowered this); (b) new-customer gross write-offs equalled about 15% of the year-end new-customer device balance ($884M / $6,020M). That is an upper bound, about $120-150 of expected loss per $1,000 financed to a new customer. So each new line that brings its own paid-off phone instead of financing one avoids roughly $200-290, plus an unpriced store visit or care contact (Verizon does not disclose cost per contact). Campus scale: 500 BYOD switches is about $0.1-0.15M. The value is a national youth-acquisition playbook that changes the financing mix. It is not a campus revenue line.
- **Dated anchor:** Jul 24 2026 Q2 call: Simplicity accounts 'basically subsidy-free', youth inflow, accounts 31% above forecast. Jun 16 2026: Simplicity at $30 per line for switchers. The FY2025 10-K (filed Feb 17 2026) has the 210-day write-off table. The Q3 2026 results (Oct 26 2026, before the video deadline) will refresh the new-account and credit-loss numbers.
- **Primary data:** (1) Carrier Lock census, N of about 300 consenting students recruited through classes, RUSA and residence halls. Each sends a screenshot of iPhone Settings > General > About > Carrier Lock (or the Android SIM-test result), plus phone model, paid off yes/no, whose account (own or parent) and current carrier. This gives the share of Rutgers phones that are ready to bring and Verizon-compatible. All data is self-reported, screenshots are deleted after coding, and there is no CPNI. (2) Switch Stopwatch: 12-20 scripted eSIM activations and port-ins on team test lines across Verizon, Visible, Total Wireless and two competitor controls (about $400 of plans). We record minutes to first call, number of steps, a failure taxonomy (port PIN, eSIM download, iMessage/RCS re-registration, visual voicemail, Wi-Fi Calling E911 address) and whether a human was needed. (3) Diaries from 10-15 volunteers who are switching anyway.
- **Demo:** Video. A student checks 'Carrier Lock: No SIM restrictions' on her paid-off iPhone 13, which sits on a parent's plan. She scans a QR code at a campus table, and Simplicity is live by eSIM in N minutes with her number ported (the stopwatch runs on screen). Split screen with the alternative, a new $1,000 phone on a 36-month plan. Tile: receivable $0 vs $1,000. Census result: 'X% of Rutgers phones could switch today without financing'. Failure-taxonomy bar chart and the fix for each failure. Poster: a live switch-readiness checker plus the stopwatch dataset.
- **Why unique:** It attacks the most concentrated loss pool in the 10-K (65% of device write-offs from customers under 210 days on 18% of receivables) through the financing mix, not credit scoring. It uses data Verizon cannot see: the lock and payoff status of phones sitting on other carriers' and parents' accounts. The angle is domestic and about finance, which keeps it apart from the explored Day-Zero Line and First Number (international, credit-invisible).
- **Biggest risk:** Verizon may prefer financed devices because payment-plan balances reduce churn, and BYOD switchers may churn faster; 90-day retention cannot be measured by Nov 8. Network-technology content is thin (provisioning, not RAN). The youth/eSIM space is crowded, with T-Mobile student plans and Visible graduate offers. Census recruitment and honest self-reporting are needed.

## I13: Priority Gap: where the deprioritized plan breaks on campus (lens cost_lines, pillar Pillar 3: Next-Gen Products & Services to Enhance Customer Experience, with Pillar 2 secondary (capex targeting)., self-score 54)

**One-liner:** Paired phones on Visible ($25, 'may be temporarily slowed when the network is congested'), Visible+ and Simplicity ('no network tiers') measure exactly where and when Rutgers students on the base plan get slowed. Verizon uses the map to offer the upgrade at the moment of pain, or to add capacity only where it saves prepaid churn.

- **Problem evidence:** (1) Q2 2026: core prepaid churn was 3.59% a month against 0.92% for postpaid phones (consumer 0.84%). Core prepaid connections were 19.351M with ARPU of $33.37. Prepaid phone net adds were only 24K in Q2, and 94K vs 134K for H1 (-29.9%) (https://www.sec.gov/Archives/edgar/data/732712/000073271226000040/a2026q2exhibit99.htm). (2) Visible's base plan says data 'may be temporarily slowed when the network is congested'. Visible+ ($35) and Visible+ Pro ($45) carry premium data (NerdWallet, https://www.nerdwallet.com/article/finance/visible-phone-service; tier change per PhoneScoop Apr 2025, https://www.phonescoop.com/articles/article.php?a=23485). (3) Simplicity (Jun 16 2026): 'there are no network tiers; everyone gets Verizon's best 5G network' (https://www.verizon.com/about/news/verizon-puts-customers-first). (4) Verizon routes the graduation moment through Visible ($20/mo, May 18 2026, https://www.verizon.com/about/news/visible-graduation-season-offers-class-of-2026). (5) Campus peak loads, e.g. 48,650 at SHI Stadium on Oct 3 2026 (secondary: https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2026_Rutgers_Scarlet_Knights_football_team).
- **Verizon tech:** QoS-class (QCI/5QI) and ARP-based prioritization in Verizon's LTE and 5G NSA/SA RAN; C-band 5G Ultra Wideband; cell congestion. The proposed product is a network-native, time-limited priority boost that the policy function (PCRF/PCF) applies per session. That is roadmap only; we do not claim it exists. 5G SA slicing is mentioned as the future path (Verizon slicing blog, Aug 25 2026, https://www.verizon.com/about/news/beyond-the-silo-5g-network-slicing).
- **Verizon money:** Money lines: prepaid churn and ARPU, with capex targeting secondary. Derived and labeled: 0.01 pp of core prepaid churn is about 1,935 connections a month (19.351M x 0.0001), about $65K of ARPU kept per monthly cohort per month. Moving a base user to Visible+ adds $10 a month. Moving them to Simplicity shifts a 3.59%-a-month churner into a segment churning about 0.84% a month. The capacity is spent only where the priority gap clusters. Honest size: Visible subscriber counts are not disclosed, and the campus effect is small. The deliverable is the method and the place-by-hour map.
- **Dated anchor:** Jul 24 2026 Q2 8-K: prepaid churn 3.59%, prepaid phone net adds down 29.9% in H1. Jun 16 2026: Simplicity has 'no network tiers'. May 18 2026: Visible graduate offer. Oct 3 2026: SHI attendance 48,650.
- **Primary data:** Four identical Android phones: Visible base, Visible+ or Pro, Verizon Simplicity, and a T-Mobile or Mint control (about $1,200 for phones and two months of plans). Every 10 minutes they run scripted tests (throughput, latency, video start and stall) at 8 campus places for about 14 days: Busch Student Center at noon, Livingston dining, College Ave Student Center, the LX and A buses between campuses, large lecture halls at class change, a dorm at 11 pm, Cook/Douglass, and SHI on any home date. The output is the 'priority gap' (base vs premium) by place and hour. A survey of about 150 student Visible users covers plan, satisfaction and switching intent, with no CPNI.
- **Demo:** Video: two identical phones side by side on the LX bus at 12:10. The Visible base video stalls while Simplicity plays. A heat map shows the priority gap across campus hour by hour. A tile shows that base users in red zones get a one-tap offer, with the churn math. Poster: a live dashboard streams the four phones during the poster session, so the poster crowd itself becomes the test.
- **Why unique:** It uses Verizon's own 2026 plan design, a no-tier postpaid plan beside a deprioritized prepaid tier, and measures the gap where youth are densest. No other team will buy four lines and log the QoS gap by place and hour. The framing is prepaid churn economics, not fan experience.
- **Biggest risk:** The result may be null: C-band capacity may leave little gap except on game days, and the remaining home dates before Nov 8 are unverified. 'Pay so you are not slowed' can read badly to student and faculty judges. Verizon already sees throughput per QCI by cell, so the student data mainly adds the customer-perceived view. Competitor controls must be fair. The stadium angle sits close to crowded stadium ideas.

## I14: Copper Harvest: remove, don't transfer (lens cost_lines, pillar Pillar 2: Cost & Sustainability Transformation (copper O&M and copper monetization), with Pillar 1 (AI vision automation)., self-score 50)

**One-liner:** Students photograph every pole around the retiring Piscataway central office. A vision model tags telco copper, fiber, cable size and double poles, then ranks spans where, once lines are migrated, Verizon should take down and recycle the copper instead of paying to transfer it to a new pole.

- **Problem evidence:** (1) CFO Skiadas, Apr 27 2026: the cost program includes 'continuing to decommission legacy elements in the network, and that includes copper and recycling that copper and also monetizing that copper' (https://www.verizon.com/about/sites/default/files/2026-06/vz_1q26_transcript_042726rm.pdf). (2) Copper retirement saved about $180M a year from 4.5M circuits and 36 COs, 'in part driven by a reduction in technician dispatches' (Investor Day 2022, https://www.verizon.com/about/sites/default/files/2022-03/Investor-Day-2022-Transcript.pdf). Emptied COs become data centers that 'sold out capability in 24 hours' (Jul 24 2026 Q2 transcript). (3) Verizon NJ notice 2026-03-A-NJ (Sept 8 2026): copper retires at 'all remaining locations' in 62 wire centers, including Piscataway PSWYNJPI, Metuchen, Dunellen, South River and Fords, 'on or after December 10, 2026' (https://www.verizon.com/about/sites/default/files/verizon-new-jersey-copper-retirement-notice-2026-0908.pdf). (4) FCC 26-19 rules took effect Oct 15 2026 with no objection procedure for copper retirement (https://www.govinfo.gov/content/pkg/FR-2026-10-01/pdf/2026-20192.pdf). (5) NJ S469 / A1454 (2026 session) would require attachers to remove attachments from a double pole 'within 10 business days' of the electric utility's notice (https://pub.njleg.gov/Bills/2026/S0500/469_I1.HTM). (6) Comparables, secondary and UNVERIFIED (search snippets only): AT&T has reportedly recycled over 32,000 t of copper since 2021, and Telus expects about $500M from its copper program.
- **Verizon tech:** Verizon outside plant: aerial copper feeder and distribution cable vs Fios fiber (slack-loop 'snowshoes', fiber tags, splice cases); copper retirement under 47 CFR 51.333; the NJUNS pole-transfer workflow. Computer vision on geotagged phone imagery, with cable diameter estimated against the messenger strand as a scale reference.
- **Verizon money:** Money lines: copper O&M and recycling proceeds. The O&M benchmark is about $5M per converted CO-year, derived from the 2022 figure ($180M / 36). That depends on full migration and is NOT credited to this idea. Credited: (a) transfer dispatches avoided at double poles in retiring wire centers. The industry estimate is $150-600 per dispatch, UNVERIFIED for Verizon. (b) Scrap. The physics is that a 24 AWG pair holds about 2.5 lb of copper per 1,000 ft, so a 600-pair cable is about 1,500 lb per 1,000 ft; the net recovery price after removal is UNVERIFIED. Honest size: the campus-area pilot is worth tens of thousands of dollars. The value is a field-verified method that scales to 62 NJ wire centers and Verizon's 'thousands' of COs.
- **Dated anchor:** Sept 8 2026 NJ copper notice (62 wire centers). Oct 15 2026: FCC 26-19 rules effective. Apr 27 2026: Skiadas on monetizing copper. Jul 24 2026: COs being converted to data centers. NJ S469/A1454 double-pole bills (2026 session). Caveat: WC 26-227 was pulled from auto-grant on Sept 25 2026 (DA 26-1038), so the timing of removal is uncertain.
- **Primary data:** A dated (Oct-Nov 2026) geotagged photo census of about 500 poles along about 15 route-miles within about 2 km of 4 Skiles Ave (Hoes Lane/RBHS, the Busch perimeter, River Rd). A control area in the New Brunswick wire center, which is not retiring, is included; wire-center boundaries are inferred, not confirmed. About 200 images are hand-labeled (attachment class, double pole, fiber present, cable diameter) to train and score the classifier. Outputs: double poles where telco copper is the last attachment, spans with fiber overlay (migration-ready) and estimated copper pounds. Photos are taken only from the public right-of-way, with no contact with crews and no CPNI. Fresh, dated ground truth beats Street View imagery that is years old.
- **Demo:** Video: a student photographs a double pole on Hoes Lane. The app draws boxes: 'telco copper, about 600-pair', 'fiber slack loop present', 'old pole still carrying copper'. Verdict: 'REMOVE after migration, don't transfer; about 0.9 t of copper in this span'. The map zooms out to 500 poles with tiles for copper tonnage and avoidable transfer dispatches. Poster: judges upload a pole photo and get a classification, next to an accuracy table against the hand labels.
- **Why unique:** It turns a named line of the CFO's 2026 cost program ('monetizing that copper') into a field-verified worklist, and ties copper retirement to NJ's double-pole pressure. No LLM-default team will walk the poles. It is not a lifeline idea, which keeps it clear of Last Copper.
- **Biggest risk:** Verizon already has outside-plant records, so judges may see little value beyond checking records. Owner (Verizon vs cable or CLEC) and pair count are error-prone to judge from the ground. Copper cannot come down until the paused discontinuances finish. Scrap economics after removal cost may be thin. Lead-sheathed cable is a sensitive topic and must be excluded. Content scores on outside plant, not 5G.

## I15: Pays Twice: spend the shrinking upgrade budget where it also buys network (lens cost_lines, pillar Pillar 2: Cost & Sustainability Transformation (equipment loss and capex efficiency)., self-score 49)

**One-liner:** Verizon has cut upgrades 27%. Spend what is left on the phones that cannot reach C-band or 5G SA, and fill those upgrades with certified refurbished C-band phones ('last year's model', in Schulman's words). Paired-phone tests on campus show how much network and customer experience each subsidy dollar buys.

- **Problem evidence:** (1) Equipment was sold at about -$3.5B in FY2025 (https://www.sec.gov/Archives/edgar/data/732712/000073271226000003/a2025q4exhibit99.htm). (2) Q2 2026: upgrade rate 2.6% vs 3.6% (https://www.sec.gov/Archives/edgar/data/732712/000073271226000040/a2026q2exhibit99.htm). Upgrade volumes are down nearly 27%, and the 10-Q cites a $1.1B equipment-cost drop from a 'strategic decrease in upgrades' (https://www.sec.gov/Archives/edgar/data/732712/000073271226000046/vz-20260630.htm). (3) Schulman, Apr 27 2026: 'can they have a handset that -- it's last year's model that's been refurbished ... Many of them ... are keeping their handsets longer' (Q1 transcript). (4) Simplicity, Jun 16 2026: 'everyone gets Verizon's best 5G network', but only if the phone can use it (https://www.verizon.com/about/news/verizon-puts-customers-first). (5) The 2026 capex guide is $16.0-16.5B (Q2 2026). C-band was on track for 80-90% of planned sites by YE2025, nearly all SA-capable (Q2 2025 call; secondary source: https://bullfincher.io/companies/verizon-communications/earnings-call/fy2025-q2).
- **Verizon tech:** C-band (n77) 5G Ultra Wideband vs the LTE/DSS layers; the 5G SA core and network slicing (Verizon slicing blog, Aug 25 2026); VoNR; device capability by TAC; Verizon Certified Pre-Owned devices. UNVERIFIED this pass and to be checked on Verizon device pages before filming: that iPhone 12-generation and older phones lack US C-band support.
- **Verizon money:** Money lines: equipment loss and capex. (1) Equipment: the price gap between a refurbished C-band phone and a new flagship on each targeted upgrade. Public CPO prices are to be collected; we expect hundreds of dollars per upgrade, UNVERIFIED. (2) Capex: GB a month moved from LTE to C-band per upgraded device, times the marginal $/GB of LTE capacity. That is a labeled assumption, and Verizon holds the real value. Honest size: unknown until Verizon plugs in its device-mix data. The student work supplies the in-field measurement and the targeting policy.
- **Dated anchor:** Jul 24 2026 Q2 call and Jul 31 2026 10-Q (strategic upgrade cut). Jun 16 2026 Simplicity ('everyone gets Verizon's best 5G network'). Aug 25 2026 Verizon 5G SA slicing blog.
- **Primary data:** Paired-device tests at 30 campus spots at 3 times of day: an old phone without C-band vs a refurbished C-band phone vs a current flagship, on the same plan type at the same moment. We log serving band (LTE/DSS/C-band/mmWave), RSRP/SINR, downlink/uplink throughput and latency (Android logging, iPhone Field Test where possible; about $500 of used phones). A campus device-age census (N of about 300, self-reported model, battery health, upgrade intent) estimates the share of student phones without C-band. A scrape of CPO prices completes the data.
- **Demo:** Video: three phones on a Busch bench run the same download. The old phone is stuck on LTE at X Mbps, while the refurbished C-band phone runs at Y Mbps, close to the flagship's Z Mbps. A tile shows the refurbished phone delivering nearly all the network benefit at a fraction of the cost. A policy card reads 'upgrade the C-band-less tail first, with last year's model'. Poster: a live three-phone race.
- **Why unique:** It joins the Consumer group's upgrade budget to RAN capacity planning in GN&T, the judges' own organization, using Schulman's own 'last year's model, refurbished' line. It can be measured on campus with $500 of phones.
- **Biggest risk:** Verizon already knows its device mix and per-layer load from network data, so the student data is illustrative. Students' phones skew new, so the campus tail is small. Capacity value may be near zero where LTE is not congested. Model-level C-band claims must be checked. There is overlap with the explored ReCircle (device lifecycle).

## I16: Still Works?: the device check the FCC stopped requiring on Oct 15, 2026 (lens customer_experience, pillar Pillar 3: customer experience and churn at a forced migration. Pillar 1 automation runs the post-cutover test., self-score 64)

**One-liner:** Before Verizon moves a home or small business off copper, a 3-minute check tells the customer which plugged-in devices (alarm panel, medical-alert base, fax, TTY, dial-up card terminal) will work on each Verizon replacement. It routes the devices that won't to a fix. After the cutover it sends one test signal and returns a green receipt. A Rutgers bench backs it with success rates for each device on each Verizon path, which nobody publishes.

- **Problem evidence:** (1) FCC 26-19's amendments took effect Oct 15, 2026 (91 FR 62334, Oct 1, 2026: https://www.govinfo.gov/content/pkg/FR-2026-10-01/pdf/2026-20192.pdf). They removed the 63.602 adequate-replacement test, which required interoperability with key applications such as alarms and medical devices. They also removed the old 63.71(a)(6) customer-notice items, including the line-power warning and 'a list of the steps the customer may take to ensure safe use' (founder-verified from eCFR/FR). (2) FCC 26-19 refused an alarm-compatibility condition and only 'encourage[s] carriers to ensure their IP networks are appropriately configured so as to prevent alarm signaling failures'. It quotes AICC: about 5M installed alarm panels still use dialer protocols (DIGEST, migration_incidents and sweep_campus_tech). (3) Verizon's own Business Phone Connect (Inseego BPC100, $129.99) supports only the Contact-ID alarm protocol; other central-station protocols are 'not supported'. Its backup is 3 AA batteries: 23 h standby, 3 h talk (https://www.verizon.com/business/shop/products/devices/connected-devices/inseego-business-phone-connect/, fetched 2026-10-21). (4) Verizon NJ notice 2026-03-A-NJ (Sept 8, 2026) retires copper in 62 NJ wire centers, including Piscataway, 'on or after December 10, 2026' (https://www.verizon.com/about/sites/default/files/verizon-new-jersey-copper-retirement-notice-2026-0908.pdf). DA 26-890 (Aug 26, 2026) covers residential and business legacy TDM voice in 'portions of ... New Jersey' and still cites the Adequate Replacement Test (https://docs.fcc.gov/public/attachments/DA-26-890A1.txt). DA 26-1038 (Sept 25, 2026) pulled that application from auto-grant (https://docs.fcc.gov/public/attachments/DA-26-1038A1.pdf). (5) Local governments are urging residents to object over these devices. Culpeper County, VA guidance (per search snippet; the page returned 403, so the verbatim text is UNVERIFIED) says replacement devices rely on battery backup that 'typically lasts 24 hours or less' and that legacy medical-alert units and alarm systems may not work over wireless units. A Lindenwold, NJ notice raised residents who depend on a landline for medical alert and security systems (snippet; the page now returns 404). (6) Precedent: after Sandy, Verizon told the FCC that Voice Link did not support 'some medical alert devices, and some (but not all) other monitoring systems like alarm systems' (FCC 14-185, DIGEST). ADT spent about $292M on the 3G sunset and still stranded customers who did not respond (ADT FY2022 10-K, https://www.sec.gov/Archives/edgar/data/1703056/000170305623000046/adt-20221231.htm).
- **Verizon tech:** Verizon's own replacement stack is the subject under test: Fios Digital Voice where fiber exists; Business Phone Connect (Inseego BPC100 / ATEL V810V) on Verizon LTE with VoLTE; One Talk; LTE/5G Business and Home Internet. The bench measures what the VoLTE voice path (AMR-WB/EVS codecs, DTMF relay) does to Contact-ID, SIA and 4/2 alarm formats, medical-alert tones, fax and TTY/Baudot. A ThingSpace Connectivity Management heartbeat ($1.50-$6/mo self-serve line, DIGEST verizon_pots_offer) carries the post-cutover green receipt. Student build: a bench with a BPC100 on a Verizon business voice line plus used devices, an open-source alarm-receiver/fax-to-email endpoint as the far end, and a web/phone 'Still Works?' flow that reads the device label (photo plus OCR) and returns a per-path verdict.
- **Verizon money:** Three lines. (1) Wireline voice and business retention at the forced migration, with Fios/FWA/One Talk pull-through; the alternative is losing the customer to cable VoIP. Verizon does not disclose NJ copper customer counts. (2) Cost of services: fewer failed cutovers, repeat dispatches and escalations. Truck rolls cost $150-600 by industry estimate (UNVERIFIED). (3) The copper program's savings clock. About $180M/yr came from 36 COs (Investor Day 2022, https://www.verizon.com/about/sites/default/files/2022-03/Investor-Day-2022-Transcript.pdf). That averages about $5M per CO per year, or roughly $0.4M per CO per month of slippage (derived average; NJ COs will differ). Emptied COs now become remote data-center space that 'sold out capability in 24 hours' (Q2 2026 call, https://www.verizon.com/about/sites/default/files/2026-08/vz_2q2026_transcript_072426rm.pdf). Honest size: tens of millions of dollars a year nationally if it removes a few months of slippage and stranded-device escalations across the roughly 1,566 wire centers noticed in 2026. Every input except the $180M/36 average is an assumption for a Verizon mentor to replace.
- **Dated anchor:** FCC 26-19 copper and discontinuance amendments effective Oct 15, 2026 (91 FR 62334, Oct 1, 2026): 63.602 and the safe-use notice items are gone. Verizon NJ copper notice Sept 8, 2026. DA 26-890 (Aug 26, 2026) and DA 26-1038 (Sept 25, 2026; the NJ legacy-voice application was pulled from auto-grant). DA 26-891 (Aug 26, 2026; business lines, authorized on or after Oct 23, 2026).
- **Primary data:** (1) A compatibility bench run by Rutgers ECE students: about 8 device types (a Contact-ID panel, an SIA panel, a 4/2 dialer, two PERS bases, a fax machine, a TTY, a dial-up card terminal) across 3 Verizon paths (BPC100/VoLTE, One Talk, wired VoIP baseline), 50 trials each, to give success rates per device and path. That costs about $1-2K of used gear plus one line, and the results exist nowhere today. (2) The OPRA/OIT bill: which Rutgers numbers still on Verizon's 732-445/932/235 exchanges carry fax or alarm service (the Rutgers telecom finding that EOHSI publishes a 732-445 fax number) gives a first real customer case. (3) For the Feb 3 poster: structured interviews with small businesses in retiring wire centers (Metuchen Main St, Piscataway Stelton Rd, Dunellen) on what is plugged into their copper and whether they plan to move to cable.
- **Demo:** VIDEO (about 2 min): a neighbor's alarm keypad and medical-alert base sit on the bench beside a Verizon BPC100. 'Still Works?' reads the device labels: 'Alarm (Contact-ID): works on Business Phone Connect. Alarm (SIA): fails, use Fios Digital Voice or a cellular communicator. Medical-alert base: 6 of 50 signals lost on VoLTE, use a cellular PERS.' Cut to after the cutover: one test signal and a green receipt reach the customer's phone. The closing card reads 'Oct 15, 2026: the FCC stopped checking. Verizon can.' POSTER TABLE: the live bench, where judges press the panic button and watch the receiver and the matrix light up green or red.
- **Why unique:** LLM-default teams will not know the six-day-old rule change, Verizon's Contact-ID-only footnote, or the DA 26-1038 pause. The device-by-path success matrix is real lab data that nobody publishes. It runs on Verizon's own replacement products, not a generic app. It is distinct from our champion Last Copper, which covers campus elevator and life-safety lines with nightly in-car audio tests. Still Works? covers residential and small-business customers with a self-serve pre-cutover check plus one post-cutover proof, and it is pitched as a churn and migration-speed tool.
- **Biggest risk:** It sits closest to the champion's territory, so the team would pick one or merge them. Alarm dealers and PERS makers already publish partial compatibility lists. Verizon Legal may not want to 'certify' third-party devices, so it must be framed as tested information, not a warranty. The DA 26-1038 pause may slow the NJ clock. The SMB interviews land after Nov 8. The bench needs a Verizon voice line (a ThingSpace data plan has no voice).

## I17: Spin-Off: the family-plan line becomes a Verizon account, not a T-Mobile line (lens customer_experience, pillar Pillar 3: onboarding friction, churn, Schulman's 'accounts, not lines' and the youth market, self-score 62)

**One-liner:** When a senior leaves a parent's Verizon plan, one tap turns that line into the graduate's own Verizon account. The number, eSIM and loyalty tenure carry over, there is no deposit, and the student discount applies. The price is no worse than a stranger pays to port in, and Verizon's network APIs bind the request to the SIM in the graduate's hand.

- **Problem evidence:** (1) Millennials and Gen Z leave family plans at 19-22 (OXIO via Telecompetitor, 2026-03-03, https://www.telecompetitor.com/most-consumers-consider-changing-mobile-plans-once-per-year-survey/), and 45% of Gen Z want their own plan (OXIO via Eagle-Tribune, 2026-03-02). (2) Verizon's college discount requires the student to be the Account Owner or Account Manager, verified by ID.me, so a student on a parent's account cannot get it (https://www.verizon.com/support/college-students-discount-faqs). (3) Transfer of Service: 'the Account Owner of the receiving account must pass a soft credit check ... A security deposit may be required' (https://www.verizon.com/support/transfer-your-service-faqs). (4) Simplicity (Jun 16, 2026) costs $45/line, with a $30 'initial promotional offer' only for customers who port a number in; existing Verizon customers pay $45 (https://www.verizon.com/about/news/verizon-puts-customers-first; https://clark.com/cell-phones/verizon-one-and-simplicity/; SamMobile 2026-06-17). Our reading: a line moved off a parent's plan keeps a Verizon number, so its holder pays $15/mo more than a stranger porting in from T-Mobile (inference; confirm in the audit). (5) T-Mobile's student plan costs $30 for one line for an 18+ primary account holder (saveonphone, 2026-08-02). (6) Verizon routes graduates to prepaid Visible: $20/mo for the Class of 2026, or a $275 year parents can prepay (2026-05-18, https://www.verizon.com/about/news/visible-graduation-season-offers-class-of-2026). Visible now brands Rutgers football (2026-09-22, https://www.verizon.com/about/news/learfield-visible-college-sports-partnership). Core prepaid churn is 3.59%/mo against 0.92% postpaid (Q2 2026 8-K, https://www.sec.gov/Archives/edgar/data/732712/000073271226000040/a2026q2exhibit99.htm). (7) In the same 8-K, retail postpaid accounts are 34.237M (-1.2% YoY) and ARPA -1.4%. On the Jul 24, 2026 call Schulman said: 'We're getting a lot more one and two-line accounts, a lot more of the youth market' (https://allmind.ai/earnings/event/2929917). (8) In FY2025, customers with tenure under 210 days caused $884M of $1,356M in device write-offs (about 65%) on about 18% of receivables (10-K, https://www.sec.gov/Archives/edgar/data/732712/000073271226000007/vz-20251231.htm). That is why a brand-new young account draws a deposit even when the line itself has six years of history. (9) No public 2025-26 source gives churn at graduation or carrier share among 18-24-year-olds (DIGEST sweep_students_churn).
- **Verizon tech:** My Verizon account roles (Owner/Manager/Member) and the Transfer of Service flow are rebuilt as a one-tap spin-off. eSIM re-provisioning keeps the same device and number; the eSIM-only iPhone Duo reaches stores Oct 23, 2026 (https://www.verizon.com/about/news/es/verizon-ofertas-preventa-iphone-18-pro). CAMARA Number Verification (network-based silent authentication over the SIM's own data session) proves the request comes from that line. CAMARA SIM Swap blocks a spin-off attempted right after a SIM change, which closes an account-takeover path. Both are offered in the US through Aduna, where Verizon is an equity partner (Aduna's 'full US coverage' claim of Feb 2026 is UNVERIFIED). ID.me student verification, already Verizon's, applies the discount at creation. Verizon Loyalty and Verizon Dollars tenure carries over. The demo uses a CAMARA-spec mock if no sandbox key is available.
- **Verizon money:** Moves three 8-K lines: retail postpaid phone churn, retail postpaid account count (each kept graduate is +1 account) and device-receivable credit losses (tenure-aware underwriting instead of a blanket deposit). Schulman's own unit: 'every single basis point is 90,000 net adds', and 25 bps is '$3 billion-plus revenues a year' (Jan 30, 2026, https://www.verizon.com/about/sites/default/files/2026-02/vz_4q25_transcript_013026r.pdf). The pitch line is 'keep 90,000 leaving kids a year and you have bought Schulman one basis point.' Honest size with labeled ASSUMPTIONS: E Verizon family-plan lines leave the nest each year (assume 500K; about 4M Americans per single-year age cohort, Verizon share unknown) and Spin-Off raises the share who stay by 10 points, so about 50K lines x $45 x 12 = about $27M/yr in recurring service revenue, before device and home-internet pull-through. Offsets: $30 price parity for graduates who would have stayed anyway, and the credit risk of new payers. Rutgers alone is tiny; the value is national.
- **Dated anchor:** Simplicity's port-in-only $30 promo, with existing customers at $45 (Jun 16-17, 2026). Schulman on youth and one- and two-line accounts, with accounts -1.2% (Jul 24, 2026). Verizon's college bundle requires home internet on the student's own account (Jul 22, 2026). T-Mobile's $30 student primary-holder plan (Aug 2, 2026). Visible branding at Rutgers football (Sep 22, 2026). The eSIM-only iPhone Duo (Oct 16/23, 2026). Q3 results on Oct 26, 2026 land before the video, so we can quote the newest account numbers.
- **Primary data:** (1) 'Whose line is it?' Class of 2027 census: target n>=600 seniors through RBS and SoE capstone classes and senior class channels, anonymous, IRB-exempt, no CPNI. It asks carrier, whose account the line is on and the student's role, awareness that the discount needs Owner/Manager status, plans after graduation, deposit and credit fears and a price threshold. A choice-based conjoint built by business-school teammates tests keep-number, no-deposit, $30 vs $45, perks and parent-paid first year. (2) Exit-flow stopwatch: 10 teammate-and-parent pairs time each carrier's path to their own account up to the commit screen (Verizon ToS, Visible, T-Mobile student, AT&T), recording steps, documents, credit checks, deposit quotes and any store visit required. (3) A price-parity audit of public pages. Together these give the first campus measurement of the graduation churn moment; carriers publish no churn by age.
- **Demo:** VIDEO: in the Busch dining hall a senior taps 'Make it mine' in a mock My Verizon. Her parent's phone gets a push and approves. On screen, Number Verification returns 'this SIM' and SIM Swap returns 'no recent change'. The account is created with no deposit, the $10 student discount and 6 years of Loyalty tenure carried. Then the census chart: 'X% of Rutgers seniors on a Verizon family plan expect to leave it within a year; Y% of them plan to leave Verizon.' The last card is the basis-point math. POSTER TABLE: two phones (graduate and parent) running the live flow against the API mock, the stopwatch comparison across carriers and the conjoint results.
- **Why unique:** It is built on a policy collision documented on Verizon's own pages: an owner-only student discount, a Transfer of Service credit check and deposit, a port-in-only $30 price, and graduates routed to prepaid Visible, which now advertises at Rutgers. It speaks Schulman's 'accounts, not lines' language with his own basis-point unit. The census is data no carrier publishes. It differs from the avoid-list First Number and Day-Zero Line (international arrivals) and Number for Life (summer number keeping). It is about the domestic family-plan exit.
- **Biggest risk:** Verizon may deliberately send graduates to Visible for a lower cost to serve, and will resist $30 parity cannibalizing $45. Youth carrier ideas are the most crowded space for LLM-assisted teams. Content scores modestly because most of the change is BSS policy, with network APIs as the security layer. New young payers really are riskier (the 210-day write-off figure cuts both ways). The census response rate is uncertain.

## I18: Fix First: send the cheapest fix that works, not a $1,000 phone, and prove it from the network (lens customer_experience, pillar Pillar 3: churn and care friction, with the Pillar 2 cost of retention, self-score 60)

**One-liner:** When a customer threatens to leave over bad signal at home or in a dorm, the save desk reads Verizon's own network data and sends the cheapest fix that will actually work: a setting, Wi-Fi Calling, a $249.99 Network Extender, Verizon One home internet, a new phone only when the old one lacks the band, or a network ticket. Seven days later it confirms from network counters that the problem is gone.

- **Problem evidence:** (1) Schulman on the Q1 2026 call (Apr 27, 2026): 'we just spent like $1,000 and did not solve the customer's issues. If we had listened and sent a femtocell to be installed at the house, we could have done that at one-third the cost', and 'The era of just the free handset, that's gone' (https://www.verizon.com/about/sites/default/files/2026-06/vz_1q26_transcript_042726rm.pdf). (2) Q2 2026 (Jul 24): promotional cost of retention -17% YoY, upgrade volumes down nearly 27%, and 'We are separating phone subsidies from our Wireless pricing' (https://www.verizon.com/about/sites/default/files/2026-08/vz_2q2026_transcript_072426rm.pdf). Verizon is retiring the handset save and needs a replacement. (3) Equipment sold at about -$3.5B in FY2025: $25.47B wireless equipment revenue against $28.98B cost (https://www.sec.gov/Archives/edgar/data/732712/000073271226000003/a2025q4exhibit99.htm). (4) Verizon's LTE Network Extender is a one-time $249.99 with no monthly fee (https://www.verizon.com/products/verizon-lte-network-extender/; price from a retailer listing, verify). (5) Bundled mobile plus home customers churn 40% less (Jan 30, 2026, https://www.verizon.com/about/sites/default/files/2026-02/vz_4q25_transcript_013026r.pdf), and Verizon One sells mobile plus home for $70 (Jun 16, 2026, https://www.verizon.com/about/news/verizon-puts-customers-first). (6) CTO, Jun 24, 2026: Verizon already uses 'aggregated, anonymized floor-by-floor indoor performance signals' (https://www.verizon.com/about/news/verizon-architecting-network-autonomy). The data exists. That it does not drive the save decision is our inference. (7) Verizon partner MarketSpark documents 'full bars' sites that still failed on poor RSRQ, so bars are not a diagnosis (https://www.marketspark.com/understanding-cellular-signal-quality/).
- **Verizon tech:** Inputs: the customer's serving-cell RSRP/RSRQ/SINR distributions, RRC and VoLTE drop counters, and device capability from the IMEI/TAC (does the phone support C-band n77, 5G SA, VoWiFi?). Each is read inside Verizon with the customer's consent during the care contact; students never see CPNI. Fix catalog: Wi-Fi Calling entitlement, LTE Network Extender (femtocell over the customer's broadband), 5G Home/LTE Home and Verizon One, a refurbished prior-year phone only when the band is the bottleneck, or a RAN ticket. Proof of fix comes from the Level-4 closed-loop automation the CTO describes, rerunning the same counters at day 7. Student build: an Android logger (CellSignalStrengthLte/Nr RSRQ and SINR, service state, Wi-Fi Calling state), a cost-ordered decision engine, and a mocked network-side KPI API.
- **Verizon money:** Promotional cost of retention and cost of wireless equipment ($28.98B in FY2025), plus postpaid churn. Per misrouted save, a $249.99 extender instead of a $1,000 phone saves about $750 (Schulman's own one-third), and a $0 settings fix saves about $1,000. Formula: savings = coverage-driven save offers per year x share that went to handsets but were fixable for less x $750-1,000, plus churn avoided when the fix actually works, plus Verizon One attach (40% lower churn). Verizon does not disclose save-desk volumes (UNVERIFIED). Illustration only: 100K misrouted handset saves a year x $750 = $75M/yr.
- **Dated anchor:** Q2 2026 call (Jul 24, 2026): CoR -17%, upgrades -27%, phone subsidies separated from pricing. Verizon One at $70 (Jun 16, 2026). The CTO on floor-by-floor indoor signals and Claude-powered network agents (Jun 24, 2026). The founding anecdote is from Apr 27, 2026, just before the window.
- **Primary data:** 'Signal Clinic': three weeks of consented volunteers (target n=150, all carriers) who say 'my phone is bad in my room', in Busch, Livingston, College Ave and Cook dorms and off-campus New Brunswick apartments. For each complaint the team records phone model and supported bands, carrier, Wi-Fi Calling state, and RSRP/RSRQ/SINR at the spot (Android logger; iPhone field-test mode). It then applies fixes in cost order (settings and Wi-Fi Calling, then a loaner Network Extender for Verizon users, then nothing) and re-measures after 7 days alongside a short diary. The output is the first public, labeled 'complaint, root cause, cheapest fix that worked' dataset, for example the share of complaints fixed for $0. One extender ($249.99) and volunteers' time are the main costs.
- **Demo:** VIDEO: a student in a Livingston basement room has 1 bar and tells Verizon chat she is leaving. The Fix First panel shows the diagnosis (network side simulated): RSRQ collapses at night, the phone lacks n77, Wi-Fi Calling is off. Recommendation: Wi-Fi Calling now plus an extender, $250, not a $1,000 phone. Day 7: dropped calls go from 6 to 0 (real clinic data). Last card: Schulman's quote and the $750-per-case math. POSTER TABLE: the live logger on a judge's Android phone, the decision tree, and the clinic chart 'X% of dorm complaints fixed for $0'.
- **Why unique:** It operationalizes the CEO's own anecdote and the 2026 retention-cost cut, and gives Verizon's network data a new job: deciding retention spend. Crowd teams build coverage maps and twins, not the save decision. A labeled fix-outcome dataset does not exist publicly. It differs from our explored Glass Ceiling and Dead Glass, which measure building attenuation for network planning. The product here is the care decision plus proof of fix, measured in dollars per save.
- **Biggest risk:** Verizon's Gemini-powered care (which handles 'the majority' of inbound consumer contacts per Pure AI, 2026-08-24) may already script extenders and Wi-Fi Calling, so judges may say 'we do this'. Per-subscriber KPIs are Verizon-internal, so the network side is simulated. Volumes are undisclosed, so the value rests on assumptions. Judges may see it as close to indoor-coverage ideas. Extenders need home broadband, and dorm networks (RUWireless Secure off Jan 5, 2027) may block them.

## I19: Day 30: every new line passes a network check before the return window closes (lens customer_experience, pillar Pillar 3: onboarding friction and early-life churn, self-score 56)

**One-liner:** In a new line's first 30 days, Verizon's network checks the things that silently break after a carrier switch or a new eSIM phone: VoLTE and Wi-Fi Calling registration, a 911 address on file for Wi-Fi Calling, RCS group texts, MMS, visual voicemail, port completion and short-code 2FA delivery. It fixes what it can over the air and tells the customer before they return the phone, call care or walk into a franchise store.

- **Problem evidence:** (1) Schulman (Jan 30, 2026): churn cause #2 is 'friction in the process, whether it's onboarding, the billing, when they call our customer service' (https://www.verizon.com/about/sites/default/files/2026-02/vz_4q25_transcript_013026r.pdf). (2) Verizon is buying switchers: the $30 Simplicity price requires porting a number in (Jun 16-17, 2026; https://clark.com/cell-phones/verizon-one-and-simplicity/). (3) Early life is the fragile period: customers with tenure under 210 days caused about 65% of FY2025 device write-offs ($884M of $1,356M; 10-K, https://www.sec.gov/Archives/edgar/data/732712/000073271226000007/vz-20251231.htm). (4) Devices can be returned within 30 days with a $50 restocking fee, and purchases at non-Verizon stores follow 'that location's own return/exchange policy' (https://www.verizon.com/support/return-policy). Another 274 company stores went to franchisees on Aug 16, 2026 (https://www.lightreading.com/operations/verizon-to-unload-274-stores-lay-off-500-corporate-employees). (5) eSIM-only phones are arriving: the iPhone Duo reached stores Oct 23, 2026 (https://www.verizon.com/about/news/es/verizon-ofertas-preventa-iphone-18-pro). OXIO: 69% of Gen Z are more likely to switch with instant eSIM activation (2026-03-03). (6) The CFO puts 'reducing complexity and call volumes' at the core of the $5B program (Apr 27, 2026). Gemini already handles 'the majority' of inbound consumer calls and chats, so each avoided contact still counts (https://pureai.com/blogs/the-pure-ai-blog/2026/08/verizon-expands-google-cloud-partnership.aspx, 2026-08-24). (7) UNVERIFIED: how often these functions break after a switch. No public data exists; the clinic measures it.
- **Verizon tech:** IMS registration state (VoLTE, VoWiFi, VoNR) from the IMS core. The Wi-Fi Calling entitlement server and whether an E911 address is present (yes/no only). RCS provisioning on Verizon's RCS backend, the MMSC, and visual-voicemail provisioning. eSIM SM-DP+ and ODSA entitlement for transfers. LNP port-completion state. Short-code SMS delivery. Fixes are pushed through device configuration or entitlement refresh and a plain-language RCS/SMS message. Student build: an on-device 3-minute checklist app, a test harness (a test RCS/MMS group, a short-code 2FA sender, a voicemail deposit, Wi-Fi Calling with cellular disabled) and a mocked network-side checker that shows what Verizon would see.
- **Verizon money:** Early-life churn and returns (wasted cost of acquisition when a switcher leaves in month 1), first-month care contacts (SG&A call-center cost) and restocking-fee disputes. Formula: value = new lines per quarter x share with a silent breakage x P(churn or return | breakage) x (CoA + early-life CLV), plus contacts avoided x cost per contact. Only the formula is solid: Verizon does not disclose new-line early-churn rates or cost per contact (UNVERIFIED). In Schulman's unit, every 90,000 kept lines a year is one basis point.
- **Dated anchor:** Simplicity's port-in-only $30 promo (Jun 16, 2026). 274 stores franchised (Aug 16, 2026). Gemini handling most care contacts (Aug 24, 2026). The eSIM-only iPhone Duo (preorders Oct 16, in stores Oct 23, 2026) drives a fall wave of eSIM transfers.
- **Primary data:** 'Switch Clinic': a two-week table at the Busch and College Ave student centers. Students who changed carrier or phone in 2026 (target n=200) run a 3-minute test with the team: join a test RCS/MMS group, receive a short-code code, leave and retrieve voicemail, place a Wi-Fi Calling call with cellular off in a basement, confirm (yes/no) that a 911 address is on file, and check eSIM and port status. The team records carrier, device, switch channel (corporate store, franchise, online, eSIM), days since the switch, what broke, whether the student noticed and whether they contacted care. The result is the first measured early-life breakage rate by carrier and channel.
- **Demo:** VIDEO: a student ports to Verizon on a new eSIM-only iPhone. The Day 30 console (network side simulated) flags 'Wi-Fi Calling has no 911 address; RCS not provisioned; visual voicemail off', pushes the config and texts 'We fixed 2 things on your new line.' Then the clinic stat: 'X of 200 recent switchers at Rutgers had at least one broken basic function; Y% never noticed.' POSTER TABLE: judges run the 3-minute test on their own phones and get a printed scorecard.
- **Why unique:** It is network-native onboarding QA (IMS, entitlement, RCS, LNP), plumbing that LLM-default teams do not reach for. The evidence comes from a physical clinic that no carrier or analyst has run, it hits the switchers Verizon is paying $30 a line to win, and it fits a dated channel shift (franchise stores, eSIM-only phones).
- **Biggest risk:** Verizon probably already monitors IMS registration and provisioning failures, and the clinic could show breakage is rare, which would kill the premise. Several failures sit with Apple or Google (iMessage, RCS clients), outside Verizon's control. Clinic consent and IRB need handling. The Wi-Fi Calling address check must stay yes/no to stay clear of location data after the $46.9M fine.

## I20: Made Whole: the network credits the lines it failed, before anyone calls (lens customer_experience, pillar Pillar 3: billing and care friction, delivered with Pillar 1 automation, self-score 54)

**One-liner:** After an outage, Verizon computes from its own registration and cell records which lines actually lost service and for how long. It posts a proportional credit automatically and sends an explanation the moment service returns. That replaces January's flat $20 per account, which customers had to claim themselves.

- **Problem evidence:** (1) The Jan 14, 2026 nationwide outage lasted about 10 hours. Verizon offered $20 per account, not per line, so a one-line student and a five-line family got the same. The credit 'does not automatically apply and needs to be manually redeemed' in myVerizon. Some users found no working redeem button, care was later opened as a claim channel, and the credit posts in 1-2 billing cycles (AppleInsider 2026-01-15, https://appleinsider.com/articles/26/01/15/verizon-offers-20-account-credit-following-daylong-cellular-outage; 9to5Google 2026-01-15, https://9to5google.com/2026/01/15/verizon-will-send-account-credits-after-yesterdays-10-hour-outage/; https://tech.yahoo.com/article/claim-verizons-20-credit-wednesdays-171909496.html). (2) NJ A5548, the Automatic Utility and Communications Service Credit Act (introduced Sept 24, 2026; pending), covers wireless carriers. It would require an automatic credit of 1/30 of the monthly bill per 3 hours of complete outage, with penalties of $1,000 per affected customer up to $25,000 per outage (https://pub.njleg.state.nj.us/Bills/2026/A6000/5548_I1.HTM). (3) FCC DA 26-1033 (Sept 25, 2026) reminds providers to notify 911 and 988 facilities within 30 minutes, after public safety complained of vague and inaccurate notices (https://docs.fcc.gov/public/attachments/DA-26-1033A1.pdf). Working out who an outage hit is a live, unsolved problem. (4) Schulman names billing and customer-service-call friction as churn cause #2 (Jan 30, 2026), and the CFO targets call volumes (Apr 27, 2026).
- **Verizon tech:** Per-device registration, attach and paging records from the MME/AMF and IMS give who lost service and when. Cell-site and sector alarms and fault management, plus Fios ONT status for home lines. The billing adjustment API. Verizon's closed-loop autonomy agents, which embed Claude per the CTO on Jun 24, 2026, do the correlation. An RCS/SMS message fires on re-registration. Carriers already estimate affected user-minutes for FCC Part 4 outage reports (47 CFR 4.9; rule text, not re-fetched); Made Whole computes the same quantity per line. Student build: an Android service-state logger, an impact engine running on synthetic core events calibrated by the panel, a credit calculator and a notification mock.
- **Verizon money:** Care contacts after outages (SG&A 'call center and information technology costs'), post-outage churn, and regulatory exposure as automatic-credit bills spread. A5548's penalties are capped at $25,000 per outage, so this is not a compliance windfall. Precision changes who gets paid: a per-line, per-minute credit goes to the people who were actually down, and the net cost against a flat $20 could go either way (UNVERIFIED). Verizon does not disclose cost per contact or outage churn. Honest size: episodic and modest, but visible to Schulman's customer-first agenda.
- **Dated anchor:** NJ A5548 introduced Sept 24, 2026. FCC DA 26-1033 on Sept 25, 2026. Gemini care on Aug 24, 2026. The CTO's autonomy and Claude agents on Jun 24, 2026. The redeem-it-yourself $20 credit dates from Jan 15, 2026, before the window but documented.
- **Primary data:** (1) A three-carrier service-state panel: 80-100 consented student Android phones log in-service, emergency-only or out-of-service state, radio technology, signal and a data-reachability ping every minute for three weeks across the four New Brunswick campuses. It gives the first ground truth of how often and how long students are actually down, by carrier, compared with what a cell-level outage model would assume. (2) A recall survey of Verizon users on campus: did you see, find and redeem the January $20, and did you contact care? (3) If a real outage hits during the window, a minute-by-minute record of exactly who lost service.
- **Demo:** VIDEO: a phone shows SOS at a Livingston bus stop. Three hours later service returns and the first message reads: 'Sorry. Your Verizon service was down for 3 h 12 min. We've credited $6.40. No need to call.' Split screen with January's 'log in, find Take Action, tap Redeem'. The map shows per-line outage minutes from the student panel. POSTER TABLE: the live logger plus a replay of a simulated outage on the impact engine.
- **Why unique:** It ties a pending NJ bill and January's documented redeem friction to a network-native per-line impact calculation. Crowd teams build outage maps and dashboards, not the billing outcome.
- **Biggest risk:** Network judges may read automatic credits as a cost concession. A5548 may die in committee. Outages are rare, so 18 days of data may contain none. It overlaps our explored Network Receipt (per-user proof of failure). Per-line impact needs Verizon-internal core data, so the core is simulated. 'Complete outage' is contested (SOS vs degraded service). Registration data is location-adjacent, so it must stay inside Verizon given the $46.9M fine.

## I21: ECRC Zero: no Rutgers 911 call detours to the national relay center during Verizon's NG911 and PBX-trunk cutovers (lens deadlines, pillar 1 - AI-Driven Automation & Efficiency, self-score 68)

**One-liner:** An AI location steward keeps an enterprise phone system's (MLTS) 911 locations validated and synced into Verizon's NG911 path. Calls from Rutgers' Cisco desk phones, Teams clients and analog emergency or elevator ports then route straight to the right PSAP, not to Intrado's national relay center (ECRC). Calls going to the ECRC is the failure Verizon put on the FCC record on Sept 28, 2026.

- **Problem evidence:** VERIFIED. (1) FCC DA 26-1042 (Sept 28, 2026, https://docs.fcc.gov/public/attachments/DA-26-1042A1.pdf). In Massachusetts First Office Application testing on Jan 12, 2026, Verizon found that 911 calls from TDM MLTS customers using third-party PS/ALI went to Intrado's Emergency Call Relay Center. Verizon 'must send these calls to a national call center for routing rather than to the appropriate public safety answering point (PSAP)', and there the caller must say aloud where they are. The waiver conditions are: within 45 days (about Nov 12, 2026) Verizon must 'individually contact all of its MLTS customers who use PS/ALI services' and obtain their location 'with updates ... on an ongoing basis'; it must report ECRC call counts at 60 days (about Nov 27, 2026) and at 120 days (about Jan 26, 2027). The Bureau puts 'all OSPs nationwide' on notice. Verizon admits some PS/ALI users are unreachable, for example when 'the enterprise uses call forwarding from a security desk to 911', a typical campus setup. Massachusetts called Verizon's notice ineffective. Also quoted: 'No OSP with customers that choose to utilize a PS/ALI service will have the location data necessary to route and deliver 911 calls in a Phase 2 compliant format unless that data is provided by the PS/ALI customers.' (2) DA 26-1043 (Sept 28, 2026, https://docs.fcc.gov/public/attachments/DA-26-1043A1.pdf): 'We encourage TDM-based MLTS users to consider transitioning to IP-based systems.' (3) Verizon's fix today is manual. Verizon 'does not natively offer PS/ALI'. Customers paste their data into an Excel template, email it to psaliupdate@verizon.com, and wait about one business day for validation (https://www.verizon.com/business/support/product-support/products/wireline-911-location-management/). (4) DA 26-891 (WC 26-228, https://docs.fcc.gov/public/attachments/DA-26-891A1.pdf) covers discontinuing PBX Trunk, B1, ISDN BRI and Key System service over copper in NJ and 8 other states, on or after Oct 23, 2026 (grant UNVERIFIED). The same TDM MLTS customers must re-home their trunks, a second point where 911 location can break silently. (5) Rutgers is an MLTS operator in mid-migration. After a Teams Phone migration, the old desk phone 'will only retain the ability to call 911' (https://it.rutgers.edu/voice/microsoft-teams-phone/, created Feb 4, 2026). Rutgers' VoIP numbers 848-445/932 belong to PAETEC ITEL/Windstream (https://www.localcallingguide.com/xmlprefix.php?npa=848&nxx=445). The legacy 732-932 numbers, including RUPD 732-932-7211, sit in blocks homed on Verizon's Piscataway 5ESS (https://www.telcodata.us/view-switch-detail-by-clli?clli=PSWYNJPIDS5); porting status is unknown. (6) Teams dynamic 911 location depends on a LIS map that matches the Wi-Fi access point (BSSID) first, then the switch port, then the switch, then the subnet. The test number 933 'routes to a bot, which then echoes back ... emergency address or location, and whether an actual emergency call would be automatically routed to the PSAP or screened first' (Microsoft Learn, updated Sept 2026, https://learn.microsoft.com/microsoftteams/configure-dynamic-emergency-calling). Rutgers turns off RUWireless Secure on Jan 5, 2027 (https://it.rutgers.edu/2026/09/22/wi-fi-at-rutgers-is-moving-to-eduroam/), which changes the SSIDs and BSSIDs that clients join. Whether Rutgers' LIS keys on those BSSIDs is UNVERIFIED. (7) RUSSECC is Rutgers' own 24/7 PSAP (https://ipo.rutgers.edu/publicsafety/rupd/shared-services-emergency-communications-center). Lumen won NJ's statewide ESInet contract in 2022, covering 248 PSAPs (https://www.telecompetitor.com/lumen-wins-next-gen-911-contract-in-new-jersey/). UNVERIFIED: whether NJ has filed an NG911 Phase 1 request. NJ is not in the DA 26-1042 appendix.
- **Verizon tech:** Verizon Business Wireline 9-1-1 location management, the PS/ALI template intake that feeds Intrado's NG911 solution for Verizon wireline 911 traffic. NG911 Phase 1 SIP delivery and Phase 2 PIDF-LO. Verizon VoIP for Operator Connect, Direct Routing and Teams Phone Mobile (https://www.verizon.com/business/products/voice-collaboration/microsoft-teams/operator-connect/). Verizon SIP trunking as the landing spot for PBX-trunk migrations. What we build is an agent. It ingests MLTS location tables: Cisco Emergency Responder/ELIN exports, Teams LIS CSVs (subnets, BSSIDs, switch ports) and analog-port lists for elevator and emergency phones. It normalizes each record and geocodes it against NJ address data (public NJ address points if available, availability UNVERIFIED; otherwise TIGER address ranges). It predicts each call's route (correct PSAP, wrong PSAP, or ECRC) with a mock ECRF built from public jurisdiction boundaries and RUSSECC's dispatch agreements, and flags records that are not dispatchable (no floor or room). It writes rows in Verizon's PS/ALI template plus Teams LIS entries, then re-checks every night for drift: new APs, moved ports, SSID changes. A mock ESRP receives SIP INVITEs with PIDF-LO and shows where a call lands before and after a fix.
- **Verizon money:** This moves Verizon Business (E&PS and Business Markets) wireline voice retention, and the SG&A and cost-of-services spend on 911 compliance. (1) Compliance. The waiver requires Verizon to contact every PS/ALI customer individually and to report ECRC counts at 60 and 120 days. A self-serve, validated, continuously synced intake cuts the cost per customer and drives the reported ECRC count down. FCC 911 enforcement precedent: Verizon paid $3.4M in 2015 over a multistate 911 outage, under a consent decree that required current PSAP contact data (https://transition.fcc.gov/eb/News_Releases/DOC-332570A1.html), and Verizon Wireless paid $274,000 in 2021 over PSAP notification failures (https://www.tlp.law/2021/12/20/fcc-settles-911-reliability-investigations-with-4-carriers/). (2) Retention and win-back. The same TDM PBX customers face the WC 26-228 PBX-trunk discontinuance and the FCC's push to IP. 'NG911-verified location' is a concrete reason to move them to Verizon VoIP for Operator Connect or SIP rather than to a rival; Rutgers' own trunks sit with Windstream today. Honest size: the number of Verizon PS/ALI customers is not public, so the CFO formula carries labeled assumptions only. Value = N_PSALI x (manual intake and outreach cost avoided + P(retain) x annual voice revenue per customer). A Rutgers-scale Teams win-back is plausible but unproven, and we claim no dollar figure for it.
- **Dated anchor:** FCC DA 26-1042 and DA 26-1043 (both Sept 28, 2026). Hard dates inside the competition: Verizon's 45-day individual-contact deadline (about Nov 12, 2026), its first ECRC-count report (about Nov 27, 2026) and the update (about Jan 26, 2027). Both reports land in PS Docket 21-479 before the Feb 3 poster. Also: the WC 26-228 PBX-trunk discontinuance, authorized on or after Oct 23, 2026; comments on the FCC 26-63 911-framework NOI, due Nov 16, 2026 (https://docs.fcc.gov/public/attachments/FCC-26-63A1.pdf); and Rutgers turning off RUWireless Secure on Jan 5, 2027.
- **Primary data:** (1) A geocode-and-route audit of every Rutgers New Brunswick and Piscataway building address: does it geocode, and which jurisdiction holds it (RUSSECC vs Piscataway vs New Brunswick vs Highland Park)? This is the first measure of how many Rutgers MLTS 911 records would route correctly under GIS-based NG911 routing. (2) A 933 walk test with an OIT or staff partner: Teams Phone clients in about 50 rooms across Busch, Livingston, College Ave and Cook/Douglass. The bot reads back the address and says whether the call would auto-route, so no real 911 call is placed. This works only for Calling Plan, Operator Connect or Teams Phone Mobile; under Direct Routing it needs the ERS provider's test line. We repeat it after Jan 5, 2027 to catch BSSID and SSID drift. (3) Answers from OIT. Is Teams' PSTN connection Operator Connect, Calling Plan, or Direct Routing with ELIN/PS-ALI? Who holds the Cisco ELIN records? How many desk phones are now 911-only? Do elevator and emergency analog ports carry room-level location? (4) LRN lookups on published Rutgers numbers. Telnyx Number Lookup returns lrn, ported_status and ocn (https://developers.telnyx.com/api-reference/number-lookup/lookup-phone-number-data). They show which numbers still ride Verizon TDM switches.
- **Demo:** VIDEO (2 min): Cold open on DA 26-1042's line about calls sent 'to a national call center'. A staffer in a Busch building dials 933 from Teams, and the bot reads back the address it would send; we show the real result, whatever it is. The ECRC Zero map then colors about 300 Rutgers buildings by predicted route: RUSSECC, wrong PSAP, or ECRC. One click writes corrected records in Verizon's PS/ALI template and in Teams LIS format. Re-run, and the map turns green. Close on the Jan 5 Wi-Fi cutover and the agent's nightly drift check. POSTER TABLE: a laptop running a mock NG911 ESRP. Judges pick a building, the agent sends a SIP INVITE with PIDF-LO, and the screen shows where the call lands before and after the fix. Beside it, a chart of Verizon's own 60-day and 120-day ECRC counts from the FCC docket.
- **Why unique:** It starts from a Verizon-specific failure that became public only on Sept 28, 2026, and ties it to three dated Rutgers events: the Teams migration, the PBX-trunk discontinuance and the Jan 5 Wi-Fi cutover. An LLM-default team cannot reach it. It is not a Gen 3 Wi-Fi-Calling or floor idea (RightRoom, Here Not Home, Floor Truth); those concern consumer handsets. This concerns the enterprise phone system's location data, which Verizon now needs for its own NG911 compliance. It hands Verizon a cheaper way to meet a waiver condition the FCC is watching. Rutgers is both an MLTS operator and a PSAP, so one campus can test both ends of the call.
- **Biggest risk:** The gate questions could hollow it out. Rutgers may already use a cloud emergency routing provider with no PS/ALI. NJ has no confirmed Phase 1 request. Rutgers' trunks are with Windstream, not Verizon. If all three hold, Rutgers is a demonstration site, not a Verizon customer at risk. Verizon may also have automated its intake internally since September. The 933 test needs a staff partner and a Teams PSTN model that supports it. The team must never place a real 911 call.

## I22: Copper Out, Compute In: rank Verizon's 1,763 retiring central offices for AI-edge conversion, with Rutgers as the Piscataway anchor (lens deadlines, pillar 2 - Cost & Sustainability Transformation (with new revenue), self-score 60)

**One-liner:** A siting model scores every central office in Verizon and Frontier's 2026 copper-retirement notices on how soon it can become a power-ready AI inference site and who nearby would buy the capacity. The demand side is proven at Rutgers, 2 km from the Piscataway CO in the Dec 10, 2026 wave.

- **Problem evidence:** VERIFIED. (1) Schulman on the Q2 2026 call (Jul 24, 2026): 'we have thousands of central offices, many of which we're taking copper out of, and we are retrofitting them to be remote data centers that are power-ready, permitted, fully redundant infrastructure ... sold out capability in 24 hours' (https://www.verizon.com/about/sites/default/files/2026-08/vz_2q2026_transcript_072426rm.pdf). (2) Light Reading (Jul 24, 2026) reports Verizon is 'retrofitting thousands of central offices where it is decommissioning copper', alongside a dark-fiber deal with Google worth more than $1B (https://www.lightreading.com/finance/verizon-lands-1b-ai-data-center-connectivity-deal-with-google). AI Connect revenue is expected to start layering in during 2027 (https://convergedigest.com/verizon-positions-ai-connect-as-new-growth-engine/). (3) CFO Skiadas (Apr 27, 2026) named decommissioning copper, 'recycling that copper and also monetizing that copper' as a cost proof point (https://www.verizon.com/about/sites/default/files/2026-06/vz_1q26_transcript_042726rm.pdf). (4) The 2026 Verizon and Frontier notices list 1,763 unique wire-center CLLIs (parsed in the repo). Implementation dates are Nov 6, 2026 for 968 Frontier-legacy COs in TX, IN, MN, WI, FL, NC and NE, and Dec 10, 2026 for 696, including 62 in Verizon NJ. Piscataway, 4 Skiles Ave (PSWYNJPI), is one of them (https://www.verizon.com/about/sites/default/files/verizon-new-jersey-copper-retirement-notice-2026-0908.pdf). (5) Space and power are freed when the switch goes, not when the copper goes. The Piscataway 5ESS host PSWYNJPIDS5 serves NXXs in three rate centers plus a remote in New Brunswick (https://localcallingguide.com/lca_prefix.php?switch=PSWYNJPIDS5). The 9-state legacy-voice discontinuance (WC 26-227) was pulled from auto-grant on Sept 25, 2026 (DA 26-1038, https://docs.fcc.gov/public/attachments/DA-26-1038A1.pdf) and had 615 filings by Oct 20. CO conversion timing is therefore partly a regulatory question. UNVERIFIED: any MW or space figure per CO; that Rutgers' 2026 AI buildout (the HELIX AI hub went to AWS, per the digest) leaves Verizon without a role.
- **Verizon tech:** CO real estate and its DC power plant (rectifiers, batteries, generators). Verizon AI Connect dark fiber and wavelength services. Fios and 5G Ultra Wideband access from campus to the CO. The TDM switch decommission sequence (5ESS PSWYNJPIDS5 and its New Brunswick remote NBWKNJNBRS2). What we build is a scoring model over the 1,763 CLLIs and addresses from the notices. Inputs: distance to anchor demand (universities and hospitals from public IPEDS and CMS lists), flood zone (FEMA NFHL), utility hosting-capacity maps where they are public, metro fiber density, and whether the CO's voice discontinuance is paused. Outputs per CO: 'months to first rack' and 'buyers within 5 km'. A latency testbed compares inference served at CO distance with cloud regions, measured over Verizon 5G UW and Fios.
- **Verizon money:** This moves AI Connect revenue, a new line that management expects to start layering in during 2027, and monetizes stranded CO assets as part of the $5B 2026 cost program. Honest size: no per-CO power or revenue figure is public, so the poster shows a labeled formula only. Revenue per CO = sellable kW x $/kW-month x 12 x utilization. An illustrative 250 kW at $150-250 per kW-month gives $0.45-0.75M a year per CO; every input is an assumption for Verizon to replace. The team's contribution is (1) ordering which of the 1,763 COs to retrofit first by demand and readiness rather than by copper date, and (2) bringing demand evidence from an anchor tenant. A second number: stranded value per month while a paused discontinuance keeps a TDM switch powered in an otherwise sellable CO. We credit no copper savings to ourselves.
- **Dated anchor:** Schulman's 'sold out capability in 24 hours' (Jul 24, 2026) and the AI Connect launch. Dec 10, 2026 implementation for Piscataway and 61 other NJ wire centers; Nov 6, 2026 for 968 Frontier COs. FCC 26-19 rules effective Oct 15, 2026, which speed copper exits. DA 26-1038 (Sept 25, 2026), which paused WC 26-227. Verizon's Q3 results (Oct 26, 2026) and Q4 results (late Jan 2027) give live AI Connect updates for the poster.
- **Primary data:** (1) A Rutgers AI demand census. Short interviews or surveys of 15-25 labs and centers (WINLAB, robotics, RBHS imaging, OARC users) on GPU-hours, latency budget and data-residency needs (PHI, export control). Add OARC queue-wait statistics if OARC will share them. (2) Measured RTT and jitter from Busch, Livingston, College Ave and Cook to the nearest cloud regions and NYC-metro colocation sites, over Verizon 5G UW and the Rutgers network, compared with the 2 km path to 4 Skiles Ave. (3) A site sheet for 4 Skiles Ave built from the public right-of-way only (generator, fiber entrances, lot, setbacks), plus the utility hosting-capacity reading for its feeder. (4) If we can get it, a non-binding letter of interest from one Rutgers unit.
- **Demo:** VIDEO: A street shot of 4 Skiles Ave, then the Busch campus 2 km away. The Schulman quote on screen. A national map of the 1,763 retiring COs lights up by score; zoom to Piscataway, where the Rutgers demand census becomes a demand curve. Live latency beat: a vision model steering a robot arm (or real-time captions) served from 'CO distance' versus the cloud, with the frame-time gap on screen. POSTER TABLE: an interactive map. Judges move sliders for power, $/kW and switch-retirement date and watch the CO ranking and payback update.
- **Why unique:** No other team will join Verizon's 2026 copper notices to its 2026 plan to turn COs into data centers, and the 1,763-CLLI dataset comes from parsing Verizon's own notices. It turns copper retirement from a cost story into a revenue story owned by GN&T Transformation. Rutgers is a credible anchor: a research university 2 km from a retiring CO in the Dec 10 wave.
- **Biggest risk:** Verizon's real-estate and AI Connect teams already hold the power, space and fiber data, so the model may look naive. Our value then rests on demand evidence, which may show that Rutgers' compute buyers want hyperscale GPU capacity (AWS/HELIX), not edge inference. A CO is not empty until its switch retires, and that discontinuance is paused. NJ data-center politics are hostile. No MW figures are public, so the money is assumption-driven.

## I23: Switchover Concierge: device-aware, no-truck-roll migration for the business lines Verizon is discontinuing (lens deadlines, pillar 1 - AI-Driven Automation & Efficiency (Pillar 3 secondary), self-score 57)

**One-liner:** Verizon has applied to discontinue B1, key-system and PBX-trunk service over copper on or after Oct 23, 2026 (WC 26-228). Before those lines go, a phone-camera AI identifies what is really plugged into each business line: fax, alarm dialer, card terminal, modem, or a door or gate phone. It matches each device to a lab-tested Verizon replacement, ships it, and proves it works remotely. The FCC stopped requiring that proof on Oct 15.

- **Problem evidence:** VERIFIED. (1) DA 26-891 (Aug 26, 2026, WC 26-228) covers 'Business Dial Tone Line (also called B1 (Business One-Line)); ISDN BRI Service; PBX Trunk Service; Foreign Exchange Service; Payphone Service ... and Key System Service' in AZ, DE, NJ, NM, OH, RI, SC, UT and VA, 'on or after October 23, 2026' (https://docs.fcc.gov/public/attachments/DA-26-891A1.pdf). Whether it was granted is UNVERIFIED; we found no notice removing it. (2) The FCC 26-19 amendments took effect Oct 15, 2026 (91 FR 62334, https://www.govinfo.gov/content/pkg/FR-2026-10-01/pdf/2026-20192.pdf). They removed 47 CFR 63.602, including '(3) Offers interoperability with key applications and functionalities', and the customer notices that the replacement 'may not provide line power' and that list 'steps the customer may take to ensure safe use'. In FCC 26-19 the FCC quoted AICC's count of about 5 million alarm panels still using dialer protocols and refused an alarm-compatibility condition (digest). (3) Verizon's own replacement has limits. The BPC100 supports Contact-ID only ('other central-station protocols are not supported'), runs on 3 AA batteries (23 h standby, 3 h talk) and costs $129.99 (https://www.verizon.com/business/shop/products/devices/connected-devices/inseego-business-phone-connect/). Verizon's POTS-replacement page offers no line-test feature (https://www.verizon.com/business/products/voice-collaboration/plain-old-telephone-service-replacement/). (4) Migration cost: AICC told the FCC that each device swap is a truck roll of up to 3 hours and that about 25% of appointments get no access (https://tma.us/wp-content/uploads/2021/05/AICC-Petition-for-Emergency-Relief-5-10-21-FINAL.pdf). Verizon said its copper savings are 'in part driven by a reduction in technician dispatches' (Malady, 2022, https://www.verizon.com/about/sites/default/files/2022-03/Investor-Day-2022-Transcript.pdf). (5) Owners cannot tell whether they are affected. Notices name wire centers, not addresses, and a number's exchange does not show where it is served. Rutgers Health's 732-235 numbers, for example, are homed on the Piscataway 5ESS (https://www.telcodata.us/view-switch-detail-by-clli?clli=PSWYNJPIDS5).
- **Verizon tech:** Replacement services: Verizon Business Phone Connect (Inseego BPC100, ATEL V810V), One Talk, LTE/5G Business Internet and Fios Digital Voice. Life-safety partner paths (CSG POTSolve, MarketSpark) for any device the matrix flags. Verizon MDM and ThingSpace Device Diagnostics for remote verification. What we build has three parts. (1) An 'Am I affected?' resolver: phone number, then LRN (Telnyx lookup), then serving switch CLLI (LERG via localcallingguide), then whether that switch appears in a 2026 retirement or discontinuance filing. (2) A vision-plus-LLM agent that reads device labels from photos and returns each device's protocol and compatibility. (3) A remote acceptance test after install: inbound and outbound call, a fax page to a test receiver, a Contact-ID test event to a software receiver, battery state through MDM, and a signed migration record.
- **Verizon money:** This moves Business Markets and E&PS wireline voice retention, plus cost of services (dispatch opex). A forced migration is when a business is most likely to leave for cable VoIP. A guided, verified Verizon path keeps the line: One Talk 'starts at $20 per line', or a BPC100 or Fios voice. A remote acceptance test replaces the verification truck roll. Labeled formula: value per wire center = lines at risk x change in retention x 12 x ARPU + dispatches avoided x cost per dispatch. Verizon does not disclose B1 counts, ARPU or dispatch cost, so the poster shows assumption ranges, alongside the lab-measured failure rates that drive the change in retention. Honest scale: small per line, but WC 26-228 spans nine states.
- **Dated anchor:** FCC 26-19 rules effective Oct 15, 2026, which removed the interoperability test and the safety notices. The WC 26-228 business-line discontinuance, authorized on or after Oct 23, 2026. NJ copper retirement on or after Dec 10, 2026. 1,763 wire centers in the 2026 notices (Nov 6 and Dec 10 waves).
- **Primary data:** (1) An LRN atlas of every published Rutgers and Rutgers Health phone and fax number, crawled from rutgers.edu pages at about $0.005 per lookup. It shows which numbers still sit on Verizon's Piscataway 5ESS and which have been ported to a CLEC, which also answers the champion's Gate A.
