(2) A consented device census of 40-60 small businesses on Easton Ave, George St and Stelton Rd, run by Rutgers Business School students: what is on each line, and whether the owner knows about the discontinuance. (3) A lab compatibility matrix on a $129.99 BPC100 and a One Talk line: G3 fax at several speeds with ECM on and off, Contact-ID versus SIA alarm formats into a software receiver, a V.22 modem, and a card-terminal dial-out. Success rates over 100 trials each.
- **Demo:** VIDEO: An Easton Ave shop owner photographs a fax machine and an alarm panel. The agent reads the labels and answers, for example, 'Your fax works at 9,600 bps with ECM off. Your panel's SIA format is not supported on BPC100, so that line goes to a certified partner path.' Ship, plug in, and the remote acceptance test runs on screen and issues a green certificate, with no truck. Then the LRN atlas: 'X of Rutgers' published numbers still ride the Piscataway switch.' POSTER TABLE: a live bench (BPC100, fax, alarm panel, receiver). Judges send a test fax and an alarm event and see pass or fail.
- **Why unique:** Three pieces of primary data nobody else will have: a lab-measured compatibility matrix for Verizon's own replacement devices, an LRN resolver that shows which numbers really ride a retiring switch, and a street-level device census. It targets the business-line classes in WC 26-228, not the campus life-safety lines Last Copper covers. The value is retention and dispatch cost, not nightly proof.
- **Biggest risk:** A judge may see it as Last Copper's MOVE step applied to small businesses, or as Verizon's existing sales and install process with an AI front end. If the BPC100 passes every test, the failure story weakens, and the product becomes a reassurance and acceptance-test tool. The 26-228 grant status and its NJ footprint are unverified. The census needs consent. LRN lookups cover published numbers, not unlisted alarm lines.

## I24: Ninety Days Out: the 911 coordination dossier for every copper exit, tested on the Rutgers 911 center that dispatches Metuchen (lens deadlines, pillar 1 - AI-Driven Automation & Efficiency, self-score 55)

**One-liner:** Since Oct 15, 2026, copper-retirement notices must be served on 911 service providers. Discontinuances of services that support 911 interconnection must show a 90-day point of contact and a dated list of the 911 bodies coordinated with, and CLECs exiting because of a copper retirement must certify they coordinated with every 911 Authority. The PSAP that answers the calls is not a named recipient. Our agent builds the dossier for each wire center: which PSAPs dispatch there, which of their 10-digit emergency and transfer lines ride the retiring switch (found by LRN), and who must be told. We test it on RUSSECC, which dispatches for Metuchen, a Dec 10 wire center.

- **Problem evidence:** VERIFIED from eCFR text effective Oct 15, 2026 (FCC 26-19, 91 FR 62334, https://www.govinfo.gov/content/pkg/FR-2026-10-01/pdf/2026-20192.pdf). (1) New 63.71(c)(7)(ii)-(iii), for applications to discontinue a service supporting interconnection trunks or traffic exchange: a 'Statement that at least 90 days prior to the planned discontinuance, the carrier provided a designated point of contact ... to the 911 Authorities ..., 911 service providers, and local exchange service providers that support essential functions within 911 networks', plus a 'List of the 911 Authorities ... with which the carrier has coordinated and the date(s)'. (2) New 63.71(h)(2): a CLEC exiting because of a copper retirement must certify it 'has notified and coordinated with all 911 Authorities ... with jurisdiction within the affected service area'. (3) New 51.333(a): copper-retirement notice goes to interconnecting carriers and '911 service providers'. The FCC no longer issues public notices or runs an objection process. (4) RUSSECC handles 911 and dispatch for New Brunswick, Metuchen and Middlesex College, and Text-to-911 for all of Middlesex County (https://middlesexcollege.edu/campus-safety/). Metuchen (MTCHNJMT) and Piscataway (PSWYNJPI) are in the Dec 10 NJ notice (https://www.verizon.com/about/sites/default/files/verizon-new-jersey-copper-retirement-notice-2026-0908.pdf). (5) Rutgers' published emergency numbers sit in NXX blocks homed on the Piscataway 5ESS (https://www.localcallingguide.com/lca_prefix.php?npa=732&nxx=932): the campus emergency line 732-932-7111 in the Emergency Action Plan (https://ipo.rutgers.edu/publicsafety/eap/reporting-emergency) and RUPD's 732-932-7211. Porting status is UNVERIFIED. (6) NJ's 911 office warns that some PSAPs 'may be forced to transition the transfer of their 9-1-1 calls ... over a 10-digit line during cutover' (https://www.nj.gov/911/interop/overview, undated). (7) Transition failures are real and silent: DA 26-1042 (ECRC misroutes, Sept 28, 2026). On Sept 25, 2026 the FCC reminded providers that outage notices to 911 and 988 facilities are due within 30 minutes, after complaints of vague notices (DA 26-1033, https://docs.fcc.gov/public/attachments/DA-26-1033A1.pdf). Verizon's 2015 $3.4M 911 consent decree required it to 'maintain up to date contact information for emergency call centers' (https://transition.fcc.gov/eb/News_Releases/DOC-332570A1.html).
- **Verizon tech:** Verizon's 911 network role in NJ: 911 tandems such as NBWK at 18 Paterson St (per an old NJ 911 rate-center document, https://www.nj.gov/911/documents/clecs/rate_center_updated08-09-04.pdf). TDM switches and LNP/LRN routing. Verizon Wholesale copper-retirement notices to interconnecting CLECs. Intrado NG911 delivery. One Talk, SIP or Fios voice as the landing spot for PSAP administrative and transfer lines. Verizon Frontline for public-safety accounts. What we build: an agent that takes a wire center, finds its PSAPs (FCC PSAP Registry plus municipal dispatch agreements), collects their published 10-digit lines, looks up each LRN and serving switch, and checks the switch against the retiring set. It then drafts the coordination letter, a contact log, and the dated list that 63.71(c)(7)(iii) asks for. For Verizon Wholesale, it produces a ready-made dossier each CLEC can attach to its 63.71(h) certification.
- **Verizon money:** Mostly cost and risk. It cuts regulatory and network-operations labor (SG&A) for a duty that now recurs across the 1,763 wire centers noticed in 2026. It also lowers the chance of a 911 incident that prolongs an FCC pause like WC 26-227's, which delays copper savings (the only public figure is about $180M a year from 36 COs, 2022). Revenue angle: moving PSAP administrative and transfer lines to Verizon IP voice, and goodwill with public-safety agencies, where Verizon Frontline competes with FirstNet. Honest size: direct revenue is small; the case is avoided delay and enforcement risk, shown with labeled assumptions only.
- **Dated anchor:** FCC 26-19 amendments to 51.333 and 63.71, effective Oct 15, 2026 (91 FR 62334, published Oct 1, 2026). Dec 10, 2026 implementation for Metuchen and Piscataway. DA 26-1033 (Sept 25, 2026). DA 26-1042 (Sept 28, 2026). WC 26-228, authorized on or after Oct 23, 2026.
- **Primary data:** (1) An LRN atlas of the published 10-digit emergency, non-emergency and transfer numbers for every PSAP and police or fire agency in the 62 retiring NJ wire centers, taken from municipal and county websites. It shows which still ride retiring TDM switches. (2) A structured interview with RUSSECC: who dials its 10-digit lines (alarm central stations, elevator call centers, other PSAPs, relay services), and was it told about Metuchen's Dec 10 date? (3) The same LRN check for Rutgers' own published emergency numbers. This is public-safety data that only a team with access to its own university's 911 center can get.
- **Demo:** VIDEO: 'On Dec 10, Metuchen's copper retires. The 911 center that dispatches Metuchen is on our campus. Under the new rules, nobody has to tell it.' Type MTCHNJMT. In 20 seconds the agent produces the dossier: PSAPs, their numbers, which ride the retiring switch, NG911 status, contacts, a draft letter, and the dated coordination list the FCC filing requires. POSTER: a table of the 62 NJ wire centers with red, amber or green per PSAP, plus findings from the RUSSECC interview.
- **Why unique:** It rests on rule text that took effect Oct 15, 2026, and on a Rutgers-run PSAP that dispatches for a retiring wire center. The LRN method shows outsiders which emergency numbers depend on a retiring switch without touching CPNI. Last Copper looks at lifeline lines inside buildings. This looks at the 911 centers and the numbers people use to reach them.
- **Biggest risk:** Verizon already knows which circuits PSAPs buy and has a regulatory team, so a judge may call the dossier internal paperwork. The 90-day point-of-contact rule covers only discontinuances of services that support 911 interconnection, so the pitch must not overstate it. RUSSECC may decline an interview. LRN sees only published numbers, not ring-downs or ALI links. Direct revenue is small.

## I25: Kitchen-Table Cutover: Rutgers Cooperative Extension moves accessibility and medical-alert households onto Verizon before the copper goes, and gives Verizon the record (lens deadlines, pillar 3 - Next-Gen Products & Services to Enhance Customer Experience, self-score 50)

**One-liner:** Since Oct 15, 2026, no federal test checks that a copper replacement works with TTYs, captioned phones, medical-alert bases or home alarms. NJ's land-grant extension service and student volunteers would record what is plugged into each vulnerable household's landline and match it to a Verizon replacement we have lab-tested. Each household gets a consented transition record. That record is the evidence the FCC wanted when it paused Verizon's 9-state voice exit.

- **Problem evidence:** VERIFIED. (1) 47 CFR 63.602 was removed effective Oct 15, 2026 (eCFR history; repo copy). It held (b)(2)(iii), 'Complies with regulations governing accessibility, usability, and compatibility requirements', and (b)(3), 'interoperability with key applications and functionalities'. The old customer notices on line power and safe-use steps are also gone (91 FR 62334, https://www.govinfo.gov/content/pkg/FR-2026-10-01/pdf/2026-20192.pdf). (2) FCC 26-56, effective Oct 13, 2026, removes the requirement that TTY-based TRS support ASCII (https://www.federalregister.gov/documents/2026/09/10/2026-18478/telecommunications-relay-service-ascii-format-requirement). (3) DA 26-1038 (Sept 25, 2026) says Verizon's residential and business legacy-voice discontinuance in portions of NJ and 8 other states 'will not be automatically granted' (https://docs.fcc.gov/public/attachments/DA-26-1038A1.pdf). WC 26-227 had 615 filings by Oct 20, including counties and a retirees' association (ECFS count, per digest). (4) Past network sunsets stranded alarm and PERS users, and PERS users are overwhelmingly elderly (AICC 2021, https://tma.us/wp-content/uploads/2021/05/AICC-Petition-for-Emergency-Relief-5-10-21-FINAL.pdf). ADT reports that customers who did not respond suffered 'loss of signal' (https://www.sec.gov/Archives/edgar/data/1703056/000170305622000042/adt-20211231.htm). (5) Verizon NJ retires copper 'at all remaining locations' in 62 wire centers on or after Dec 10, 2026.
- **Verizon tech:** Replacement services: Fios Digital Voice with battery backup, Verizon LTE Home Phone and 5G Home, Phone Connect adapters, RTT on Verizon wireless for former TTY users, Network Extender for weak indoor signal, and Verizon app alerts for battery and service. What we build has three parts. (1) The photo-to-compatibility agent, tuned to home devices: captioned-phone models, TTYs, PERS base stations and alarm panels. (2) A lab matrix of those devices over Fios Digital Voice, BPC100 VoLTE and 5G Home voice. (3) A consented, signed 'safe transition' record for each household that Verizon can summarize in WC 26-227 or WC 26-214.
- **Verizon money:** This moves Consumer conversion from copper voice to bundles, and the timing of copper savings. (1) A guided, verified move puts a voice-only copper household on a Fios or 5G Home bundle. Schulman: bundled customers show 'a 40% reduction in churn versus stand-alone Mobility' (https://www.verizon.com/about/sites/default/files/2026-02/vz_4q25_transcript_013026r.pdf). (2) Documented safe migration of vulnerable users answers the stated concern behind objections in WC 26-227 and could shorten the pause. The only public savings figure is about $180M a year from 36 converted COs (2022). Honest size: revenue per household is small and the effect on approval is speculative. Labeled formula: households x P(convert to bundle) x ARPU uplift + months of pause avoided x monthly copper cost (assumed).
- **Dated anchor:** FCC 26-19 rules effective Oct 15, 2026. FCC 26-56 effective Oct 13, 2026. The DA 26-1038 pause (Sept 25, 2026) and 615 filings by Oct 20, 2026. Copper retirement in 62 NJ wire centers on or after Dec 10, 2026.
- **Primary data:** (1) A lab matrix nobody has published: a captioned phone, a TTY, two common medical-alert base stations and a home alarm, each tested over Fios Digital Voice, BPC100 VoLTE and 5G Home voice, with success rates over 50 trials. (2) A consented inventory of 20-30 households in the retiring NJ wire centers, drawn from Rutgers staff and retiree volunteers and from Rutgers Cooperative Extension contacts if a county office agrees. It records what is plugged into each landline, whether the household knows about the retirement, and the battery-backup status. (3) A map of where RCE has county offices (statewide presence UNVERIFIED in this pass) and which of them fall in the 62 wire centers.
- **Demo:** VIDEO: A kitchen table with a captioned phone and a medical-alert base. The copper notice appears on screen. A student volunteer photographs the devices, and the agent says which Verizon service each one works on, from our lab results. Swap, test call, signed record. POSTER: the lab bench and matrix, plus a map of the 62 wire centers with RCE offices.
- **Why unique:** It targets the exact criteria the FCC deleted on Oct 15: accessibility and compatibility with devices such as medical alerts. It uses a channel only a land-grant university has, and its lab results are primary data. It is residential, so it overlaps neither the crowd's campus apps nor Last Copper's building lifelines.
- **Biggest risk:** It reads as a variant of the champion's reframe ('the FCC paused it; we prove people can still call for help'), applied to homes. It is off-campus and, within 18 days, needs human-subjects consent and an RCE partnership we do not have. Verizon already runs residential migration outreach. The benefit to approval speed is speculative.

## I26: Switchback: Rutgers has two phone carriers, and the TDM exit decides which one it keeps (lens enterprise_growth, pillar Pillar 2: Cost & Sustainability Transformation (a cost-neutral consolidation for Rutgers that also empties a 5ESS host for Verizon), with an AI migration planner (Pillar 1), self-score 68)

**One-liner:** Rutgers' main VoIP numbers ride a competitor (PAETEC/Windstream), while its legacy and Rutgers Health numbers sit on Verizon's Piscataway 5ESS, in the wire center Verizon is retiring. Rutgers is moving to Teams under a review of every vendor contract, and Verizon is exiting PRI, T1, PBX-trunk and B1 services. Switchback maps every Rutgers number and checks every extension's 911 location with Microsoft's 933 readback test. It then moves the campus onto Verizon Operator Connect and Teams Phone Mobile, proving each extension, so Verizon wins the account back instead of joining the 'most customers' Verizon itself says have already left.

- **Problem evidence:** 1) E&PS revenue in 2025 was $13.5B. Wireline fell $532M on 'declines in networking, traditional data and voice communication services', and wireless service fell $193M from 'pressure in Public Sector in part from government efficiency efforts' (Verizon FY2025 10-K, https://www.sec.gov/Archives/edgar/data/732712/000073271226000007/vz-20251231.htm). 2) Verizon's June 11, 2026 section 214 application (MCI/MCImetro) discontinues ISDN PRI, Full T1, Digital PBX Trunks and VFX in 48 states on or after Jul 31, 2026, and admits 'most customers have already left for such alternatives'. The replacements it names are IP Integrated Access, FWA, Webex Calling and One Talk (filed at FL PSC Jun 23, 2026, https://www.psc.state.fl.us/library/filings/2026/03694-2026/03694-2026.pdf). 3) DA 26-891 / WC 26-228 (Aug 26, 2026): Verizon NJ is discontinuing B1 business lines, PBX trunks, key systems, FX and ISDN BRI on or after Oct 23, 2026 (https://docs.fcc.gov/public/attachments/DA-26-891A1.pdf). Whether it was granted is UNVERIFIED. 4) Numbering data (LERG via localcallingguide, updated 2026-10-10): Rutgers' main blocks 848-445 and 848-932 have been held by CLEC PAETEC ITEL (Windstream, now Uniti) since 2010-10-17 (https://www.localcallingguide.com/lca_prefix.php?npa=848&nxx=445). Legacy 732-445 and 732-932 and the Rutgers Health block 732-235 are Verizon NJ numbers on 5ESS host PSWYNJPIDS5 at 4 Skiles Ave, Piscataway (https://localcallingguide.com/xmlprefix.php?npa=732&nxx=235). That is the wire center in Verizon's Sept 8, 2026 copper retirement notice (https://www.verizon.com/about/sites/default/files/verizon-new-jersey-copper-retirement-notice-2026-0908.pdf). 5) Rutgers Teams Phone page (created Feb 2026): 'Your current physical deskphone will be reprogrammed. It will only retain the ability to call 911' (https://it.rutgers.edu/voice/microsoft-teams-phone/). 6) FCC DA 26-1043 (Sept 28, 2026) tells MLTS operators they must supply location for 911 calls and encourages TDM MLTS users to move to IP. In DA 26-1042, Verizon's own NG911 Phase 1 rollout sent TDM PBX 911 calls with missing third-party PS/ALI to Intrado's national relay center instead of the PSAP (https://docs.fcc.gov/public/attachments/DA-26-1042A1.pdf). 7) Rutgers FY27 margin is 0.11% and every vendor agreement is under review (https://finance.rutgers.edu/sites/default/files/2026-07/Budget-%20FY27-%20BOG%2026July09.pdf; https://wrsu.org/?p=8775). 8) UNVERIFIED (search-result summary only): Uniti's 2026 8-K lowered its outlook to a $400-450M net loss.
- **Verizon tech:** Verizon VoIP for Microsoft Teams Operator Connect: ordered from the Teams Admin Center over a peered Azure connection (https://www.verizon.com/business/products/voice-collaboration/microsoft-teams/operator-connect). Microsoft Teams Phone Mobile, where Verizon was the first US carrier and the work number lives on the Verizon SIM (https://www.phonearena.com/news/Verizon-is-the-first-US-carrier-to-launch-Microsoft-Teams-Phone-Mobile_id144855). Webex Calling, One Talk and IP Integrated Access, the PRI replacements Verizon itself names (Rutgers also runs Cisco/Webex). Teams dynamic emergency calling: LIS subnet/BSSID-to-room records become PIDF-LO on the call that Verizon delivers to NJ's 911 network. Microsoft's 933 test service plays back the detected location without reaching a PSAP (https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/microsoftteams/what-are-emergency-locations-addresses-and-call-routing). LNP ports in from PAETEC, and trunks and numbers move off 5ESS PSWYNJPIDS5. We do not claim Verizon NG911: NJ's ESInet is Lumen's.
- **Verizon money:** E&PS advanced-communications and wireline voice (the -$532M line), plus wireless lines (the -$193M public-sector line) through Teams Phone Mobile. Formula with labeled assumptions: revenue = U x P x 12 + M x A x 12, where U = Teams/Webex voice users (OIT to confirm) and P = Operator Connect $/user/month (not public). ASSUMED: U = 10,000 and P = $8 gives about $1.0M/yr. M = 1,000 Teams Phone Mobile lines at A = $40 gives about $0.5M/yr. Rutgers total is about $1.5M/yr, all assumed. Defensive value: the Verizon-held 732 numbers leave if Rutgers consolidates onto Windstream instead. GN&T value: every trunk and number moved off the Piscataway 5ESS brings the CO closer to retirement and reuse. Verizon's 2022 copper-to-fiber program saved about $180M/yr from 36 COs, roughly $5M per CO on average (derived; https://www.verizon.com/about/sites/default/files/2022-03/Investor-Day-2022-Transcript.pdf). Honest view: per-user voice revenue is small and shrinking. The value is controlling the account and pulling through mobile lines, plus a repeatable win-back playbook for every public institution in the 9-state WC 26-228 footprint.
- **Dated anchor:** Jun 11, 2026: Verizon applied to discontinue PRI, T1, digital PBX trunks and VFX nationwide on or after Jul 31, 2026, admitting 'most customers have already left'. Aug 26, 2026: DA 26-891 / WC 26-228 covers NJ B1 lines and PBX trunks, on or after Oct 23, 2026. Sept 28, 2026: DA 26-1042/1043 on MLTS 911 location. Feb 2026: Rutgers Teams Phone. Jul 9, 2026: Rutgers vendor-contract review. Oct 10, 2026: LERG ownership data for Rutgers' blocks.
- **Primary data:** (1) Number census: run every Rutgers department main line published in public directories (no personal numbers) through LERG and a porting/carrier lookup at about $0.01 each. Output: the share still on Verizon NJ TDM, the share ported to PAETEC and the share on others, split by campus and for Rutgers Health. (2) 933 walk audit, with OIT sign-off: dial 933 from Teams, Webex, Cisco desk and analog phones in about 50 rooms across Busch, Livingston, College Ave, Cook/Douglass, HELIX and RBHS. Log the location played back, or whether 933 works on Rutgers' path at all. No PSAP is involved. (3) OIT interview: trunk carrier, contract end date, number of Teams migrations, and how many desk phones are now 911-only. (4) OPRA request for the voice contract and the Verizon and Windstream invoices. (5) The same LERG map for the other seven NJ Supercomputer universities and the 21 county governments, published as the 'NJ public-sector voice map'.
- **Demo:** 2-minute video: open on the LERG map, 'Rutgers has two phone companies' (848 = Windstream; 732-235/445/932 = Verizon's Piscataway 5ESS, the CO in the Sept 8 notice). Cut to Verizon's own filing on screen: 'most customers have already left'. A student dials 933 from a Teams laptop in a lecture hall, and the playback gives the wrong building or says the test is unsupported. The Switchback planner shows each building's location records, the trunk path and a cutover plan. The student redials on a Verizon-routed line (One Talk, or an Operator Connect sandbox if Verizon mentors lend one), and the correct room plays back. Close on a CFO card with labeled assumptions. Poster table: a live 933 readback station, the NJ public-sector voice map, and migration economics against Rutgers' current bill.
- **Why unique:** It rests on live numbering forensics that no LLM default contains (Rutgers' main numbers sit on a competitor CLEC, and its health-sciences numbers sit on the retiring Piscataway switch). It also uses two post-cutoff Verizon 214 filings and Verizon's own DA 26-1042 failure. Enterprise voice is invisible to consumer-app teams. It hands E&PS a named win-back target and GN&T a switch-emptying lever, on one slide.
- **Biggest risk:** Rutgers may already be locked into a multi-year Windstream or Microsoft Calling Plan contract. OIT may refuse 933 testing, or 933 may not work on Direct Routing; that is still a finding but weakens the demo. Voice is commoditized and shrinking, so Verizon judges may rate the money low. Operator Connect pricing is not public, and the WC 26-228 grant status is unverified. The 911-location wedge sits near our earlier explored location ideas (RightRoom, Floor Truth), so it must stay the wedge, not the product.

## I27: SuperLink: Verizon AI Connect as the network for New Jersey's new public supercomputer (lens enterprise_growth, pillar Pillar 3: Next-Gen Products & Services to Enhance Customer Experience, self-score 66)

**One-liner:** On Oct 20, 2026, at HELIX, the Governor announced a $125M+ NJ Supercomputer Initiative. It will put eight research universities and the county colleges on one SHI-hosted machine from 2027, and the announcement names no network. SuperLink measures how much Rutgers data actually has to move and designs the Verizon AI Connect fabric for the corridor: HELIX, RBHS, Busch, the SHI site and the AWS on-ramp. It uses SLA-backed 5G Enhanced Internet slices for instruments and campuses without fiber.

- **Problem evidence:** 1) Rutgers Today, Oct 20, 2026: Gov. Sherrill announced the NJ Supercomputer Initiative at HELIX, backed by more than $125M ($75M Malachowsky gift, $25M SHI International, $25M NJEDA). 'SHI International will host and operate the supercomputer infrastructure in its existing New Jersey facilities.' Partners are Rutgers, Kean, Montclair State, NJIT, Princeton, Rowan, Seton Hall, Stevens and the NJ Council of County Colleges. 'Dozens of projects are being reviewed for potential supercomputer use in 2027'. Each institution plans annual contributions (https://www.rutgers.edu/news/rutgers-partnering-new-jersey-supercomputer-initiative-drive-innovation-solve-shared-statewide). 2) SHI's AI & Cyber Labs are co-located with its Ridge data center in Piscataway (ribbon cutting Apr 8, 2025; https://njbiz.com/on-the-scene-shi-international-unveils-new-ai-cyber-labs/). That the supercomputer sits there is our INFERENCE, UNVERIFIED. 3) HELIX opened July 2026 as RWJMS's new home with labs for 80 research teams, and a HELIX-to-RBHS (Busch) shuttle runs every 35 minutes, so Rutgers' medical research is now split across two towns (https://rwjms.rutgers.edu/about/helix). Rutgers and RWJBarnabas put their AI Innovation Hub at HELIX with AWS (Sep 16, 2026; https://www.dailytargum.com/article/pou9nzfpro16-20260916). 4) JD Power 2026 Business Internet study (Verizon release Aug 26, 2026): 'heightened customer sensitivity to downtime contributed to a 12-point cross-industry drop in large-enterprise performance and reliability metrics' as AI data demands grow, and Verizon ranked #1 for Large Enterprise (https://www.verizon.com/about/news/verizon-business-tops-jd-power-study). 5) Verizon AI Connect: a Google dark-fiber deal 'well in excess of a billion dollars' and COs retrofitted 'to be remote data centers that are power-ready, permitted, fully redundant' (Q2 call, Jul 24, 2026; https://www.verizon.com/about/sites/default/files/2026-08/vz_2q2026_transcript_072426rm.pdf). Also a Corning deal for 80M+ fiber miles, 2027-2032 (https://www.verizon.com/about/news/verizon-corning-broadband-ai-fiber-agreement). 6) New Brunswick's council cancelled a data-center plan after a protest (Feb 19, 2026; https://www.dailytargum.com/article/new-brunswick-city-council-cancels-data-center-plans-in-response-to-protest-20260219). New compute sites face local resistance, which favors connecting existing sites over building new ones.
- **Verizon tech:** Verizon AI Connect: dark fiber and 100G/400G wavelengths in the New Brunswick-Piscataway corridor, linking HELIX, RBHS/Busch, Cook and the SHI site, with an AWS Direct Connect handoff for the HELIX AI hub. 5G Network Slice - Enhanced Internet (FWA, 200/45 Mbps with an SLA, aimed at uplink-heavy AI and computer-vision work, launched Dec 18, 2025; https://www.verizon.com/business/products/networks/5g-network-slice/) for field instruments, NJAES stations and county-college labs without fiber. 5G Ultra Wideband for mobile instruments. Roadmap: the retrofitted Piscataway CO (4 Skiles Ave, in the Sept 8, 2026 copper notice) as a power-ready staging and inference edge.
- **Verizon money:** E&PS AI Connect, fiber and wavelength revenue, either direct or wholesale through NJEdge, the state's research-and-education network. ASSUMED: 10 research-site 100G waves at $6K/mo plus 18 county-college 10G links at $1.5K/mo is about $1.0M/yr. Add slice FWA sites at about $250/mo each. Optional CO-edge colocation of 1 MW at $150/kW-month is about $1.8M/yr. All prices are assumptions, since Verizon publishes none. Honest view: this is small next to Google's $1B. The value is a public-sector AI Connect reference with multi-year, state-backed demand from 2027, and a template for every state that funds a shared AI compute consortium. It also uses Verizon fiber that Corning supply is expanding anyway.
- **Dated anchor:** Oct 20, 2026: the NJ Supercomputer Initiative was announced at HELIX, one day before today. Sep 16, 2026: Rutgers-RWJBarnabas AI Innovation Hub with AWS. Jul 24, 2026: Google AI Connect deal and CO retrofits. Sep 8, 2026: Corning fiber deal. Aug 26, 2026: JD Power downtime finding. July 2026: HELIX opened.
- **Primary data:** (1) Data-gravity census: interview 10-15 Rutgers PIs whose projects are among the 'dozens being reviewed' for 2027, through the Office of Advanced Research Computing. Collect dataset size, daily growth, where the instrument sits (cryo-EM and sequencing at HELIX/RBHS, the coastal digital twin, telescope surveys) and how much latency each workflow tolerates. (2) Measured paths: iperf3/perfSONAR-style throughput, latency and loss tests from Busch, Cook, HELIX and RBHS to public endpoints in the Piscataway/NYC metro and to an AWS region, at peak and off-peak. (3) A time-to-science table, e.g. 'a 40 TB dataset takes N days on today's path and hours on a 100G wave'. (4) 5G UW uplink measurements at 2-3 field sites (an NJAES farm, a coastal station) to test the case for the Enhanced Internet slice.
- **Demo:** 2-minute video: the Oct 20 headline, then the question 'where does the data go?'. A corridor map shows HELIX, RBHS, Busch, the SHI Piscataway site (labeled as inference) and the Verizon CO. A student starts a 100 GB transfer from a Busch lab, and a timer shows hours on today's path. The SuperLink planner overlays the proposed Verizon wave route with latency, capacity and the new transfer time. A 5G slice router on a field instrument uploads imagery. Close on a money card with labeled assumptions. Poster table: the corridor map, the measured transfer table and PI quotes on what they could not run in 2026.
- **Why unique:** Its anchor is one day old, so no LLM default contains it. It ties Verizon's newest growth leg (AI Connect, Q2 2026) to a state-funded public-sector buyer, and it is a fiber and CO planning problem that network-planning judges work on every day. The primary data comes from Rutgers PIs and Rutgers paths, which no other team will collect.
- **Biggest risk:** NJEdge almost certainly already connects these campuses and may simply carry the traffic, leaving Verizon as a wholesale supplier at best. The supercomputer's site is not public. For most projects the WAN may not be the bottleneck, since Rutgers may own fiber across the 2-3 km to Piscataway. PIs may not answer within 18 days. The revenue is small next to hyperscaler AI Connect deals, and judges may read it as a sales plan rather than a campus innovation.

## I28: Measured Primary: let the basement pick the carrier, and make Verizon earn it (lens enterprise_growth, pillar Pillar 1: AI-Driven Automation & Efficiency (automated carrier election and re-election), with Pillar 3, self-score 65)

**One-liner:** Fire communicators, elevator gateways, blue lights and pump-station RTUs choose a carrier once, at power-up, from signal bars on an install afternoon. Verizon's own life-safety partners split those lines across three carriers. Measured Primary runs a 7-day, three-carrier radio trial at every critical endpoint and elects the carrier that actually delivers there. Where Verizon wins, it becomes primary through the new IoT Network Continuity eSIM, with automatic backup. The result becomes a performance spec Rutgers can write into every building and maintenance contract.

- **Problem evidence:** 1) NAPCO's StarLink MAX3 Fire communicator measures each carrier (Verizon, AT&T, T-Mobile) at power-up and locks onto the best one (https://www.napcosecurity.com/media/pdfs/A893-StarLink-MaxFire3.pdf). Rutgers began installing NAPCO StarLink dual-path communicators in 450 buildings in 2020; the carrier is UNVERIFIED (https://www.prnewswire.com/news-releases/napco-security-technologies-announces-project-at-rutgers-university-301088901.html). 2) Verizon's POTS page hands elevator and fire-alarm lines to partners MarketSpark and CSG (https://www.verizon.com/business/products/voice-collaboration/plain-old-telephone-service-replacement/). MarketSpark ships dual SIMs on Verizon, AT&T and T-Mobile, and documents sites that showed 'full bars' yet failed on poor RSRQ (https://www.marketspark.com/understanding-cellular-signal-quality/). 3) Talkaphone's VoLTE blue-light retrofit kit (Feb 2025) bundles a 2-year AT&T SIM, and Verizon is not mentioned (https://www.talkaphone.com/products/blue-light-phones/retrofit-kit/). 4) FCC 26-19, effective Oct 15, 2026, removed the 63.602 adequate-replacement test. Under the new 63.71(f)(2), mobile wireless at 5/1 Mbps is enough for auto-grant, and the coverage maps behind it are outdoor-only under 47 CFR 1.7004(c)(5) (https://www.govinfo.gov/content/pkg/FR-2026-10-01/pdf/2026-20192.pdf). The FCC also quotes AICC that about five million alarm panels still use dialer protocols. Nobody checks the machine room. 5) Verizon's IoT Network Continuity flyer (PDF created Oct 8, 2026) promises eSIM network selection 'primary, backup, public and private' managed in ThingSpace, 'without extra hardware or truck rolls' (https://www.verizon.com/business/resources/flyers/iot-network-continuity.pdf). 6) In the Frontline survey (Oct 13, 2026), over 77% name 'reliability and resilience' as their primary operational requirement (https://www.verizon.com/about/news/verizon-frontline-2026-public-safety-communications-survey).
- **Verizon tech:** Verizon IoT Network Continuity eSIM: primary and backup profile selection through ThingSpace. ThingSpace Connectivity Management API (activate, suspend, change plan) and Device Diagnostics. ThingSpace Intelligence Wireless Network Performance API, with near-real-time network conditions and 30-day device experience history, for monthly re-election (https://thingspace.verizon.com/documentation/apis/intelligence/wireless-network-performance.html). LTE-M and Cat-1, where Verizon promises LTE-M is 'built to last' while T-Mobile is reported to refarm LTE around 2028. RSRP/RSRQ/SINR and attach-success logging. Verizon-certified communicators and gateways from NAPCO, MarketSpark and CSG POTSolve.
- **Verizon money:** E&PS IoT connectivity: Verizon wins the primary slot on critical endpoints that today go to whichever carrier showed the best bars at power-up. Self-serve ThingSpace lines cost $1.50-$60 per month (https://thingspace.verizon.com/start/); partner wholesale rates are unverified. ASSUMED for Rutgers: about 1,500 endpoints (450 fire communicators plus elevators, blue lights, pump RTUs and BAS gateways) at $8/mo is about $144K/yr. ASSUMED nationally: each point of primary share on FCC 26-19's ~5M dialer panels as they move to cellular, at $3/mo wholesale, is about $1.8M/yr. A by-product for GN&T planning is a deep-indoor weak-spot list (machine rooms and basements) that drive tests and outdoor maps never see. Honest view: per-line revenue is small, and Verizon will lose some rooms on the data.
- **Dated anchor:** Oct 8, 2026: Verizon IoT Network Continuity flyer. Oct 15, 2026: FCC 26-19 takes effect, with the adequate-replacement test removed and outdoor maps sufficient. Oct 13, 2026: Frontline survey (77% reliability). Aug 2026: the Rutgers elevator design standard names phone vendors but no carrier or line type.
- **Primary data:** (1) 7-day, three-carrier logs in 20-30 Rutgers critical-equipment rooms (fire-panel rooms, elevator machine rooms, pump stations, refuge areas), with Facilities and fire-marshal access. Every 15 minutes, log RSRP, RSRQ, SINR, attach success and a test-message round trip, then compare across game days, class changes and nights. (2) A census, from panel labels and vendor dashboards, of which carrier each existing NAPCO communicator, Knightscope blue light and elevator gateway uses today. (3) For each room, how far the at-install winner differs from the 7-day winner. Nobody publishes this data, and Verizon RF planners do not have it for basements.
- **Demo:** A $150 logger box (a Pi with three Cat-M1/Cat-1 modules, or three phones) sits in a Busch basement. The dashboard replays the week, showing that the carrier with the best bars at install was not the most reliable at night or on game day (whichever way the data falls; we report it honestly). The AI elects a primary for each room. The ThingSpace API flips a demo device's profile, and when we pull the antenna to simulate an outage, the eSIM falls back. A planner tab lists Verizon's weakest basements. Poster: a per-room scorecard and a draft performance clause for the Rutgers design standards ('7-day measured RSRQ and delivery rate at the panel location').
- **Why unique:** It uses a 13-day-old Verizon product and a 6-day-old FCC rule change. It produces indoor radio data at life-safety endpoints that nobody publishes. It turns Verizon's reliability brand (JD Power #1, Frontline 77%) into a procurement criterion Verizon can win on measured performance, instead of a coin flip at power-up. It is not about copper: it covers endpoints already on cellular.
- **Biggest risk:** Facilities may not grant machine-room access in time. Network Continuity's details (whether backup profiles include rival carriers, and pricing) are unverified. The data may show Verizon losing in many basements, and we must say so. Per-line revenue is small. It overlaps the champion's MOVE step and our earlier indoor-measurement idea (Glass Ceiling), so the pitch must stay on carrier election and procurement.

## I29: Slice the Branch: turn hospital and public-agency branch circuits into 5G slices with an SLA (lens enterprise_growth, pillar Pillar 3: Next-Gen Products & Services, with a Pillar 2 cost case for the customer, self-score 61)

**One-liner:** In 2025 Verizon lost $532M of E&PS wireline revenue as T1, PRI and MPLS branch circuits died. In June 2026 it admitted that 'most customers have already left' for cable, fiber and wireless alternatives. Slice the Branch qualifies every small site of a hospital system or public agency for Verizon's SLA-backed 5G Enhanced Internet slice, using measured signal and a sector-capacity check. Rutgers' sites are the first case: Rutgers Health and UBHC clinics, NJAES research farms and county extension offices. Each branch moves to Verizon wireless instead of to Comcast.

- **Problem evidence:** 1) Verizon FY2025 10-K: E&PS wireline fell $532M on 'declines in networking, traditional data and voice communication services', and public-sector business 'has been and may in the future be negatively affected by a reduction of the federal and state government workforce and other government cost efficiency measures' (https://www.sec.gov/Archives/edgar/data/732712/000073271226000007/vz-20251231.htm). 2) Verizon's Jun 11, 2026 application to discontinue PRI, T1, digital PBX trunks and VFX nationwide says 'most customers have already left for such alternatives', which it lists as cable, fiber, fixed wireless, mobile wireless and satellite. Verizon's own named replacements include FWA and One Talk (https://www.psc.state.fl.us/library/filings/2026/03694-2026/03694-2026.pdf). 3) FCC 26-19, effective Oct 15, 2026, treats facilities-based mobile wireless at 5/1 Mbps as enough for a technology-transition auto-grant (https://www.govinfo.gov/content/pkg/FR-2026-10-01/pdf/2026-20192.pdf). 4) JD Power 2026 (Verizon release Aug 26, 2026): Verizon ranked #1 for Medium Business across all seven factors including cost of service, and large-enterprise reliability metrics fell 12 points industry-wide on downtime sensitivity (https://www.verizon.com/about/news/verizon-business-tops-jd-power-study). 5) Verizon's 5G Network Slice - Enhanced Internet (Dec 18, 2025) is fixed wireless at 200/45 Mbps with an SLA (https://www.verizon.com/business/products/networks/5g-network-slice/). 6) Rutgers' central network went down from 8:09 AM to 2:06 PM on Oct 12, 2026 (https://it.rutgers.edu/alerts/2026/10/12/issues-with-network-and-access-to-it-services/). A Windows update blocked the RBHS networks on Oct 14, 2026 (per the research digest; URL not captured). Rutgers' design standards still put pump-station RTUs on 'telephone line' (https://ipo.rutgers.edu/pdd/consultants-div33-utilities). 7) Verizon's Sept 8, 2026 NJ copper notice covers 62 wire centers, many of them rural (https://www.verizon.com/about/sites/default/files/verizon-new-jersey-copper-retirement-notice-2026-0908.pdf).
- **Verizon tech:** 5G Network Slice - Enhanced Internet (SLA-backed FWA), 5G Business Internet and LTE Business Internet. FWA eligibility made capacity-aware: sell slices only where sector headroom protects mobile users. SD-WAN with cellular as primary or as a diverse path. One Talk for site voice, ThingSpace for site IoT such as pump RTUs and alarm dialers, and the ThingSpace Intelligence network-performance API for per-site SLA evidence.
- **Verizon money:** This replaces the declining E&PS wireline networking revenue with growing FWA and slice revenue at sites Verizon would otherwise lose to cable. Verizon added 193K FWA customers in Q2 2026 (https://www.verizon.com/about/news/verizon-delivers-record-2q26-results); an SLA business slice carries a higher ARPU. ASSUMED for Rutgers: about 100 branch sites at $250/mo (slice FWA plus One Talk) is about $300K/yr. ASSUMED for a hospital system with 100-300 ambulatory sites: $0.3-0.9M/yr. Capacity-aware qualification avoids selling into congested sectors, which protects consumer experience and churn, where Schulman says every basis point equals 90K net adds. Honest view: each site is small, and the value is volume across hospital systems, counties and school districts.
- **Dated anchor:** Jun 11, 2026: Verizon's PRI/T1 exit filing ('most customers have already left'). Aug 26, 2026: JD Power. Oct 15, 2026: FCC 26-19 lets mobile 5/1 Mbps count as a replacement. Oct 12 and Oct 14, 2026: Rutgers and RBHS network outages. Sept 8, 2026: NJ copper notice.
- **Primary data:** (1) A census of Rutgers' off-campus sites from public addresses (Rutgers Health and UBHC clinics, NJAES research farms, county extension offices; the extension footprint is UNVERIFIED until compiled), matched to the 62 wire centers in Verizon's Sept 8 notice. (2) Drive and walk tests at 15-25 sites in 4-5 counties: 5G UW and LTE RSRP/SINR, uplink and downlink, latency and jitter, on weekdays during clinic hours. (3) A one-month retail 5G Business Internet line at one site, carrying clinic-like traffic (EHR sessions, telehealth video) with loss and jitter logs. (4) Rutgers Health IT interview: today's WAN circuit types, contract terms, and what stopped during the Oct 12 and Oct 14 outages.
- **Demo:** 2-minute video: a map of Rutgers' 21-county footprint lights up green where a slice qualifies, amber where only LTE works and red where fiber should stay. In a clinic exam room, a telehealth video keeps running on a 5G slice router while the wired line is unplugged. The planner shows the measured SINR, a sector-headroom check (a proxy, labeled) and per-site economics against today's circuit. Close on Verizon's own words, 'most customers have already left', and the line: 'this is how they stay'. Poster: the county map, the measured site table and a hospital-system TCO.
- **Why unique:** It uses Verizon's own June 2026 admission and a little-known SLA slice product. It frames FWA as an E&PS branch WAN with capacity-aware qualification, which is a network-planning problem, rather than a consumer home-internet pitch. The drive-test data comes from Rutgers' rural sites, which no other team will visit.
- **Biggest risk:** Enhanced Internet slice availability and pricing in NJ are unverified. Sector-capacity data is internal to Verizon, so our qualification is only a proxy. Some Rutgers Health clinics may run on RWJBarnabas networks rather than Rutgers'. Many sites are fine on cheap cable broadband, so the SLA premium has to earn its price. Judges may see it as ordinary FWA selling, so creativity is low, and it sits near the copper-exit family.

## I30: Second Ring: lines an efficiency review would cancel become a reserve that wakes with priority (lens enterprise_growth, pillar Pillar 1: AI-Driven Automation & Efficiency (an agent that manages the line lifecycle), with Pillar 2, self-score 57)

**One-liner:** Verizon lost $193M of public-sector wireless revenue in 2025 to 'government efficiency efforts', and Rutgers is now reviewing every vendor contract. Second Ring turns idle lines into a near-zero-cost eSIM reserve assigned to the non-badge staff who actually run a campus or hospital emergency: facilities, housing, IT and clinicians. When the EOC declares an incident, an agent wakes the right lines in under a minute with Frontline priority or a Business App Management slice, then puts them back to sleep.

- **Problem evidence:** 1) Verizon FY2025 10-K: E&PS wireless service revenue fell $193M, 'primarily driven by pressure in Public Sector in part from government efficiency efforts'. Risk factor: public-sector business 'has been and may in the future be negatively affected by a reduction of the federal and state government workforce and other government cost efficiency measures' (https://www.sec.gov/Archives/edgar/data/732712/000073271226000007/vz-20251231.htm). 2) Rutgers FY27 has a 0.11% margin ($7.06M) and a hiring freeze, and every vendor and service agreement is being reviewed (https://finance.rutgers.edu/sites/default/files/2026-07/Budget-%20FY27-%20BOG%2026July09.pdf; https://wrsu.org/?p=8775). A new Chief Risk Officer started Oct 2026 (https://www.dailytargum.com/article/u-apppoints-former-cornell-compliance-worker-as-chief-risk-officer-20261002). The permanent COO covers public safety and emergency management (https://ipo.rutgers.edu/henry-velez). 3) Frontline survey (Oct 13, 2026): 77% call smartphones the most relied-on technology in a crisis, over 77% put reliability and resilience first, and about 49% say network reliability decides the plan they pick for their personal devices (https://www.verizon.com/about/news/verizon-frontline-2026-public-safety-communications-survey). 4) The Frontline Network Slice was extended to phones nationwide on Apr 23, 2026 (https://www.verizon.com/about/news/verizon-frontline-network-slice-available-nationwide). The Frontline page says priority and preemption are not yet offered on 5G UW (https://www.verizon.com/business/solutions/public-sector/public-safety/). The Business App Management slice is per-user, works through MDM and guarantees bandwidth for chosen apps (https://www.verizon.com/business/products/networks/5g-network-slice/). 5) Rutgers incidents are run by non-badge staff. The Oct 12, 2026 network outage ran 8:09 AM to 2:06 PM (https://it.rutgers.edu/alerts/2026/10/12/issues-with-network-and-access-to-it-services/), an Aug 7, 2026 power outage took down Wi-Fi, wired network and VPN overnight, and the Facilities call center is staffed only 7:00-4:30 (https://ipo.rutgers.edu/university-facilities/operations-services).
- **Verizon tech:** Suspend and restore of business lines through the ThingSpace Connectivity Management API and Verizon business account APIs, on dual-SIM eSIM profiles pushed by MDM. Verizon Frontline (priority and preemption on LTE; Frontline Network Slice on phones) for eligible roles. Business App Management slice for work apps such as work orders, Teams and EHR for everyone else. WPS for NS/EP-eligible roles. An EOC console and agent that maps Emergency Operations Plan roles to lines.
- **Verizon money:** Defensive and surge revenue. ASSUMED: if a review flags 600 of about 3,000 Rutgers lines as idle at $40/mo, cancelling them loses about $288K/yr. A $5/mo reserve keeps about $36K/yr plus the numbers and eSIMs, adds paid activation days and slice/priority add-ons, and keeps a rebid off the table. Converting about 300 BYOD essential staff to business lines with a slice adds about $150K/yr (assumed). Honest view: this is small at one campus and lowers ARPU on the lines it saves. The point is a product that answers the 10-K's named public-sector headwind with a retained low-ARPU line instead of a cancelled one, across every university, hospital and agency now under efficiency review.
- **Dated anchor:** Oct 13, 2026: Frontline survey. Oct 1-2, 2026: Rutgers CRO appointed. Sept 23, 2026: permanent COO. Jul 9, 2026: Rutgers budget, hiring freeze and vendor review. Oct 12 and Aug 7, 2026: Rutgers outages. Apr 23, 2026: Frontline slice on phones (little known).
- **Primary data:** (1) OPRA request for Rutgers' Verizon wireless line inventory: lines by plan and how many show zero or near-zero usage. These are public records with no personal data. (2) An anonymous 'second ring' survey run with OEM and IP&O: which roles worked the Oct 12 and Aug 7 incidents and the Homecoming crowd of 48,650, and how they communicated (device, carrier, personal or Rutgers line). (3) Measured time-to-wake: suspend, restore and first successful call on 5 demo lines through the API, repeated 50 times, reported in seconds.
- **Demo:** 2-minute video: the 10-K sentence on screen ('government efficiency efforts'), then a budget spreadsheet striking idle lines. In the EOC console, an operator types 'Steam break, Busch campus'. Twenty-five reserve eSIMs wake on staff phones in about 40 seconds. Under a simulated crowd, the work-order app holds its bandwidth on the slice while a best-effort phone stalls. The incident closes and the lines sleep. Close on a cost card comparing cancel, keep and reserve. Poster: the OPRA findings, measured wake times and the role map.
- **Why unique:** It is built directly on the public-sector decline named in the 10-K and on Rutgers' July 2026 review. A dormant-line product is something no consumer-app team would design. It applies Frontline and slicing to the non-badge staff who actually restore a campus, with role-based eligibility instead of a police app.
- **Biggest risk:** Verizon may see dormant lines as cannibalization, and business suspension terms limit how long a line can sleep. Frontline eligibility for non-public-safety staff is decided case by case (unverified). OPRA may take longer than 18 days. It overlaps our explored Code Path (Business App Management slice for clinicians) and the crowded campus-safety space. Verizon's Frontline Crisis Response Team already lends devices free in disasters.

## I31: WHO ANSWERS? (Last Copper v3): prove the route and the location, not the wire (lens extend_champion, pillar Pillar 1, AI-Driven Automation & Efficiency: automated nightly proof plus AI fault triage replace the yearly manual press-the-button check. Pillar 3 is secondary (a sellable assurance add-on)., self-score 72)

**One-liner:** When an elevator's emergency line leaves copper, the code-required line check can stay green while HELP reaches a voicemail, the old contractor, or an operator told the car is in Nashville. WHO ANSWERS rides the Verizon life-safety gateway line. With no work inside the car, it proves every night and on every real call the three things the line check never touches: the call gets through in both directions, it reaches the right 24/7 answerer within A17.1's 45 s, and the answerer sees the right building and car. Verizon gets a wire-center view of every lifeline it moved.

- **Problem evidence:** VERIFIED unless marked.

(1) The code checks the wire, not the call. ASME A17.1 2.27.1.1.6(a): line verification 'shall not require activation of the two-way communication link(s)', and a failure may take up to 24 h to annunciate (Inquiries 13-319, 13-343): https://cstools.asme.org/csconnect/FileUpload.cfm?View=yes&ID=61439. A vendor summary dated Jun 29 2026 says the path self-check runs 'without placing a test call': https://dataremote.com/blog/elevator-emergency-phones-after-copper

(2) Phones mostly fail on route and location, not dead wire. Chicago Building Violations open data, Jan 2023 to Oct 20 2026: inspector comments about the emergency phone appear in 1,613 of the 6,725 inspections that drew an elevator maintain/repair violation (24%). By keyword: 181 routing/answering-service, 143 address/location/car-ID, 81 'not working', 86 repeat, 3 dialing the previous maintenance provider. Passing inspections are not in the data, so this is not a per-car failure rate. Citation dated Oct 9 2026: 'FREIGHT STATES 920 S FRANKLIN BUT THEIR PROPMT IS STATING THAT THE CALL IS COMING FROM NASHVILLE, TN.' https://data.cityofchicago.org/resource/22u3-xenr.json

(3) A live line can still fail the rider. In Farmingdale NY (News12; page footer says 2026, exact date UNVERIFIED), a trapped tenant pressed HELP 3-4 times and got a voicemail: 'We're sorry, nobody can help you right now.' https://newyork.news12.com/trapped-between-floors-fairfield-fieldstone-apartments-tenant-says-elevators-emergency-help-button-failed. A17.1 requires a 24 h staffed answering point, no automated answering, and a redirect after 45 s: https://www.ashb.com/wp-content/uploads/2024/08/IS-2024-115.pdf

(4) The migration is when failures surface. Ooma AirDial's director at BOMA 2024 (vendor claim): 'When we replace existing elevator phone lines, we find about 50% of them don't work at the time we go to replace them.' https://buildings.com/industry-news/article/55126180/facing-the-copper-sunset-8-tips-to-choose-a-pots-replacement-boma-2024

(5) The rules just changed. FCC 26-19 rules took effect Oct 15 2026, deleting 63.602 (the adequate-replacement test, including 'interoperability with key applications') and the 63.71(a)(6) safe-use notices: https://www.govinfo.gov/content/pkg/FR-2026-10-01/pdf/2026-20192.pdf. Verizon's B1, PBX-trunk and key-system copper exit (DA 26-891, WC 26-228) covers NJ and 8 other states, on or after Oct 23 2026; whether it was granted is UNVERIFIED. The FCC paused the separate residential/business voice exit (DA 26-1038, Sept 25 2026): https://docs.fcc.gov/public/attachments/DA-26-1038A1.pdf

(6) Verizon's own offer has a gap. The POTS Replacement page names elevator and fire lines, hands life-safety lines to CSG and MarketSpark, and mentions no line test or end-to-end test: https://www.verizon.com/business/products/voice-collaboration/plain-old-telephone-service-replacement/. POTSolve ('exclusive to Verizon') lists 'programmable notifications', not tests: https://d3bql97l1ytoxn.cloudfront.net/app_resources/426631/documentation/1399193_1705470148879_en-US.pdf

(7) Scale. About 900,000 US elevators (Kings III vendor estimate): https://www.kingsiii.com/help-phone-blog/elevator-facts-you-didnt-know-you-needed/. FDNY logged 27,247 occupied-elevator emergencies in 2025, 98.5% with alarm source PHONE: https://data.cityofnewyork.us/resource/8m42-w767.json

(8) Rutgers. The Aug 2026 elevator standard names Avire, Wurtec and Kings III and does not say who answers: https://ipo.rutgers.edu/pdd/appendix_07_elevator_escalator_design_guidelines. The Facilities call center works only 7:00-4:30. On Oct 12 2026 the campus network was down 8:09 AM-2:06 PM with no root cause published: https://it.rutgers.edu/alerts/2026/10/12/issues-with-network-and-access-to-it-services/. Fire communicators (2020) and blue lights (2023) are already off copper, so Rutgers is the pilot, not a countdown.
- **Verizon tech:** Voice path under test: Verizon POTS Replacement, Life & Safety tier (CSG POTSolve, 'exclusive to Verizon', on a Verizon wireless business internet plan). The bench uses Business Phone Connect / Inseego BPC100 ($129.99, Cat-4 HD VoLTE on Verizon's IMS core).

WHO ANSWERS unit: sits at the gateway's analog port in the machine room or telecom closet, so no work inside the car. It has a fail-safe series relay with car off-hook sensing (the car always wins the line), an FXO interface, and a DSP (Goertzel and matched filters). It reports over its own ThingSpace self-serve LTE-M line ($1.50-$6/mo, https://thingspace.verizon.com/start/) and uses the Connectivity Management API (connection history, reachability, callbacks) and Device Diagnostics. ThingSpace plans are data-only, so test calls always ride the gateway's own voice line.

The tests:
- L1, nightly, no human involved: a call over the gateway line to a Verizon-hosted test responder (One Talk number plus Asterisk). It checks two-direction tones, DTMF integrity, VoLTE registration, RSRQ/SINR and battery state.
- L2, passive, on every real call, metadata only: digits dialed are compared with a registry (catches the old contractor and 911 misprogramming), plus seconds to answer and 45 s redirect behavior. An on-device audio classifier detects voicemail/IVR answer from spectral features only; no audio is stored.
- L3, monthly, about 10 seconds of a person's time: a scheduled call to the real answering point announces building and car and asks 'press 1 if your screen shows this'. ASME Inquiry 13-1022 says A17.1 does not bar automated testing through routing systems.
- Optional L4: the founder's two-transducer in-car acoustic test, for owners who want mic and speaker proof (installed by a licensed mechanic).

Cutover Watch: rolls results up per wire center and per migration batch for Network Transformation. Production path: L1/L2 become firmware in Verizon's life-safety gateway, with results in Verizon device management and no added box. The prototype uses an open ATA plus a Raspberry Pi.
- **Verizon money:** Retention at the forced migration. Verizon's own life-safety channel is multi-carrier:
- MarketSpark ships dual SIMs including AT&T and T-Mobile.
- AirDial is certified on all three carriers.
- Talkaphone's VoLTE retrofit kit ships an AT&T SIM only (https://www.talkaphone.com/products/blue-light-phones/retrofit-kit/).
A path that is proven on Verizon is the reason to make Verizon primary.

Honest sizing (assumptions labeled):
- A cellular elevator line at $45-55/mo (vendor claim) brings $540-660/yr.
- Every 10,000 elevator lines held is worth $5.4-6.6M/yr of E&PS revenue.
- An assurance add-on at $5-10 per line per month (price anchors: RATH SmartView Hub $9.99/device/mo; Wurtec Watch under $1/day) adds $0.6-1.2M/yr per 10,000 lines.

Risk avoided: Verizon credits about $180M/yr of copper savings to 36 all-fiber COs, 'in part driven by a reduction in technician dispatches' (2022 Investor Day, https://www.verizon.com/about/sites/default/files/2022-03/Investor-Day-2022-Transcript.pdf). One 'dead elevator phone after Verizon cutover' story, while WC 26-227 is already paused (DA 26-1038), could delay a 9-state exit that is worth far more than this tool costs.

Honest limit: revenue per line falls compared with POTS, so the money is retention and avoided delay, not growth.
- **Dated anchor:** - Oct 15 2026: FCC 26-19 rules take effect, removing the adequate-replacement/interoperability showing and the safe-use notices (91 FR 62334).
- Oct 23 2026: authorized date for Verizon's B1/PBX-trunk/key-system copper exit (DA 26-891, WC 26-228).
- Oct 9 2026: Chicago citation of a phone whose prompt says the call comes from 'NASHVILLE, TN'.
- Oct 12 2026: Rutgers' 6-hour campus network outage. Any elevator phone on OIT's IP voice path would have been exposed; whether any is on it is unverified.
- Aug 2026: Rutgers elevator standard names the phone vendors.
- Sept 25 2026: DA 26-1038 pause.
LLM-default teams will still cite the old adequate-replacement test and a 'Dec 10 copper cut'.
- **Primary data:** (1) Pre-announced HELP audit with Facilities and RUPD/RUSSECC in 30-50 cars across Busch, Livingston, College Ave and Cook/Douglass. For each car we record who answers, seconds to answer, whether the answerer knows the building and car without asking, and 45 s redirect behavior. NJ keeps no entrapment statistic and inspects at most yearly, so this is the first NJ dataset of its kind.

(2) Walk audit of 100+ cars in public buildings, needing no permission: phone vendor, location label, whether the 'Communications Failure' jewel is lit, display or camera present.

(3) Bench 'green light' experiment. A used RATH or Viking elevator phone runs on a real Verizon BPC100 line. We inject antenna loss, mains loss, battery pull, SIM pull and a wrong programmed number, and record whether and when the phone's own line monitor notices, compared with WHO ANSWERS.

(4) Gate A via OIT and OPRA: line type and carrier for each elevator phone, and whether any phone rides the Cisco/IP path that went down on Oct 12.
- **Demo:** Video beat (about 40 s): a student presses HELP on a mock car panel wired to a real elevator phone on a real Verizon gateway. The screen splits. On the left, the phone's line-monitor LED stays green. On the right, WHO ANSWERS reports: 'answered by voicemail X / location announced: Nashville, TN X / 52 s X'. The Oct 9 2026 Chicago citation text appears, then our Rutgers audit number ('N of M cars: answerer knew the car'). Close on the Cutover Watch map: 'wire center PSWYNJPI, batch 7: 3 lifelines failing since cutover'.

Poster table: the real phone, gateway and unit. Visitors flip four fault switches (old contractor, voicemail answerer, wrong label, antenna off). The line monitor stays green while WHO ANSWERS catches each fault in under a minute and routes a ticket to the right owner (Verizon, contractor, answering point or Facilities), with a hash-chained evidence record.
- **Why unique:** VERDICT ON THE CHAMPION: keep the problem, change the hero. The in-car two-transducer sensor should not lead:
- it needs licensed elevator work;
- 2N LiftIP and SafeLine already ship audio self-tests;
- AVIRE SENTINEL already sells managed lift-line migration with automated test calls in the UK;
- MarketSpark already pitches campuses audits plus post-cutover monitoring.

The new facts point elsewhere. Chicago's failure split (route and location comments outnumber 'not working' about 4 to 1), the Nashville and voicemail cases, and A17.1's ban on activating the voice link all say the uncovered gap is who answers and what they see. That can be tested from the line side with no car work, through the migrated carrier path.

What no one we found offers:
- route, location and answer-time proof through the migrated Verizon path;
- a carrier-facing wire-center rollup;
- a measured NJ HELP audit.

Drop the Dec 10 countdown. Make FIND a consent registry (see NOTICE ME). Use SILENT AFTER CUTOVER as the Cutover Watch engine.

Prior art, stated honestly: Viking's daily dial-tone test, RATH 10 min-23 h line checks, Kings III's own dispatch center, and the EN 81-28 3-day test calls.
- **Biggest risk:** - We found no documented US case of an elevator phone dying because of a copper retirement.
- L3 needs the answering center's cooperation.
- The HELP audit needs Facilities and RUPD sign-off within 18 days; the fallback is the walk audit plus the bench.
- A series relay on a life-safety line needs a fail-safe design and AHJ comfort.
- The passive voicemail classifier needs counsel review (features only).
- Rutgers' copper exposure is small, so the pitch must be national-first.
- CSG, MarketSpark or AVIRE could add the same feature; the moat is the carrier-side rollup plus our data.

## I32: SILENT AFTER CUTOVER: Verizon's network notices the lifeline that stopped calling home (lens extend_champion, pillar Pillar 1, AI-Driven Automation & Efficiency: unsupervised periodicity detection plus an AI cause classifier over network records, routed automatically to the migration desk., self-score 70)

**One-liner:** Fire-alarm dialers, burglar panels, medical-alert bases and some monitored elevator phones all place periodic test calls, and Verizon's switch saw that heartbeat for years on copper. SILENT AFTER CUTOVER runs inside Verizon and compares each migrated line's call rhythm before and after its cutover. It flags lines that went quiet, began retry storms or now dial a different number, within one missed period. Nothing is installed and the customer does nothing. It is the check the FCC 'encouraged' on Oct 15, 2026 but never defined, and only a carrier can run it.

- **Problem evidence:** VERIFIED unless marked.

(1) The FCC asked carriers and defined no test. FCC 26-19 declined AICC's G.711/RFC 2833 condition, noted 'five million installed [alarm] panels that use dialer-based protocols', and only 'encourage[s] carriers to ensure their IP networks are appropriately configured so as to prevent alarm signaling failures': https://docs.fcc.gov/public/attachments/FCC-26-19A1.pdf. The rules took effect Oct 15 2026: https://www.govinfo.gov/content/pkg/FR-2026-10-01/pdf/2026-20192.pdf

(2) Verizon's replacement depends on a customer step that fails silently. The Voice Connect/BPC100 guide requires entering '##21*MDN#' alarm-number codes and warns: 'Failure to verify may result in your alarm not working... Verizon is not responsible for unsuccessful alarm responses' (https://www.verizon.com/content/dam/verizon/support/consumer/documents/phone/voice-connect-user-guide-2nd-gen.pdf). The BPC100 supports Contact ID only: https://www.verizon.com/business/shop/products/devices/connected-devices/inseego-business-phone-connect/

(3) Gateway monitoring watches the box, not the device's calls.
- MarketSpark: 'full signal bars' yet failing transmissions, caused by RSRQ (https://www.marketspark.com/understanding-cellular-signal-quality/).
- Talkaphone: DTMF polling 'may not function as intended' over cellular (https://www.talkaphone.com/products/blue-light-phones/retrofit-kit/).

(4) Lifelines have a heartbeat by code. NFPA 72-2013 requires PSTN facilities used by DACTs to be 'exercised at least once every 6 hours' (DMP white paper, https://www.dmp.com/assets/LT-1456.pdf). NFPA 72-2025 TIA 25-1 (effective May 8 2024) keeps scheduled tests for the two-line DACT option at 'alternating 6-hour intervals'.

(5) Sunsets strand devices silently.
- AICC 2021: about 6M 3G alarm radios, about 40% on Verizon (2.6M homes and businesses); truck rolls of up to 3 h; about 25% of appointments without access (https://tma.us/wp-content/uploads/2021/05/AICC-Petition-for-Emergency-Relief-5-10-21-FINAL.pdf).
- ADT admits non-responders suffered 'loss of signal'.
- At UAA, blue lights were intermittently dead for about 3 years and found only by annual walks (https://www.thenorthernlight.org/stories/emergency-telephones-down-across-campus).

(6) Verizon's own record shows testing catches silent transition failures. DA 26-1042 (Sept 28 2026): during the NG911 rollout, MLTS 911 calls went to Intrado's national relay center, and Verizon found this in First Office Application testing (https://docs.fcc.gov/public/attachments/DA-26-1042A1.pdf).

(7) Scale and clock.
- 1,763 unique wire-center CLLIs appear in the 2026 Verizon/Frontier notices.
- The Aug 7 wave (969 wire centers: TX 274, IN 224, MN 163, WI 136, FL 93 and more) is eligible on or after Nov 6 2026 (https://www.verizon.com/about/terms-conditions/network-disclosures).
- The Frontier-area legacy-voice exit (WC 26-170) is on or after Nov 16 2026 (https://docs.fcc.gov/public/attachments/DA-26-763A1.pdf).
- **Verizon tech:** Production, inside Verizon:
- Pre-cutover TDM switch call records for each copper line (time, called number, duration, attempts) are joined through the migration order to the post-cutover records of the replacement line: IMS/VoLTE CDRs for BPC100/Voice Connect, POTSolve and One Talk, plus gateway telemetry from Verizon device management.
- Detector: per-line periodicity (autocorrelation and Lomb-Scargle on call times, plus destination clustering) finds 'heartbeat lines': alarm test timers, PERS check-ins, monitored elevator auto-tests, fax polls and utility RTUs.
- After cutover, an expected-arrival model flags four things: (a) a heartbeat missed by more than 1.5 periods; (b) retry storms, meaning rapid re-dials to the receiver, the network signature of a missing kiss-off (the per-panel retry count is UNVERIFIED and the lab measures it); (c) destination drift, a new or disconnected number; (d) duration drift.
- A naive-Bayes triage labels the likely cause (alarm number not programmed, unsupported format, power or battery, RF, wrong number) and routes it to the migration desk.
- Cutover Watch rolls this up per batch and wire center, e.g. 'batch 7: 1,940 heartbeat lines, 37 silent since cutover'.

Student build:
- A synthetic CDR generator calibrated on our lab traces.
- Lab devices: a real Contact ID/SIA alarm panel, a PERS base and a fax on (a) emulated copper (Asterisk plus an FXS ATA) and (b) a real Verizon BPC100 line.
- Central station: Asterisk AlarmReceiver on a real number.
- A ThingSpace LTE-M probe ($1.50/mo) records RF context.
- **Verizon money:** 1. Rescue before the emergency. Each silent lifeline caught means one proactive call instead of a post-incident escalation, a repeat truck roll (AICC: up to 3 h each, 25% no-access) or a regulator complaint.

Formula, every input a labeled assumption to replace with lab or Verizon data: rescues = migrated lines x heartbeat share x silent rate. For example, 100,000 migrated lines x 20% x 3% = 600 rescues. Value = rescues x (avoided repeat dispatch + retained line value at $45-55/mo).

2. Regulatory velocity. The 9-state WC 26-227 exit is paused (DA 26-1038). A batch-level record of 'heartbeat lines rescued and confirmed working after cutover' is the evidence that answers objections about vulnerable users. Copper savings arrive only when a CO empties: about $180M/yr from 36 COs (2022), partly from fewer dispatches.

3. Retention. Alarm dealers pick carriers, and in the 3G sunset 2.6M alarm sites had to move off Verizon's network. A carrier that tells the dealer 'your customer's panel went quiet after our cutover' keeps the line.

Cost: existing records, marginal compute, no hardware.
- **Dated anchor:** - Oct 15 2026: FCC 26-19 takes effect; it 'encourages' alarm-failure prevention with no defined test.
- Sept 28 2026: DA 26-1042, a Verizon transition failure caught by testing.
- Sept 25 2026: DA 26-1038 pauses WC 26-227.
- Nov 6 2026: 969 wire centers become eligible for retirement, two days before our video is due.
- Nov 16 2026: the WC 26-170 Frontier-area legacy-voice exit.
- **Primary data:** (1) Lab dataset: two weeks of call-cadence and retry signatures from real alarm, PERS and fax devices on Verizon's real copper-replacement path versus emulated copper, with six injected faults (missing ##21, SIA format, mains loss, antenna off, wrong receiver number, dead batteries). It is the first public measurement of how lifeline devices fail on Verizon's replacement and how fast a network-side detector would see it.

(2) Rutgers 'time-to-notice' baseline: aggregate supervision statistics from Rutgers' own supervised lifelines. These are counts and hours-to-restore only, with no personal data:
- missed-test and trouble events on the 450-building NAPCO StarLink fire communicators (Rutgers Emergency Services);
- Knightscope blue-light daily health reports (OIT and Public Safety).

(3) Optional: OIT CUCM call counts and periodicity for analog emergency and elevator ports, to test the detector on real institutional lines.
- **Demo:** Video beat (about 40 s): a national map of the 969 wire centers that become eligible on Nov 6 2026. Zoom to one and animate a batch cutover. A heartbeat raster of 2,000 lines shows 37 going dark. One row expands: 'Alarm line: test call every 6 h to receiver 1-8xx for 3 years. Since cutover: 0 calls. Likely cause: alarm number not programmed (##21). Call the customer and the dealer.' Cut to the bench: a time-lapse of a real panel on a real Verizon BPC100 missing its tests, with the detector firing at 1.5 periods.

Poster table: visitors delete the ##21 entry or pull the antenna. The worklist updates live while a 'naive monitoring' column stays green ('device online').
- **Why unique:** Only the carrier sees both sides of the cutover: the copper heartbeat and the replacement line's calls. It needs zero installs, covers every migrated line including owners who never buy anything, and generalizes to every lifeline type that heartbeats.

It complements device-side ideas in our pool (Kiss-Off Check, Plug-and-Prove) and does not duplicate them. Alarm central stations do notice missed tests on their own accounts, but they do not know a carrier cutover just happened, and many residential accounts test only monthly.

UNVERIFIED: whether Verizon already runs pre/post call-pattern checks internally. Ask a mentor before Nov 8.

RELATION TO THE CHAMPION: this is the strongest Verizon-only engine for Last Copper's Cutover Watch, and it could replace PROVE as the hero if Verizon judges value 'no hardware, every line' over the elevator story.
- **Biggest risk:** - CPNI optics after the upheld $46.9M fine. It must be framed as service assurance of the same service under 47 USC 222(c)(1)(A)/(d)(1): inside Verizon, call metadata only, no content or location, with counsel review.
- Elevator phones heartbeat only when a monitor runs auto-tests, so coverage is strongest for alarms and PERS.
- Students never touch real CDRs, so the demo is synthetic plus lab data.
- Verizon may say it already does this.
- Our pool's Kiss-Off Check covers the same failure from the device side; the orchestrator may merge them.

## I33: LIFELINE LAB: the Verizon-certified combinations for elevator phones leaving copper (lens extend_champion, pillar Pillar 1, AI-Driven Automation & Efficiency: automated, repeatable certification testing and scoring replace per-site trial and error. Pillar 3 is secondary., self-score 66)

**One-liner:** Elevator phones, gateways and answering services are each certified on their own, but nobody tests the combination after the copper is gone, and since Oct 15, 2026 the FCC no longer asks anyone to. LIFELINE LAB is an automated fault-injection rig at Rutgers. It runs real elevator phones (RATH, Viking, 2N/Wurtec) through Verizon's copper-replacement paths and publishes a pass/fail matrix: does HELP connect in both directions, do the location message and the Deaf text data survive, and does the phone's own failure light come on when the LTE link dies? Verizon gets a 'Lifeline Certified on Verizon' list its partners can sell from.

- **Problem evidence:** VERIFIED unless marked.

(1) Compatibility is now the owner's problem. FCC 26-19 (effective Oct 15 2026) removed the 'interoperability with key applications' showing (https://www.govinfo.gov/content/pkg/FR-2026-10-01/pdf/2026-20192.pdf). Verizon's BPC100 guide: 'Compatibility is determined by your testing ... at time of install' (https://www.verizon.com/content/dam/verizon/support/consumer/documents/phone/voice-connect-user-guide-2nd-gen.pdf).

(2) Known incompatibilities exist.
- RATH SmartView 2 (Deaf text/video retrofit) needs a line 'capable of supporting modem modulated devices', and AVIRE recommends its own LTE gateways as 'fully tested' (AVIRE datasheet, prior-art pass).
- Talkaphone says DTMF polling 'may not function as intended' over cellular, and its retrofit kit ships an AT&T SIM only (https://www.talkaphone.com/products/blue-light-phones/retrofit-kit/).
- The BPC100 supports Contact ID only.
- MarketSpark reports 'full bars' installs that still failed on RSRQ (https://www.marketspark.com/understanding-cellular-signal-quality/).

(3) A17.1 line verification never activates the voice link and may take 24 h (https://cstools.asme.org/csconnect/FileUpload.cfm?View=yes&ID=61439). Behind any gateway, the 'line' the phone checks is generated locally by the gateway's FXS port (standard ATA design). None of the datasheets we read says what each gateway does to dial tone and loop current when the LTE link drops. That gap is the lab's first measurement.

(4) Ooma at BOMA 2024 (vendor claim): about 50% of elevator lines don't work when replaced (https://buildings.com/industry-news/article/55126180/facing-the-copper-sunset-8-tips-to-choose-a-pots-replacement-boma-2024).

(5) NC State Construction Office: changing an elevator's phone service may trigger an A17.1-2019 communications upgrade (https://www.doa.nc.gov/sco-scc-2023-plain-old-telephone-service-pots-copper-telephone-line-replacement-service-requirements/open). NJ restored IBC 3001.2 (visual plus audible) for new and replaced cars from Aug 17 2026 (https://www.nj.gov/dca/codes/codreg/pdf_rule_adoptions/model_codes_2024_adopt.pdf).

(6) UK precedent: AVIRE SENTINEL sells managed lift-line migration with automated test calls ahead of the Jan 2027 switch-off (https://www.avire-global.com/en-uk/product/software/sentinel-lift-sentinel-lift-emergency-connection-service-connection-service/).
- **Verizon tech:** Paths under test, all on Verizon production:
- Business Phone Connect / BPC100 (Cat-4 HD VoLTE);
- One Talk;
- Verizon LTE Business Internet plus an ATA;
- POTSolve, if CSG lends a unit.

Rig:
- relay-driven fault injection: an RF step attenuator (0-40 dB at the antenna port), mains cut, battery pull, SIM pull, wrong programmed number;
- an Asterisk answering-point emulator with a 45 s redirect timer;
- AlarmReceiver and modem/text-path checks;
- automated scoring scripts.

The rig reports over a ThingSpace self-serve LTE-M line ($1.50/mo) and uses the Connectivity Management API (connection history, reachability callbacks) to timestamp radio events. The certification program is modeled on Verizon Open Development / Fast Track device certification (https://opendevelopment.verizonwireless.com/design-and-build/approved-modules), applied to life-safety combinations instead of modules. Roadmap: RedCap modules from the approved list.
- **Verizon money:** Channel pull-through:
- a published list of passing combinations lets CSG, MarketSpark, elevator OEMs and monitors sell Verizon as the primary path without re-testing at every site;
- fewer failed installs and second truck rolls;
- an answer to 'Verizon's own adapters aren't life-safety grade' (MarketSpark's public argument).

Sizing (labeled assumption): every 10,000 elevator lines kept on Verizon at $45-55/mo is $5.4-6.6M/yr.

Certification fees are possible, as with ODI, but small. Honest: the lab itself makes little money. It is a channel and risk tool, and it supplies the evidence for WHO ANSWERS and SILENT AFTER CUTOVER.
- **Dated anchor:** - Oct 15 2026: FCC 26-19 deletes the interoperability prong.
- Oct 23 2026: authorized date for the B1/key-system exit (DA 26-891).
- Aug 17 2026: NJ restores IBC 3001.2.
- Aug 2026: Rutgers' elevator standard names the vendors to test.
- Jan 31 2027: UK switch-off (the SENTINEL precedent).
- **Primary data:** The matrix itself, which only a lab team will produce:
- 3-5 used elevator phones (eBay or Rutgers surplus, matching Rutgers' approved vendors) x 2-3 Verizon paths x 8 faults;
- headline metric: minutes until each phone's own 'communications failure' indication fires after LTE loss, or 'never within 24 h'.

RSRQ/SINR in about 10 Rutgers elevator machine rooms (with a Facilities escort) sets realistic attenuation steps. Optionally, a Rutgers Emergency Services fire-alarm technician witnesses the alarm-path tests.
- **Demo:** Video beat (about 30 s): 'We cut the cell signal to an elevator phone on Verizon's copper replacement. Its own failure light stayed dark for __ hours' (state whatever we measure, even if it is 2 minutes). Then the matrix wall: green and red cells by phone and path.

Poster table: the rack. Visitors turn the attenuator knob and watch one matrix cell flip, with a QR code to the public matrix.
- **Why unique:** No public US combination matrix exists. Certification today is per product: UL/ASME for phones; PTCRB and Verizon ODI for gateways. This primary data is generated by us and is useful to Verizon, partners and AHJs.
