Other founders in our pool test residential BPC100 installs (Plug-and-Prove, Kiss-Off Check); this lab targets elevator phones and the code-mandated monitoring they rely on.

RELATION TO THE CHAMPION: this is the cheapest, most defensible primary-data engine for Last Copper. Even if the champion is replaced, the lab survives.
- **Biggest risk:** - Getting used elevator phones and a Verizon business line within 18 days.
- The result could be dull: if gateways drop loop current on LTE loss, phones notice promptly and the headline shifts to route and location.
- Vendors and Verizon may dislike a public 'fail' list, so publish to Verizon first.
- Elevator-literate judges will ask for UL-listed configurations.
- The demo is less emotional than a HELP press.

## I34: NOTICE ME: an opt-in lifeline registry, the UK telecare rule done voluntarily by Verizon (lens extend_champion, pillar Pillar 3, Next-Gen Products & Services (customer experience at a forced migration), with Pillar 1 automation of notice and sequencing., self-score 62)

**One-liner:** Since Oct 15, 2026, Verizon's copper-retirement notices must reach other carriers and 911 providers, but not the alarm company, elevator contractor or medical-alert firm whose device sits on the line. NOTICE ME lets those firms and building owners register the numbers they monitor. Verizon resolves each number to its real serving wire center and cutover date, sends direct notice, moves those lines first, and confirms each one works afterward. It is the UK's 'no telecare user migrated without a functioning solution' rule, offered by Verizon as a choice rather than a mandate, and it replaces FIND's CPNI inference with consent.

- **Problem evidence:** VERIFIED unless marked.

(1) The new 47 CFR 51.333(a) requires copper-retirement notice to interconnecting carriers and 911 service providers. It does not require notice to premises owners, elevator owners or alarm monitoring firms, and the objection-and-resolution procedure is gone (effective Oct 15 2026): https://www.govinfo.gov/content/pkg/FR-2026-10-01/pdf/2026-20192.pdf. DA 26-1061 (Oct 1 2026) opens master docket WC 26-214 for customer objections to technology-transition discontinuances: https://docs.fcc.gov/public/attachments/DA-26-1061A1.pdf

(2) Owners cannot tell from the notice whether they are affected.
- Notices list wire centers, while owners think in townships.
- Verizon's 2016 NJ notice shows the New Brunswick wire center serving Piscataway addresses.
- Numbers homed on the Piscataway 5ESS (PSWYNJPIDS5) can be served from the New Brunswick remote (NBWKNJNBRS2): https://localcallingguide.com/lca_prefix.php?switch=NBWKNJNBRS2
- Customers of CLECs on Verizon loops are told nothing by Verizon. The NJ notice says interconnecting entities must make their own arrangements: https://www.verizon.com/about/sites/default/files/verizon-new-jersey-copper-retirement-notice-2026-0908.pdf

(3) NJ already expects a notice for fire lines. NJ DCA Bulletin 11-2 says: 'Before the existing approved transmission service is disconnected, the MFVN provider must notify the subscriber that an approved fire alarm contractor must be present for the installation of the new service' (https://www.nj.gov/dca/codes/publications/pdf_bulletins/b_11_2.pdf). Meeting that requires knowing which lines feed fire dialers.

(4) The monitoring industry knows where the lifelines are. AICC counts about 5M dialer panels (FCC 26-19), elevator monitoring is concentrated (OEM call centers, Kings III), and FDNY logged 27,247 occupied entrapments in 2025.

(5) UK precedent.
- Feb 12 2025 Telecare National Action Plan: 'No telecare user will be migrated to digital landline services without the communication provider, the user, or the telecare service provider confirming that the user has a compatible and functioning telecare solution in place' (https://www.ispreview.co.uk/index.php/2025/02/new-gov-action-plan-to-support-vulnerable-users-in-uk-digital-phone-switchover.html).
- Landline providers signed data-sharing agreements with local authorities and telecare operators to find about 1.8M telecare users (Communications Council, Jun 3 2025, https://commscouncil.uk/press/pstn-switch-off-campaign).
- The switch-off is set for Jan 31 2027.

(6) The FCC paused Verizon's 9-state voice exit (DA 26-1038, Sept 25 2026). The specific vulnerable-user objections are UNVERIFIED because ECFS is blocked for us.
- **Verizon tech:** Registry API in CAMARA style: REST, with mTLS for bulk registrants such as monitoring centers. Behind it, Verizon's network inventory resolves number to serving switch and wire center, then to the copper-retirement and discontinuance schedule.

Other parts:
- direct-notice engine (email, API, webhook);
- a sequencing hook into the migration-order workflow ('registered lifeline: move first, never just cut');
- post-cutover confirmation by SILENT AFTER CUTOVER's heartbeat check or a WHO ANSWERS test call;
- replacement offers from the POTS Replacement Life & Safety tier (POTSolve).

Student build: a public-data resolver that combines LERG-derived switch and rate-center data (localcallingguide, telcodata) with our parsed CSV of 1,763 wire-center CLLIs from the 2026 notices. It openly shows its limit, that only Verizon's records give the true wire center. It also includes the registrant portal, the notice generator and a sequencing simulator.
- **Verizon money:** 1. Consent-based lead capture. Registrants are exactly the life-safety accounts at risk of leaving for cable VoIP or AT&T/T-Mobile kits, and registered lines get a Verizon offer first. Every 10,000 lines retained at $45-55/mo is $5.4-6.6M/yr (labeled assumption).

2. Regulatory velocity. A documented, consent-based notice and confirmation program answers vulnerable-user objections in WC 26-227 and WC 26-214. Copper savings (about $180M/yr from 36 COs in 2022) start only when the wire center empties.

3. Compliance. It helps satisfy NJ DCA Bulletin 11-2 notice expectations for fire-alarm lines.

Cost: a web service plus an inventory lookup. Honest: the value depends on enough monitoring firms registering.
- **Dated anchor:** - Oct 15 2026: 51.333 now requires notice to carriers and 911 providers only.
- Oct 1 2026: DA 26-1061 opens master docket WC 26-214.
- Sept 25 2026: DA 26-1038 pause.
- Jan 31 2027: UK switch-off under a confirm-before-migrate rule.
- **Primary data:** (1) Rutgers as registrant number 1. With OIT, Facilities and Emergency Services, compile Rutgers' life-safety line inventory (elevator, refuge, fire, blue light, pump-station RTUs) with carrier and line type. This answers Gate A.

(2) Resolve Rutgers' published legacy numbers, e.g. RUPD 732-932-7211 on PSWYNJPIDS5, and show why a number does not identify its wire center.

(3) With team and faculty approval, short structured interviews with the elevator contractor, monitor and fire-alarm monitoring firm that serve Rutgers: did any of them receive a copper or discontinuance notice?
- **Demo:** Video beat (about 30 s): a split screen. 'UK: no telecare user is moved until someone confirms it works.' 'US since Oct 15: notice goes to carriers and 911, not to you.' A monitoring firm uploads 500 numbers. The resolver returns per-number status (retiring copper, wire center, date / not Verizon / unknown, ask the carrier), and the auto-notice goes out. Rutgers appears as registrant number 1.

Poster table: a lookup kiosk with synthetic and public Rutgers numbers.
- **Why unique:** It turns the CPNI-radioactive FIND step into consent: the parties who already know which lines are lifelines tell Verizon. We found no US carrier offering this (UNVERIFIED beyond our limited search). The UK built the same idea through a charter and data-sharing agreements, so it has a working precedent.

RELATION TO THE CHAMPION: this should replace the FIND slide in Last Copper.
- **Biggest risk:** - Network effect: it only works if monitoring firms register.
- Verizon legal may fear liability ('we registered and you still cut it').
- The student resolver is approximate.
- It overlaps our pool's Ninety Days Out and Substantially Similar framing.
- The demo is less tactile.
- It is policy-heavy for network-planning judges.

## I35: SHARED FATE: find the lifelines that die with the campus network, and give them a Verizon path that doesn't (lens extend_champion, pillar Pillar 1, AI-Driven Automation & Efficiency: automated dependency-graph mining and outage replay. Pillar 2 is secondary (avoid over-building: only lines that share fate get a second path)., self-score 59)

**One-liner:** Rutgers already moved its fire communicators and blue lights off copper, and its desk phones to Cisco and Teams. Each such move trades the phone company's battery-backed loop for paths that may share fate with campus power and the campus network. Those went down for about 10 hours on Apr 27, about 15 hours from Aug 7 to 8, and about 6 hours on Oct 12, 2026. SHARED FATE maps every lifeline's path, replays each real outage to count which lifelines would have gone silent and for how long, and gives those lifelines an independent Verizon path. A nightly check proves the path really is independent.

- **Problem evidence:** VERIFIED unless marked.

(1) Rutgers' 2026 outages:
- Apr 27, 10:08 AM-8:06 PM: data-center power outage (https://it.rutgers.edu/alerts/2026/04/27/issues-with-network-connectivity-and-it-services/).
- Aug 7, 6:36 PM to Aug 8, 9:45 AM: a power problem took down RUWireless Secure, 'the wired network in some locations' and VPN (https://it.rutgers.edu/alerts/2026/08/07/some-it-services-unavailable-due-to-power-outage/).
- Oct 12, 8:09 AM-2:06 PM: network outage with no root cause published (https://it.rutgers.edu/alerts/2026/10/12/issues-with-network-and-access-to-it-services/).

(2) Rutgers' lifelines have already moved:
- NAPCO StarLink dual-path (IP plus cellular) fire communicators in 450 buildings, 2020 (https://www.prnewswire.com/news-releases/napco-security-technologies-announces-project-at-rutgers-university-301088901.html). Yet the March 2025 Division 13 standard still specifies a Silent Knight 5104 fed by two phone lines (https://construction.rutgers.edu/pdd/consultants-div13-spec-construction).
- Blue lights since 2023 send daily health reports and are monitored by Knightscope; their network type is UNVERIFIED (https://it.rutgers.edu/rutgers-next-gen-network/next-gen-network-enhancements/).
- Teams Phone leaves migrated desk phones able to call 911 only (https://it.rutgers.edu/voice/microsoft-teams-phone/).
- OIT runs 'analog and emergency' voice services on its own system. The path of elevator phones is UNVERIFIED.

(3) Code expects path independence for fire but not for elevators. NFPA 72-2019 requires that 'a single point of failure shall not cause more than a single path to fail' (https://www.redmond.gov/DocumentCenter/View/32016). A17.1 has no dual-path rule for elevator phones (none found).

(4) One shared dependency can take out many lifelines. At UAA, elevator phones, fire systems and blue lights all rode the same failing copper, found only by annual walks (https://www.thenorthernlight.org/stories/emergency-telephones-down-across-campus).

(5) The national push is toward enterprise IP. DA 26-1043 (Sept 28 2026) encourages TDM MLTS users to move to IP (https://docs.fcc.gov/public/attachments/DA-26-1043A1.pdf). FCC 26-19 (Oct 15 2026) removed the replacement-adequacy test. Every enterprise that absorbs lifelines into its own IP voice inherits shared fate with its IT network.
- **Verizon tech:** The independent path is Verizon LTE through the POTS Replacement Life & Safety tier (POTSolve) or Business Phone Connect, with a 24 h battery where specified. The nightly independence proof runs from a ThingSpace self-serve LTE-M probe and uses Connectivity Management callbacks. It checks that the lifeline's test call reaches the answering point while the campus path is deliberately bypassed: the probe verifies the arrival route and ANI at a Verizon-hosted test responder. For Rutgers fire, it confirms the StarLink cellular leg stays live when the IP leg fails.

The AI layer is a dependency graph (device, port, voice gateway or VG, CUCM/Teams, building UPS, campus core, data center, carrier trunk) built from OIT and Facilities inventories. Min-cut and outage replay on that graph produce, for each past outage, 'lifeline-minutes lost'. A ranked fix list then adds a Verizon path only where the cut set is shared.
- **Verizon money:** E&PS lines sold as the independent path for institutions (universities, hospitals, office campuses) that move lifelines onto their own IP voice. This is a recurring line per shared-fate lifeline, not a blanket replacement.

Sizing (labeled assumption): if a large campus has 200-400 lifelines and 20-30% share fate with campus IP or power, that is 40-120 Verizon lines at $45-55/mo, or about $22-79K/yr per campus.

Channel: Verizon E&PS higher-ed and healthcare accounts, with Rutgers (OIT and the CIO, who judged in 2025) as the reference.

Honest: the money is small per campus. The strategic value is a Verizon-specific argument against 'just put it on our Teams/Cisco system'.
- **Dated anchor:** - Apr 27, Aug 7 and Oct 12 2026: Rutgers outages, all publicly logged by OIT.
- Sept 28 2026: DA 26-1043 pushes MLTS to IP.
- Oct 15 2026: FCC 26-19 takes effect.
- Jan 5 2027: RUWireless Secure is turned off, the next forced campus cutover, so any lifeline-adjacent device on Wi-Fi must be re-checked.
- **Primary data:** (1) A lifeline path inventory from OIT and Facilities: which port, gateway, power and carrier each elevator phone, refuge call box, blue light and fire communicator uses. This is Gate A done properly.

(2) A replay of the three 2026 outages against that inventory: lifeline-minutes lost per outage.

(3) One witnessed test during a planned OIT maintenance window: does an elevator phone on an OIT analog port still reach its answerer when the campus core or CUCM is down (is SRST present)?

(4) Carrier and RSRQ for a sample of StarLink and blue-light units.
- **Demo:** Video beat (about 30 s): the OIT alert for Oct 12 2026 appears on screen ('8:09 AM'), then a campus map where lifeline icons go dark live as the outage replays, with the counter showing 'X lifeline-hours silent'. Toggle 'Verizon independent path' on for the shared-fate subset, and the map stays lit. Close on the nightly independence proof log.

Poster table: an interactive graph where visitors cut a node (a VG, a UPS, the core) and see which lifelines die.
- **Why unique:** It moves the champion's problem from 'Verizon's copper' to 'any cutover, including the campus's own'. Rutgers' exposure is real here even though its Verizon copper exposure is small, and the outage dates are public, post-June-2026 facts. No other founder in our pool uses Rutgers' own 2026 outages as the lifeline test case.

RELATION TO THE CHAMPION: this is the honest Rutgers-first story if Gate A fails.
- **Biggest risk:** - Needs OIT and Facilities to share path inventories within 18 days.
- The answer may be reassuring (StarLink is already dual-path, and elevator phones may sit on SRST-protected analog ports).
- Verizon's own outages (Jan 2026) weaken 'Verizon as the independent path', so the pitch must claim independence, not invulnerability.
- Small dollars per campus.
- It can read as a critique of OIT in front of the CIO judge.

## I36: LOOSE ENDS: find the dead drop before the city does (lens primary_data, pillar Pillar 1, AI-Driven Automation & Efficiency, with Pillar 2 cost reduction as the payoff., self-score 68)

**One-liner:** Every migration, disconnect and copper retirement leaves a cable hanging on a pole. Loose Ends puts AI on Verizon's own vehicle cameras to find low-hanging and disconnected Verizon drops and double poles, then bundles them into the same truck roll as the migration, before a city inspector writes a citation. NYC data already shows Verizon draws more than half of these citations.

- **Problem evidence:** PRIMARY ANALYSIS (ours, reproducible). (1) NYC DOT HIQA inspection data (ydkf-mpxb). From 2025-01-01 to 2026-09-30, DOT inspectors wrote 14,087 non-permit Corrective Action Requests (CARs) whose text cites 'unsecured' cable. 7,951 of them (56.4%) went to VERIZON NEW YORK INC., more than Cablevision/Optimum (4,376 across two entities), Spectrum (1,032) and Con Ed (701) combined. In Verizon's CAR text, 85% say 'low hanging', 60% say 'broken/disconnected', 63% mention fiber and 33% mention telephone cable. A typical record: 'unsecured, broken/disconnected Verizon 2-conductors telephone and telephone cables low hanging ... Please correct the issue for public safety.' Verizon's yearly count of these CARs: 1,009 (2021), 2,570 (2022), 3,546 (2023), 5,395 (2024), 4,822 (2025), 3,130 (Jan-Sep 2026), so about 400 a month continue. Across all DOT inspection outcomes since 2025, Verizon drew 10,421 CARs against 31,238 passes, about 24% of pass/CAR/NOV results. Con Ed drew about 9%. (2) NYC street permits (tqtj-sjs8): Verizon took 2,934 'REMOVAL OF POLES' permits (incl. protected) in NYC from Jan 2025 to Oct 2026, 4,491 pole-related permits in total, 2,482 of them in Queens. Pole cleanup is already a large recurring Verizon workload. CONTEXT (verified). Schulman, 1Q26 call (Apr 27, 2026): 'continuing to decommission legacy elements in the network, and that includes copper and recycling that copper and also monetizing that copper' (https://www.verizon.com/about/sites/default/files/2026-06/vz_1q26_transcript_042726rm.pdf). Verizon NJ notice 2026-03-A-NJ (Sept 8, 2026) retires copper at all remaining locations in 62 NJ wire centers, including Piscataway (https://www.verizon.com/about/sites/default/files/verizon-new-jersey-copper-retirement-notice-2026-0908.pdf). Since Oct 15, 2026 the FCC copper-retirement objection process no longer exists (FR 2026-10-01, https://www.govinfo.gov/content/pkg/FR-2026-10-01/pdf/2026-20192.pdf), so retirements and their dead drops will speed up. NJ A1454 (pre-filed for the 2026 session) would require attachment owners to remove attachments from double poles within 10 business days of an electric utility's notice (https://pub.njleg.gov/Bills/2026/A1500/1454_I1.HTM). The bill is pending. ComEd began piloting drone+AI pole inspection in Aug 2026, reporting 86% agreement with human inspectors (secondary source: https://hoodline.com/2026/08/comed-sends-drones-and-ai-to-hunt-down-rotting-poles-before-they-snap/). We found no Verizon pole-vision program. Verizon does buy visual-inspection AI for building facades (IBM Maximo case study, 2026: https://www.ibm.com/fr-fr/case-studies/verizon).
- **Verizon tech:** Verizon Connect Integrated Video and AI Dashcam, a Verizon Business product (ABI 2026 top video-telematics innovator; https://www.verizonconnect.com/resources/article/verizon-connect-named-top-video-telematics-innovator/). Cameras go first on Verizon's own technician vans, with an opt-in data program for municipal fleets on Verizon Connect. Clips upload over Verizon LTE/5G. An edge detector runs on the dashcam and a cloud classifier separates copper drop, fiber drop and double pole. The tool connects to Verizon's wireline OSP: aerial copper and fiber drops, the poles Verizon owns or jointly owns, and the copper-retirement and migration work-order flow. Optional: ThingSpace-connected tilt sensors on double poles that Verizon cannot clear right away.
- **Verizon money:** Cost of services ($27.8B FY2025) and the 2026 cost program (about $9B; preview at https://www.hudson-labs.com/research/verizon-q3-2026-earnings-preview-vz-results-and-guidance), which management says includes recycling and monetizing copper. The defensible number today is volume, not dollars: about 4,800 reactive unsecured-cable CARs a year in NYC alone, 1,649 Verizon NOVs since 2025, and about 1,500 Verizon pole removals a year in NYC. A labeled formula covers three sources of value. (a) Avoided reactive dispatches: CARs a year x cost per dispatch. Cost per dispatch is an ASSUMPTION of $200-400, which gives about $1-2M a year for NYC unsecured-cable CARs only. (b) Drops removed on the migration visit rather than a second trip during the copper exit: drops x avoided-trip cost. This is the large term, but the drop count is UNVERIFIED. (c) Copper recovered, plus avoided NOV fines and injury claims. Honest size: millions to low tens of millions a year at Verizon scale. It is not a $100M line.
- **Dated anchor:** NYC DOT records through Sept 30, 2026 show about 350-460 Verizon unsecured-cable CARs every month in 2026. Verizon NJ copper retirement notice: Sept 8, 2026. FCC 26-19 copper and discontinuance rules took effect Oct 15, 2026, with no objection process. ComEd's drone/AI pole pilot dates from Aug 2026. Schulman's 'monetizing that copper' comment was Apr 27, 2026 and the cost program was reaffirmed on Jul 24, 2026.
- **Primary data:** (1) The NYC mining above, already reproduced. We would extend it to NOV text, repeat locations, and blocks where an unsecured-cable CAR followed a Verizon pole or installation permit. (2) The first NJ baseline, since NJ publishes no inspection data. We would film 40-60 km of streets with a roof-mounted wide-angle phone or dashcam in the retiring Piscataway wire center (PSWYNJPI, around 4 Skiles Ave and Hoes Lane) and in New Brunswick (NBWKNJNB, not retiring) as a control. Each item gets labeled: low-hanging or disconnected drop, double pole, copper vs fiber vs coax. Target: 1,500 or more labeled poles, which also trains the detector. (3) OPRA requests to Piscataway Township and New Brunswick public works and police for low-hanging or downed telephone-wire and double-pole complaints from 2023-2026. (4) Mapillary CC-BY-SA imagery of the same streets for an older-vs-now comparison.
- **Demo:** VIDEO (2 min): 0:00 dashcam footage on Hoes Lane, Piscataway, with a box snapping onto a sagging disconnected drop, labeled 'Verizon copper drop, disconnected, 2.1 m clearance'. 0:20 the NYC number fills the screen: '7,951 city citations since Jan 2025, 56% of all of them'. 0:45 the map clusters 14 hazards on 3 streets into one work order scheduled with that block's fiber migration. 1:20 before/after photo, plus a counter of CARs avoided and copper recovered. 1:45 the ask: a 30-day pilot on 10 Verizon vans in one NJ wire center. POSTER TABLE: a tablet plays our Piscataway clips with live detections, a judge taps any NYC block to see its citation history, and a printed precision/recall table for the detector sits beside it.
- **Why unique:** No LLM-default team will mine NYC DOT inspection comments. The 56% figure exists only because we computed it. Outside-plant hygiene and the copper exit are day-to-day problems in GN&T, and the judges come from network planning. The idea is on no AVOID list. It builds on a Verizon product (Verizon Connect) and a stated Verizon goal (monetizing copper) instead of a student app.
- **Biggest risk:** (1) The CAR trend may track DOT enforcement intensity more than plant condition, and NYC is not NJ. Our NJ survey could find few hazards. (2) Imagery cannot reliably tell a dead cable from a live one, or Verizon's from another attacher's. Precision has to be measured and shown. (3) Forward-facing dashcams can miss overhead cables, so a wider or roof-mounted camera may be needed. (4) To Verizon judges this can read as a gotcha. It has to be framed as Verizon getting ahead of the city, not shaming Verizon. Lead-sheathed cable is a sensitive topic to avoid. (5) Faces and plates must be blurred, and municipal fleet video needs contractual consent. (6) The dollar value rests on an assumed cost per dispatch.

## I37: CLEARED TO CUT: address-level proof that the copper replacement works indoors (lens primary_data, pillar Pillar 1, AI-Driven Automation & Efficiency (automated replacement-readiness evidence and fix routing), feeding Pillar 2 copper-exit savings., self-score 67)

**One-liner:** From Oct 15, 2026 Verizon can win automatic approval to exit copper by certifying that every location has 5/1 Mbps mobile wireless, as shown on an outdoor-only map. Cleared to Cut measures that claim indoors, in basements and ground-floor shops, starting in Verizon's retiring Piscataway wire center, and turns each failure into a fix (FWA receiver at the window, Network Extender, small-cell ticket) before the cut. The FCC then sees evidence instead of objections.

- **Problem evidence:** FCC 26-19 (adopted Mar 26, 2026) accepts National Broadband Map mobile coverage at 5/1 Mbps as an adequate replacement: 'We decline to adopt additional verification requirements for the availability of mobile wireless service beyond the data reflected in the National Broadband Map' (https://docs.fcc.gov/public/attachments/FCC-26-19A1.pdf). The rules took effect Oct 15, 2026 (FR Oct 1, 2026: https://www.govinfo.gov/content/pkg/FR-2026-10-01/pdf/2026-20192.pdf). 47 CFR 1.7004(c)(5) requires those maps to 'depict outdoor coverage' only (https://www.ecfr.gov/current/title-47/chapter-I/subchapter-A/part-1/subpart-V/section-1.7004). Verizon's 9-state legacy-voice application, which includes Verizon New Jersey (DA 26-890, Aug 26, 2026, WC 26-227), was pulled from auto-grant on Sept 25, 2026 by DA 26-1038 (https://docs.fcc.gov/public/attachments/DA-26-1038A1.pdf). A secondary trade report says the pause followed comments from 'rural residents with spotty cell coverage' and medical-alert users (https://www.alarmgrid.com/blog/2026/10, UNVERIFIED counts). DA 26-1061 opened master docket WC 26-214, where any customer can object (https://docs.fcc.gov/public/attachments/DA-26-1061A1.pdf). MarketSpark (secondary) reports that NE and MN regulators questioned whether the map overstated alternatives in an earlier Verizon application (https://www.marketspark.com/major-carriers-retiring-copper-2026/). Precedent: after Sandy, Verizon's wireless Voice Link replacement on Fire Island and the NJ barrier islands was incompatible with 'fax machines, DVR services, credit card machines, some medical alert devices ... alarm systems', and Verizon rebuilt Fire Island with fiber (FCC 14-185, https://docs.fcc.gov/public/attachments/FCC-14-185A1.pdf). Verizon NJ notice 2026-03-A-NJ (Sept 8, 2026) covers 'all remaining locations' in 62 wire centers, including Piscataway PSWYNJPI (https://www.verizon.com/about/sites/default/files/verizon-new-jersey-copper-retirement-notice-2026-0908.pdf).
- **Verizon tech:** Verizon 5G (C-band Ultra Wideband and Nationwide) and LTE. Verizon FWA (5G/LTE Home and Business Internet) with window or outdoor CPE placement as the main fix. One Talk and Business Phone Connect (BPC100) as the voice replacement. Network Extender (current 2026 model UNVERIFIED). The product is an Android measurement agent (TelephonyManager RSRP/RSRQ/SINR, a 5/1 throughput test, and a VoLTE call test) plus a model that predicts indoor 5/1 probability for each address. Inputs are the BDC outdoor claim, NJ MOD-IV parcel attributes (building age, class, floors) and measured penetration loss. In production it would ingest Verizon's own planning and MDT data. The output is a per-address evidence card and an FWA or small-cell work order.
- **Verizon money:** Copper-exit savings and timing. The 2022 Investor Day reported about $180M a year of operating savings from 4.5M circuits and 36 converted COs (https://www.verizon.com/about/sites/default/files/2022-03/Investor-Day-2022-Transcript.pdf), roughly $5M per CO per year as an order of magnitude, labeled. Emptied COs become 'remote data centers' that 'sold out capability in 24 hours' (2Q26 call, Jul 24, 2026: https://www.verizon.com/about/sites/default/files/2026-08/vz_2q2026_transcript_072426rm.pdf). Value formula: months of approval delay avoided x per-CO savings x COs affected, plus FWA revenue from copper customers moved to FWA rather than lost to cable, minus fix costs. Honest: we cannot attribute the DA 26-1038 pause or its length to coverage. We claim only that address-level evidence lowers the chance of the next pause and of failed migrations.
- **Dated anchor:** The FCC 26-19 rules took effect Oct 15, 2026; the new 63.71(f)(2) 5/1 mobile-wireless certification and the removal of 63.602 come from them. DA 26-1038 (Sept 25, 2026) pulled Verizon's NJ application from auto-grant. DA 26-1061 (Oct 1, 2026) opened objection docket WC 26-214. Verizon NJ copper notice: Sept 8, 2026.
- **Primary data:** (1) Paired indoor and outdoor measurements at about 300 locations inside the Piscataway wire center, each with a 5/1 throughput test, RSRP/RSRQ/SINR and a 2-minute VoLTE call, on Verizon and on the other two carriers as a benchmark. Locations: students' own Piscataway apartments and houses (consented), public buildings, shops that give permission, and Rutgers Busch, Livingston and RBHS Hoes Lane ground floors and basements. (2) The BDC-claimed coverage for each measured location, giving claimed-vs-measured gap statistics. (3) A full read-through taxonomy of WC 26-227 and WC 26-214 comments, read in a browser because ECFS blocks scripted PDF fetches, classified into coverage, medical alert, alarm, fax, 911, price and other. (4) FWA fix trials: a 5G Home receiver at the window of each failing location, showing what fraction one cheap fix clears.
- **Demo:** VIDEO: 0:00 the FCC sentence 'We decline to adopt additional verification...' over an outdoor coverage map of Piscataway. 0:15 a student in a basement laundromat: the map says covered, the phone shows 3.1/0.4 Mbps and the call drops. 0:35 Cleared to Cut flags the address, the agent picks a fix, and a window FWA receiver measures 180/20. The evidence card turns green. 1:00 the roll-up (numbers to be measured): X% of indoor sites fail, Y% fixed by FWA, Z% need a small cell, mapped. 1:30 the Voice Link 2013 headline beside 'not this time: proven, address by address'. 1:50 the ask: run it on one NJ wire center before the WC 26-227 decision. POSTER: an interactive map that toggles 'what the FCC map claims' and 'what we measured', with the comment taxonomy beside it.
- **Why unique:** The 5/1 certification test and the end of verification took effect six days ago, so LLM-default teams will not know them. The pitch turns Verizon's biggest transformation program into an evidence problem that only boots on the ground in a specific retiring wire center can solve. It is distinct from Last Copper: it covers every copper location (homes, shops), not life-safety lines, and its output is an approval-and-retention evidence pack.
- **Biggest risk:** (1) Verizon already has MDT, planning tools and crowd data and may say 'we know'. Our edge is indoor ground truth at specific addresses, which is thin. (2) Piscataway is suburban with good C-band coverage, so the shock may not materialize. The fallback story is 'evidence to end the pause', which is weaker. (3) Measuring Verizon's gaps can look adversarial to Verizon judges, so it must be framed as internal pre-certification QA. (4) Students cannot know which addresses are actually still on copper, so the location sample is a proxy. (5) Overlap perception with Last Copper and the copper crowd (14 of 14 internal lenses landed on copper).

## I38: NOBODY TOLD US: the door-to-door cutover census and a one-photo migration concierge (lens primary_data, pillar Pillar 3, Next-Gen Products & Services to Enhance Customer Experience, with Pillar 1 automation of migration triage., self-score 63)

**One-liner:** We knock on 200 doors in the NJ wire centers Verizon is retiring and photograph what is really plugged into the copper (alarm panel, fax, card terminal, elevator phone, gate opener). From that we build an AI concierge: the customer snaps one photo, gets the Verizon replacement that actually works, and books it, so the cutover keeps the customer instead of handing them to cable.

- **Problem evidence:** The new 47 CFR 51.333 notice goes to interconnecting carriers and 911 service providers, not to end users, building owners or alarm companies. Effective Oct 15, 2026 (FR Oct 1, 2026: https://www.govinfo.gov/content/pkg/FR-2026-10-01/pdf/2026-20192.pdf). The briefing's founder check of eCFR found that the old 63.71(a)(6) customer-notice items (no line power, steps for safe use) are gone. Verizon may require customers to migrate 'well before' Dec 10, 2026 (notice 2026-03-A-NJ, Sept 8, 2026). DA 26-891 (Aug 26, 2026, WC 26-228): Verizon NJ applied to discontinue B1 business lines, PBX trunks, key systems, ISDN BRI and payphones, authorized on or after Oct 23, 2026. We found no pause notice for this docket, but grant status is UNVERIFIED. Verizon's own BPC100 replacement supports 'only the Contact-ID alarm protocol' and runs on 3 AA batteries (23 h standby) (https://www.verizon.com/business/shop/products/devices/connected-devices/inseego-business-phone-connect/). Voice Link precedent (FCC 14-185) lists fax, credit-card machines, medical alerts and alarms as incompatible. In the same record, a Public Knowledge survey found 24% of landline keepers kept the line for a medical alert and 17% for a security system. The AICC (May 10, 2021, https://tma.us/wp-content/uploads/2021/05/AICC-Petition-for-Emergency-Relief-5-10-21-FINAL.pdf) reported that migration truck rolls run up to 3 hours and that about 25% of appointments get no access. Schulman named friction in onboarding, billing and care calls as a churn cause (4Q25 call, Jan 30, 2026). E&PS revenue was $13.5B, down 4.8% in FY2025.
- **Verizon tech:** Business Phone Connect (Inseego BPC100, ATEL V810V/V810VD), One Talk, LTE/5G Business Internet, and Verizon's life-safety partners (CSG POTSolve, 'exclusive to Verizon'; MarketSpark). The concierge is a multimodal model that identifies device make and model from a photo and checks compatibility (alarm protocol, fax, line power, battery runtime) against Verizon's replacement catalog. After the cutover, a One Talk or BPC100 test call confirms each device works. A wire-center readiness dashboard serves Network Transformation.
- **Verizon money:** Retention of business wireline and E&PS customers at a forced-migration moment (E&PS $13.5B, -4.8%; business wireless churn 1.23% FY2025), plus fewer failed migrations and no-access truck rolls. The formula has labeled assumptions: copper business lines in a wire center x the share that would churn at cutover (MEASURED by our census's 'what would you do' and 'who would you call' questions) x ARPU x lifetime, plus repeat visits avoided x cost per visit. Honest: per-line revenue often falls when POTS becomes wireless, so this is retention and cost avoidance, not growth.
- **Dated anchor:** Notice 2026-03-A-NJ (Sept 8, 2026). DA 26-891 (Aug 26, 2026), authorized date Oct 23, 2026, two days from now. FCC 26-19 customer-notice changes effective Oct 15, 2026. DA 26-1038 pause (Sept 25, 2026) on the parallel residential/business voice docket.
- **Primary data:** (1) A structured census of 150-250 small businesses and institutions in Piscataway (PSWYNJPI) and the nearby retiring Metuchen, Dunellen and South River wire centers, with New Brunswick (not retiring) as the control. Targets: medical and dental offices, pharmacies, daycares, auto shops, houses of worship, and apartment buildings with elevators. We record awareness of retirement, number of copper lines, a photo of every device on each line, the monthly bill, and 'if forced to change, who would you call first?' (2) OPRA requests to Piscataway Township, Piscataway schools and the local MUA for Verizon invoices and line inventories (B1 lines, alarm dialers, pump-station autodialers). (3) An accompanied channel check: with an owner's permission and in person, ask a Verizon store or business rep what replaces their photographed devices, and score the answer against Verizon's own spec. We never pose as anyone. (4) A labeled photo set for the device classifier.
- **Demo:** VIDEO: 0:00 a Piscataway shop owner on camera: 'Nobody told me.' 0:15 a student photographs the alarm keypad by the phone jack. The concierge answers: 'Honeywell Vista-20P; if it reports in a non-Contact-ID format, BPC100 won't carry it; route to POTSolve; book before Dec 10.' 0:45 census results (to be measured): awareness %, device mix, % who would call cable first. 1:20 after the cutover, an automatic test call shows a green check. 1:40 a wire-center readiness bar for Network Transformation. POSTER: a judge photographs a sample device on the table (fax, keypad, card reader) and gets a verdict and the right SKU.
- **Why unique:** Door-to-door primary data in the exact wire centers Verizon is retiring, gathered by people who live there, is the purest form of data only Rutgers students can collect. It rests on rule changes six days old. The buyer and metric are Verizon Business retention, not life-safety compliance.
- **Biggest risk:** (1) The founder or judges may see it as a variant of Last Copper's FIND and MOVE. (2) Verizon already sends discontinuance notices and runs outreach, so the gap may be smaller than it looks. (3) A photo cannot reveal alarm reporting format or programming, so the verdict needs a follow-up question or test call. (4) Businesses may refuse, and with n of about 200 the confidence intervals are wide. (5) The channel check must not read as a gotcha to Verizon judges. (6) WC 26-228 grant status and the Dec 10 date may shift.

## I39: NEVER DIG ALONE: a joint-trench matcher for Verizon fiber and small cells, starting with Rutgers' 2027 trenches (lens primary_data, pillar Pillar 2, Cost & Sustainability Transformation (capex and restoration cost, fewer street cuts)., self-score 62)

**One-liner:** An AI agent reads every public street-opening permit, paving schedule and campus capital plan and tells Verizon's fiber and small-cell planners 'someone is opening your block in the next 90 days'. NYC data shows a quarter of Verizon's long trenches went into a block another utility had opened in the previous six months. The agent starts with the hot-water trenches Rutgers just approved on Busch and Livingston.

- **Problem evidence:** PRIMARY ANALYSIS (ours, NYC permits tqtj-sjs8). From Jan 2025 to Oct 20, 2026, Verizon New York pulled 8,906 street-opening permits (225,198 ft). 1,739 were 'MAJOR INSTALLATION - TELEPHONE', and 30.2% of those were on 'protected' streets (recently resurfaced, with costlier restoration). Of the 671 Verizon major installations of 100 ft or more (102,180 ft), 166 (24.7%, 25,600 ft) were on a block where Con Ed, National Grid, Spectrum, NYC DEP or others had pulled a major, main or conduit permit within the previous 180 days. Within plus or minus 180 days the figure is 35.6%. Across all 1,739 major installations it is 30.4%. DOT inspections since 2025 record 3,201 'Work Not Done, Permit Expired' outcomes on Verizon permits, showing planning churn. CONTEXT. GAO-12-687R (2012) on dig once (https://www.gao.gov/products/gao-12-687r) is cited for 25-33% urban construction savings. That percentage comes via secondary sources (e.g., https://insidetowers.com/cell-tower-news-dig-rights-way-critical-broadband-deployment) and the primary is UNVERIFIED. FHWA is cited as saying about 90% of right-of-way fiber cost is excavation and restoration (https://globalpolicy.ieee.org/wp-content/uploads/2017/10/IEEE17022.pdf, secondary). The Verizon-Corning deal (Sept 8, 2026) covers 80M+ miles of fiber for 2027-2032 toward 40-50M passings (https://www.verizon.com/about/news/verizon-corning-broadband-ai-fiber-agreement). Upper C-band Auction 115 is scheduled for Apr 27, 2027 (FCC 26-46; FR Aug 3, 2026: https://www.federalregister.gov/documents/2026/08/03/2026-15725/auction-of-flexible-use-licenses-in-the-upper-c-band-for-next-generation-wireless-services-scheduled). On Oct 1, 2026 the Rutgers BOG approved replacement of hot-water distribution piping on the Livingston and Busch campuses (https://www.dailytargum.com/article/u-board-of-governors-create-real-estate-degree-program-new-professorships-approve-campus-repairs-20261009). Rutgers owns 64 miles of underground conduit from RUNet 2000 (https://it.rutgers.edu/2025/08/06/from-runet-2000-to-the-next-gen-network-rutgers-journey-into-the-future-of-network-infrastructure/). Verizon's 2026 capex guide is $16.0-16.5B (Jul 24, 2026).
- **Verizon tech:** Verizon fiber builds (Fios FTTH, small-cell and C-band/upper C-band backhaul, AI Connect long-haul routes), Verizon OSP engineering, and the network-planning workflow. The agent combines (a) public permit and paving feeds (NYC DOT Socrata APIs today; NJ municipal and county permits via OPRA; NJDOT schedules), (b) an LLM that reads capital-plan and BOG documents for trench scope and dates, (c) street-segment entity resolution, and (d) a match score against Verizon's planned routes, measured C-band and mmWave capacity gaps (our RF logger along the Rutgers trench route), and protected-street calendars.
- **Verizon money:** Capex and restoration cost per foot and per passing ($16.0-16.5B 2026 capex guide; Corning-scale fiber program 2027-2032). Labeled formula: overlap feet x attainable share x cost per foot x savings fraction. In our NYC sample, 25,600 ft of long Verizon trenches in 21 months overlapped a recent utility trench. Applying a 25-33% urban savings figure (secondary-cited GAO) to an ASSUMED cost per foot gives hundreds of thousands to low millions of dollars for NYC alone. A national fiber and small-cell build could reach tens of millions a year if coordination is feasible. Honest: many Verizon cuts are customer-driven service trenches that cannot be planned months ahead, and joint trenching needs another party's schedule and separation rules.
- **Dated anchor:** Rutgers BOG approval of the Busch/Livingston hot-water piping replacement (Oct 1, 2026). Verizon-Corning fiber deal (Sept 8, 2026). Upper C-band Auction 115 announced Aug 3, 2026 for Apr 27, 2027. NYC permit data current to Oct 20, 2026. Capex guide reaffirmed Jul 24, 2026.
- **Primary data:** (1) The NYC permit and inspection overlap analysis above, already run, extended with the DOT resurfacing schedule (xnfm-u3k5) to count Verizon cuts made just after a block was repaved. (2) OPRA requests to Rutgers for the hot-water replacement's route, phasing and schedule at block level, and to New Brunswick and Piscataway for road-opening permits from 2024-2026 and 5-year paving plans. (3) Interviews with Rutgers IP&O Utilities and OIT about conduit fill and their policy on leasing conduit. (4) A walk-and-RF survey of the trench route measuring Verizon C-band and mmWave capacity gaps, so the match shows where Verizon actually needs fiber.
- **Demo:** VIDEO: 0:00 an excavator on Busch campus with the caption 'Rutgers is opening this street next year.' 0:15 a NYC map where red pairs light up: 'Con Ed opened this block 47 days before Verizon did, 166 times since 2025.' 0:45 the agent's alert card: block, other party, window, feet, protected-street flag, estimated savings. 1:15 the Rutgers trench route overlaid with measured C-band gaps and the Verizon conduit 'ride-along' proposal. 1:45 the ask: pilot the alert feed for one Verizon NYC construction region for 60 days. POSTER: an interactive map with window and feet sliders, so judges can test the matching themselves.
- **Why unique:** This is real Verizon-specific open-data analysis that no LLM-default team will run, plus a dated local asset (the Oct 1, 2026 BOG approval) that only a Rutgers team will turn into a Verizon fiber opportunity. It speaks the capex language of network planners and is on no AVOID list.
- **Biggest risk:** (1) Block-level matching is approximate: a permit is not the work, sides and depths differ, and gas and electric separation rules may block a shared trench. (2) NYC already has coordination processes, and Verizon may coordinate informally, so the novelty may be smaller. (3) Verizon's median major permit is 50 ft, mostly unplannable service cuts. (4) The Rutgers route may not go where Verizon needs fiber, and OPRA may withhold drawings for security. (5) Content scoring: judges may call it a planning tool rather than a use of network technology. (6) Dollar savings rest on secondary-cited GAO percentages and an assumed cost per foot.

## I40: PLACE-FIRST SAVE: send the fix, not the phone (lens primary_data, pillar Pillar 3, Next-Gen Products & Services to Enhance Customer Experience, with Pillar 2 cost (retention spend)., self-score 59)

**One-liner:** When a customer says 'no signal at home', retention often reaches for a new handset. Place-First Save uses consented, measured room-level evidence to decide whether the problem is the device or the place, then sends the cheapest fix that works (Wi-Fi Calling, Network Extender, FWA, or a small-cell ticket). It is proven first in Rutgers residence halls, where students are both the customers and the testers.

- **Problem evidence:** Schulman, 1Q26 call (Apr 27, 2026): 'So we just spent like $1,000 and did not solve the customer's issues. If we had listened and sent a femtocell to be installed at the house, we could have done that at one-third the cost' (https://www.verizon.com/about/sites/default/files/2026-06/vz_1q26_transcript_042726rm.pdf). FY2025 equipment was sold at about -$3.5B ($25.47B revenue vs $28.98B cost). Q2 2026 consumer promotional cost of retention fell about 17% and upgrades about 27% YoY (2Q26 call, Jul 24, 2026: https://www.verizon.com/about/sites/default/files/2026-08/vz_2q2026_transcript_072426rm.pdf). 'Every single basis point is 90,000 net adds', and friction in customer-service calls is a named churn cause (4Q25 call, Jan 30, 2026: https://www.verizon.com/about/sites/default/files/2026-02/vz_4q25_transcript_013026r.pdf). Network Extender historically sold for about $250 (RCR 2009/2016, https://rcrwireless.com/20160707/wireless/verizon-samsung-network-extender-tag17); the 2026 model and price are UNVERIFIED. Rutgers cannot fund in-building fixes: NB Housing's FY27 surplus fell to $93K and housing plant O&M rose 8.41% (https://finance.rutgers.edu/sites/default/files/2026-09/FY2027%20Financial%20Public%20Summary%20vFinal_0.pdf).
- **Verizon tech:** Network Extender (femtocell), Wi-Fi Calling, 5G Home/LTE Home FWA, in-building small cells, and RAN KPIs and MDT for the serving cells. The product is a triage model in care and retention flows that combines the customer's consented on-device measurements (a 48-hour logger: RSRP/RSRQ/SINR, handovers, call drops) with network KPIs for the address and outputs device vs place, the recommended fix, and the cost against a handset offer. After the fix, an automatic re-measurement closes the ticket.
- **Verizon money:** Promotional cost of retention and the equipment subsidy (equipment margin about -$3.5B FY2025), plus churn (1 bps = about 90,000 net adds). Labeled formula: coverage-driven retention contacts x share currently resolved with a handset x (handset cost minus fix cost), plus saves from fixes that actually work. Verizon does not disclose coverage-complaint volumes, so every input except our measured misrouting rate is an ASSUMPTION. Honest: the size is unknown until Verizon supplies volumes, but it maps directly onto a cost the CEO named.
- **Dated anchor:** 2Q26 results (Jul 24, 2026): promotional CoR down about 17% and upgrades down about 27%. Verizon reports Q3 2026 on Oct 26, 2026, before the video, so we would quote any retention update from that call. Rutgers FY27 budget (Jul 9, 2026) and the Sept 2026 public summary show no money for residence-hall coverage fixes.
- **Primary data:** (1) A consented survey of 300+ Rutgers students on all carriers covering building and floor, coverage problems, whether they contacted care, what was offered (new phone, credit, SIM swap, Extender, Wi-Fi Calling tip, nothing), outcome, and intent to switch. (2) 48-hour room logs from complainants' own phones, which give a device-vs-place ground truth. (3) A small set of documented, real care interactions by students about their own real problems: consented, no pretexting, and no subscriber data from Verizon. Possible shock (to be measured): the share of measured place problems that were offered a phone, against those offered a place fix.
- **Demo:** VIDEO: 0:00 a basement dorm room with one bar, and a care chat offering an upgrade. 0:20 a Schulman quote card: '$1,000 and did not solve the customer's issues'. 0:35 Place-First Save shows the room's 48-hour log and classifies 'place, not phone, 92%'. It proposes Wi-Fi Calling plus a Network Extender at about a third of the handset's cost, and a re-measure shows the fix holding. 1:10 survey results across Rutgers halls. 1:40 the ask: A/B the triage in one Verizon care queue. POSTER: the classifier running live on sample logs that judges pick.
- **Why unique:** It turns the CEO's own retention anecdote into a measurable care-and-network product, using data only students-as-customers can supply: their own complaints, offers and rooms. It sits between network planning and care, where the judges have influence.
- **Biggest risk:** (1) Overlap with AVOID-list indoor-coverage ideas (Dead Glass, Glass Ceiling) and the ScarletAgent concierge. (2) Verizon care may already check coverage before offering devices. (3) Sensitivity over location and CPNI after the upheld $46.9M fine; it must stay customer-initiated and consented. (4) Survey self-selection, and residence halls may already have DAS or good coverage. (5) The 2026 Network Extender model, price and availability are unverified. (6) The money cannot be sized without Verizon volumes.

## I41: WRONG ANSWER: the elevator phone thinks a recording is a person; Verizon's network knows it isn't (lens silent_failures, pillar Pillar 1, AI-Driven Automation & Efficiency: automated call-outcome classification replaces the yearly manual HELP check. Pillar 3 is secondary., self-score 68)

**One-liner:** Elevator phones, refuge call boxes and blue lights 'connect' to whatever picks up: voicemail, a 'not in service' recording, 911 instead of the 24-hour service, or last year's contractor. The code's line check is barred from placing the call that would notice. On Verizon's copper-replacement lines, the network already sees each HELP call's dialed number, whether a human answered and how fast. WRONG ANSWER turns that into a nightly pass/fail per lifeline, with no hardware in the car.

- **Problem evidence:** 1) Our own keyword classification of Chicago Building Violations open data (elevator codes EV1110/EV1111 with phone or two-way comments, Jan 1 2023 to Oct 20 2026; raw pull saved at scratchpad/gen4/silent_failures/chicago_phone.json) found 1,885 violations in 1,721 inspections.
- 111 inspections (6.4%) cite a WRONG ANSWERER: 104 phones programmed to 911 instead of a 24-hour answering service, 4 reaching voicemail or an answering machine, and 4 reaching a previous or wrong maintenance provider.
- 158 inspections (9.2%) cite a wrong answerer OR a missing or wrong car-ID/address message. 55 of the 379 phone-cited inspections in 2026 alone fall in that group.
- Examples: 2026-08-31 'CURRENTLY DIALING PREVIOUS MAINTENANCE PROVIDER'; 2026-10-09 'THEIR PROMPT IS STATING THAT THE CALL IS COMING FROM NASHVILLE, TN'; 2024-01-04 '(NOT ANSWERING MACHINE OR VOICE MAIL)'.
- Passing inspections are not in the dataset, so this is not a failure rate. https://data.cityofchicago.org/resource/22u3-xenr.json
2) Every one of those phones passes the code's line check. ASME A17.1 2.27.1.1.6 line verification 'shall not require activation of the two-way communication link(s)', and a failure may go 24 h before it is signalled (Interpretations 13-319 and 13-343). https://cstools.asme.org/csconnect/FileUpload.cfm?View=yes&ID=61439
3) A17.1 requires a 24-hour staffed answerer, 'no automated answering', and redirect of an unacknowledged call after 45 s (Kings III/ASHB code guide, 2023-09). https://www.ashb.com/wp-content/uploads/2024/08/IS-2024-115.pdf
4) Elevator phones cannot tell a recording from a person. Viking's K-1600-EHFA has 'advanced call progress detection' and hangs up on 'CPC, silence, busy signal, dial tone, time-out or touch tone command', rolling to the next number. Nothing documents recognising an intercept recording or voicemail. Inference: a recording keeps the phone 'connected'. https://vikingelectronics.com/products/k-1600-ehfa/
5) The same failure class covers refuge call boxes. IBC 1009.8.1: where the control point is not constantly attended, the elevator-landing two-way system 'shall have a timed automatic telephone dial-out capability to a monitoring location or 9-1-1' (code text as reproduced by Kings III; NJ adopted the 2024 IBC on Aug 17 2026, but whether NJ amended 1009.8 is UNVERIFIED). https://www.kingsiii.com/code-compliance/international-building-code/
6) Rutgers:
- The Facilities Service Call Center (848-445-1234) is staffed 7:00 am to 4:30 pm only, so any lifeline or posted after-hours number that lands there reaches no one at night (inference). https://ipo.rutgers.edu/university-facilities/operations-services
- Rutgers' August 2026 elevator standard names three phone vendors (Avire, Wurtec, Kings III) and three installers, and does not say who answers. https://ipo.rutgers.edu/pdd/appendix_07_elevator_escalator_design_guidelines
- RUPD's published 732-932 number sits on Verizon's Piscataway 5ESS (PSWYNJPIDS5), in a wire center under retirement notice. https://localcallingguide.com/lca_prefix.php?switch=PSWYNJPIDS5
7) Verizon's own transitions produce wrong answerers, and testing is what catches them:
- DA 26-1042 (Sept 28 2026): during Verizon's NG911 Phase 1, MLTS 911 calls went to a national call center instead of the PSAP. Verizon found this on Jan 12 2026 in First Office Application testing. https://docs.fcc.gov/public/attachments/DA-26-1042A1.pdf
- Verizon's business 911 support page lists '9-1-1 call routes to a Call Relay Center instead of the local PSAP' as a trouble the customer reports after the fact. https://www.verizon.com/business/support/product-support/products/wireline-911-location-management/
- **Verizon tech:** Network-side checks on lifeline-flagged Verizon voice lines: One Talk ($20/line), Business Phone Connect (Inseego BPC100) and CSG POTSolve ('exclusive to Verizon', on a Verizon wireless business internet plan).
(a) Signalling only, on EVERY call including real emergencies, with no audio touched:
- dialed number vs the owner's registered answering-point list (catches 911-instead-of-service and the old contractor);
- the SIP final response (404/410 vacant, 486 busy, 408/480 no answer);
- answer supervision (200 OK) and seconds-to-answer against A17.1's 45 s;
- early-media Special Information Tone (SIT) detection, which is a standard feature of carrier and enterprise session border controllers. AudioCodes documents 'SIT Event Detection' that maps the 913.8/1370.6/1776.7 Hz tone families to call outcomes. https://techdocs.audiocodes.com/session-border-controller-sbc/mediant-500-sbc/user-manual/version-740/Content/UM/SIT%20Event%20Detection.htm
(b) Media classification (human / voicemail beep / IVR / recording) ONLY on scheduled weekly test calls placed at 3 AM with no passenger present. Features only, no recordings.
(c) Verdicts and tickets flow through ThingSpace Connectivity Management callback URLs to the owner, contractor or Verizon. https://thingspace.verizon.com/documentation/apis/connectivity-management.html
Students never see CPNI; the production version runs inside Verizon on lines whose owners opt in.
- **Verizon money:** Mainly Enterprise & Public Sector retention (E&PS revenue $13.5B in FY2025, down 4.8%), not new revenue.
- Verizon's POTS Replacement page sends elevator and fire-alarm lines to partners. MarketSpark ships dual SIMs across Verizon, AT&T and T-Mobile (https://www.marketspark.com/?p=11809), and the Talkaphone blue-light retrofit kit ships AT&T-only SIMs.
- 'Only the carrier can prove who answered' is a reason to keep a lifeline single-carrier on Verizon.
- Price anchors: RATH SmartView Hub is $9.99/device/month and the BPC100 retails at $129.99. WRONG ANSWER could be a roughly $2-3/line add-on to the life-safety tier.
CFO formula, every input an ASSUMPTION: retained lifeline lines x monthly line revenue x 12. For example, 50,000 lines x $30 = $18M/yr of revenue protected, plus 50,000 x $2.50 x 12 = $1.5M/yr of add-on revenue.
Risk value for Network Transformation: when WC 26-228 business lines (B1, PBX trunks) and the Piscataway copper move, any lifeline whose programmed destination changes or goes vacant is caught that night, not at next year's inspection. Honest size: small direct dollars; the argument is retention and avoided incidents.
- **Dated anchor:** - DA 26-1042 (Sept 28 2026): Verizon's own cutover sent 911 calls to the wrong center, caught only by testing.
- FCC 26-19 rules effective Oct 15 2026 (91 FR 62334): the replacement-interoperability test (former 63.602) is removed.
- DA 26-891 / WC 26-228: Verizon NJ business lines (B1, PBX trunk, key system), authorized on or after Oct 23 2026.
- Chicago citations dated Aug 31 2026 ('dialing previous maintenance provider') and Oct 9 2026 ('Nashville, TN').
- Rutgers' elevator standard, August 2026.
- **Primary data:** 1) Sticker census: photograph every posted emergency or after-hours number in about 40 buildings (elevator lobbies, refuge areas, lab corridors, parking decks, pools).
- Classify each number by carrier and switch with LERG data (Verizon PSWYNJPIDS5 retiring TDM, CLEC, wireless, ported).
- At 2 AM, test only the non-dispatch numbers that advertise 24/7: human, voicemail, recording or no answer; seconds to a human; and whether the answerer knows which Rutgers building it is. Never 911 or RUPD dispatch lines.
2) 'Who answers' registry: ask Facilities, with an OPRA backup filed now (7-business-day clock), for the programmed destination of every elevator, IBC 1009.8 refuge and blue-light line.
3) Pre-announced HELP-press audit with Facilities on 10-20 cars, shared with the champion.
4) Done already: the Chicago reclassification (111 of 1,721 inspections), which no other team will have.
- **Demo:** BENCH: a real hands-free elevator phone (Viking K-1600 class, used, about $150-300) on a Verizon BPC100. HELP is pressed four times, to four destinations:
(1) a teammate, who answers;
(2) a voicemail box;
(3) a number we cancelled yesterday, which plays the carrier recording;
(4) an IVR labelled 'old contractor'.
The phone's own 'connected' indicator lights for all four. Our console, an FXO tap plus a Pi running SIT, voicemail-beep and speech classifiers and the call log, shows 1 PASS (human, 6 s) and 3 FAIL, each with a reason and a ticket routed to the right owner.
VIDEO BEAT (20 s): split screen, 'Phone: 4 of 4 connected' against 'Network: 1 of 4 reached a person', then the Chicago number (111 inspections) and 'the code's line check never places this call'.
POSTER TABLE: visitors press HELP, pick a destination card and watch the verdict.
- **Why unique:** - The A17.1 line check is barred from activating the link.
- Elevator phones roll over on busy or no answer but accept a recording as an answer.
- Gateways (POTSolve, MarketSpark, AirDial) supervise power, battery and signal, not who answered.
- Only the carrier sees the destination and outcome of every HELP call without touching the elevator.
Distinct from the champion's PROVE (an in-car acoustic sensor): this is the zero-install network layer, and it covers refuge boxes, blue lights, pool and garage phones too. It is not on the AVOID lists, and no other founder's idea classifies the far end of lifeline calls. The Chicago reclassification and the Rutgers sticker census are data LLM-default teams will not have.
- **Biggest risk:** - Overlaps the champion's 'answer and redirect time' check; the orchestrator may merge it as PROVE's network tier.
- Real classification needs Verizon IMS/SBC access; the demo uses a bench tap.
- Rutgers may not release programmed destinations before Nov 8.
- Some monitoring vendors may already verify answer type (UNVERIFIED).
- Direct revenue is small.

## I42: QUIET LINE: every migrated lifeline has a heartbeat in Verizon's own call records; flag the ones that stop after the cutover (lens silent_failures, pillar Pillar 1, AI-Driven Automation & Efficiency: change-point and periodicity detection on migrated lines, matched to a labelled failure library., self-score 61)

**One-liner:** Alarm communicators place a test call every 24 hours, elevator lines run scheduled tests, and fax lines have a handshake rhythm. Verizon already holds that heartbeat for every line it migrates off copper. QUIET LINE learns each line's pre-cutover fingerprint and watches for 30 days after. A test call that stops, durations that collapse into retry storms, or new failure codes become a ticket the same week, with no device at the customer. Its failure-signature library comes from lifeline gear we break on purpose on a real Verizon line.

- **Problem evidence:** 1) FCC 26-19 (adopted Mar 26 2026; copper and discontinuance rules effective Oct 15 2026):
- removed the adequate-replacement test, including 'interoperability with key applications and functionalities';
- told device makers they 'have been on notice for almost a decade';
- only 'encourage[s] carriers to ensure their IP networks are appropriately configured so as to prevent alarm signaling failures'.
https://docs.fcc.gov/public/attachments/FCC-26-19A1.pdf ; effective date https://www.govinfo.gov/content/pkg/FR-2026-10-01/pdf/2026-20192.pdf
2) Scale: FCC 26-19 quotes AICC that about five million installed alarm panels still use dialer protocols. In 2021 AICC said each device migration is a truck roll of up to 3 h and about 25% of appointments end without access. https://tma.us/wp-content/uploads/2021/05/AICC-Petition-for-Emergency-Relief-5-10-21-FINAL.pdf
3) Failures are silent and the replacement is narrow:
- Verizon's BPC100 supports only the Contact ID alarm protocol; other central-station protocols are 'not supported'. https://www.verizon.com/business/shop/products/devices/connected-devices/inseego-business-phone-connect/
- ADT admits customers who did not respond at the 3G sunset suffered 'loss of signal', after about $292M of conversions. https://www.sec.gov/Archives/edgar/data/1703056/000170305623000046/adt-20221231.htm
4) Approval risk is live:
- DA 26-1038 (Sept 25 2026) pulled Verizon's 9-state legacy-voice discontinuance, NJ included, from auto-grant. https://docs.fcc.gov/public/attachments/DA-26-1038A1.pdf
- DA 26-1061 (Oct 1 2026) opened master docket WC 26-214, where any customer can object to a technology-transition discontinuance. https://docs.fcc.gov/public/attachments/DA-26-1061A1.pdf
5) Inside Verizon nothing watches the heartbeat. Verizon's POTS Replacement page offers no line test or end-to-end verification feature (text search of the page, Oct 21 2026). https://www.verizon.com/business/products/voice-collaboration/plain-old-telephone-service-replacement/
- **Verizon tech:** - Verizon's own call-detail and IMS records for migrated lines, joined to Network Transformation cutover dates per line.
- Per-line periodicity (autocorrelation of call times), destination, duration and final-response features; a change-point detector over a 30-day post-cutover window; nearest-signature matching against our lab library.
- Covers lines landing on Business Phone Connect, One Talk or POTSolve.
- Output: per-line tickets and a per-wire-center 'day-14 nothing-broke' certificate. This is Cutover Watch without any hardware.
- Legal basis is use of the customer's own records to provide and protect the service it buys (47 USC 222(c)(1)(A), (d)(1)). It runs inside Verizon, and students never see CPNI.
- This is the champion's FIND run in reverse: FIND finds lifelines before the cut; QUIET LINE proves they survived it.
- **Verizon money:** It protects the copper-retirement program and lowers its cost of service.
- Verizon's copper-to-fiber migration yielded about $180M/yr of operating savings from 36 COs, partly through fewer dispatches (Investor Day 2022). https://www.verizon.com/about/sites/default/files/2022-03/Investor-Day-2022-Transcript.pdf
- The 2026 cost program names copper decommissioning and monetization (Q1 2026 call). https://www.verizon.com/about/sites/default/files/2026-06/vz_1q26_transcript_042726rm.pdf
- Each discontinuance delayed by objections (WC 26-214) or paused (DA 26-1038) defers those savings and the CO-to-data-center conversions Schulman described in July 2026.
- It also avoids repeat truck rolls, because the ticket names the likely fault before dispatch.
CFO formula, inputs are ASSUMPTIONS: (silent failures caught x escalation-and-repeat-dispatch cost avoided) + (days of discontinuance delay avoided x daily program savings). Verizon does not disclose truck-roll cost, so this stays a labelled formula.
- **Dated anchor:** - FCC 26-19 effective Oct 15 2026 (no replacement test; carriers only 'encouraged' to prevent alarm-signalling failures).
- DA 26-1038 (Sept 25 2026) pause.
- DA 26-1061 (Oct 1 2026) customer-objection docket WC 26-214.
- DA 26-891 / WC 26-228: NJ business lines, on or after Oct 23 2026.
- **Primary data:** A failure-signature library that Verizon does not have: what a broken lifeline looks like in a call record. We put real gear on a real Verizon BPC100 line and on an analog reference line:
- a Contact ID alarm panel with its 24-h test timer (used, about $100);
- a voice autodialer;
- a hands-free elevator phone;
- a fax machine.
We inject about 8 fault classes: receiver number not keyed into the gateway, an SIA-format panel, antenna removed, battery-only operation, DTMF distortion, vacant destination, busy receiver, and a gateway factory reset. Each attempt is logged as a CDR-like row (time, duration, retries, outcome), giving a few hundred labelled rows by Nov 8.
We also file an OPRA request for Rutgers' Verizon invoices to find remaining Verizon lines and their service addresses.
- **Demo:** VIDEO BEAT: a 'heartbeat monitor' wall of 200 synthetic migrated lines in a Piscataway-like wire center. Cutover happens at day 0. Three traces flatline or turn ragged. The agent writes: 'Line 7 (alarm, Contact ID): 24-h test call stopped after cutover; matches lab signature receiver-not-keyed (0.93); send config fix, no truck.'
The live part on the bench is our real alarm panel on the BPC100 producing the ragged retry pattern on cue.
POSTER TABLE: visitors pull a fault card, and the heartbeat on screen changes shape within one test cycle.
- **Why unique:** - It needs nothing installed at the customer, and it works on 100% of migrated lines.
- Only Verizon holds the before-and-after records.
- The labelled failure signatures come from a student lab that broke real lifeline devices on a Verizon line, which no LLM-default team will produce.
- Distinct from the tactile founder's Kiss-Off Check (device-side acknowledgement) and Plug-and-Prove (install-time self-test), and from the champion's FIND (pre-cutover).
- Not on the AVOID lists.
- **Biggest risk:** - Verizon may already run post-migration call-pattern checks (UNVERIFIED).
- Counsel may limit the use of call records.
- The wire-center demo is synthetic; only the bench is real.
- Some lifelines (elevator phones) call too rarely to have a heartbeat, so it pairs with WRONG ANSWER's scheduled tests.
- Savings cannot be sized from public data.

## I43: THIRTY MINUTES: Verizon must tell 911 centers within 30 minutes when its network fails; nobody tells the elevator (lens silent_failures, pillar Pillar 1, AI-Driven Automation & Efficiency: automatic impact mapping and notification. Pillar 3 (customer experience for enterprise lifeline customers) is secondary., self-score 62)

**One-liner:** When a network goes down, the lifelines riding it go silent and no one is told. That happens when Rutgers' own network fails (Oct 12, 6 h; Aug 7-8, 15 h) and when Verizon's does (Jan 2026, Aug 2026). Verizon already runs a 30-minute outage-notice process for 911 and 988 centers. THIRTY MINUTES extends it to the owners of lifeline lines: which elevator phones, alarm paths and call boxes are dark, what to do now, and a re-tested all-clear when they are back. A $1.50/month LTE-M 'witness' in each building closet reports the campus network's own outages over Verizon.

- **Problem evidence:** 1) DA 26-1033 (Sept 25 2026): 'Providers must notify 911 and 988 special facilities about such an outage as soon as possible, but no later than 30 minutes after the discovery of the outage', with a follow-up within 2 hours. No equivalent duty exists toward the owners of the elevator, alarm or call-box lines in the footprint. https://docs.fcc.gov/public/attachments/DA-26-1033A1.pdf
2) Rutgers' 2026 outages, from OIT's own alerts:
- Oct 12, 8:09 AM to 2:06 PM, network down, root cause unpublished. https://it.rutgers.edu/alerts/2026/10/12/issues-with-network-and-access-to-it-services/
- Aug 7, 6:36 PM to 9:45 AM Aug 8, a power problem took down RUWireless Secure and the wired network in some locations. https://it.rutgers.edu/alerts/2026/08/07/some-it-services-unavailable-due-to-power-outage/
- Apr 27, 10:08 AM to 8:06 PM, a data-center power outage. https://it.rutgers.edu/alerts/2026/04/27/issues-with-network-connectivity-and-it-services/
Any lifeline riding campus IP lost that path for those hours.
3) Rutgers lifelines with an IP leg:
- 450 buildings got NAPCO StarLink IP-plus-cellular fire communicators in 2020. https://www.prnewswire.com/news-releases/napco-security-technologies-announces-project-at-rutgers-university-301088901.html
- The blue lights send daily health reports to one vendor, Knightscope (OIT page modified 2026-06-04). https://it.rutgers.edu/rutgers-next-gen-network/next-gen-network-enhancements/
Nobody has published how many lifeline-hours each outage cost.
4) Codes tolerate long silence:
- A17.1 allows up to 24 h before a dead elevator line is signalled (Interp. 13-343). https://cstools.asme.org/csconnect/FileUpload.cfm?View=yes&ID=61439
- NFPA 72 multi-path failure reporting is within 6 h (edition-dependent per the digest; the NJ edition is UNVERIFIED), so a 6-hour outage can pass with no report.
5) Verizon outages in 2026:
- Jan 14-16 nationwide software outage, potentially about 2M customers. https://www.cnet.com/tech/mobile/verizon-network-outage-jan-2026-cause/
- Aug 9-10 Southern California fiber vandalism, phones showing SOS. https://abc7.com/post/vandalized-fiber-lines-cause-verizon-service-outage-thousands-southern-california-customers/19653900/
- National Law Review (Apr 23 2026): elevator phones and fire-panel communicators 'increasingly rely on wireless networks or IP pathways. If these pathways fail without redundancy, life safety compliance can be jeopardized.' https://natlawreview.com/article/when-network-goes-dark-why-communication-outages-are-becoming-critical-risk-cre
6) This is why buyers choose multi-carrier: MarketSpark, Verizon's own life-safety partner, ships dual SIMs including AT&T and T-Mobile. https://www.marketspark.com/?p=11809
- **Verizon tech:** - Verizon's existing outage detection and its DA 26-1033 PSAP-notification workflow.
- Network inventory mapping each lifeline-flagged line (ThingSpace, One Talk, BPC100, POTSolve) to its serving sites and core elements.
- ThingSpace Connectivity Management reachability, connection history and callback URLs. https://thingspace.verizon.com/documentation/apis/connectivity-management.html
- For campus outages, an LTE-M 'witness' (ThingSpace self-serve, $1.50-$6/month) in each telecom closet. It watches the local LAN path and reports over Verizon while the campus network is down.
- Verizon IoT Network Continuity eSIM (flyer created Oct 8 2026) for automatic primary/backup switching. https://www.verizon.com/business/resources/flyers/iot-network-continuity.pdf
- On restoration, each line is re-tested (WRONG ANSWER's test call) and gets a signed all-clear receipt.
- **Verizon money:** 1) Retention: answers the main argument for splitting lifelines across rival carriers, 'one carrier can go down'. A Verizon-only lifeline with a 30-minute owner notice and proof of restoration keeps the whole line on Verizon instead of on a dual-SIM kit that also pays AT&T or T-Mobile.
2) E&PS sales: LTE-M witnesses and LTE backup lines for buildings whose lifelines share fate with the campus network. Witness lines are $1.50-$6/month. LTE backup prices are UNVERIFIED (search snippets only).
3) Defensive: NJ A5548 (introduced Sept 24 2026, pending) would require automatic outage credits, with penalties of $1,000 per affected customer and up to $25,000 per outage. The same per-line impact map computes exactly who was affected. https://pub.njleg.state.nj.us/Bills/2026/A6000/5548_I1.HTM
Honest size: small per-line revenue. The value is trust and retention at the moment customers decide between single- and multi-carrier lifelines.
- **Dated anchor:** - DA 26-1033 (Sept 25 2026).
- Rutgers outages Oct 12 2026 and Aug 7-8 2026.
- Verizon's Aug 9-10 2026 fiber-vandalism outage.
- NJ A5548 (Sept 24 2026).
- IoT Network Continuity flyer (Oct 8 2026).
- **Primary data:** 1) Outage natural experiment: the three 2026 Rutgers outages already ran the drill, and nobody has counted the result.
- Ask Rutgers Fire Safety, with an OPRA backup, for the central station's trouble and restoral log for the 450 StarLink communicators during Apr 27, Aug 7-8 and Oct 12: how many reported IP-path failure, how many lost both paths, and for how long.
- Ask for Knightscope's daily blue-light health reports for the same dates.
- Walk down with Facilities which lifelines ride campus IP or campus power.
- Output: 'lifeline-hours lost' per 2026 outage, the first such number for any campus.
2) Deploy 10 LTE-M witnesses in student-accessible closets and labs for 14 days to measure baseline reachability.
- **Demo:** VIDEO BEAT: replay Oct 12 on a Busch map. At 8:09 AM the campus core goes down; witnesses flip in seconds; '14 lifelines single-path, 3 dark'; the owner notice arrives at T+4 min. Then switch to 'Verizon sector outage': notice at T+30 min listing affected cars and alarm paths, with a fallback instruction. On restoration, the re-test receipts turn green.
POSTER TABLE: a mini closet rack. Visitors unplug the Ethernet uplink; the LTE-M witness texts the 'building owner' phone on the table within a minute.
- **Why unique:** - Carriers notify PSAPs, not lifeline owners.
- A campus cannot measure its own outage with the network that is down.
- It reuses a process Verizon already runs for regulators.
- The outage-log natural experiment is Rutgers-only primary data.
- Distinct from LastPath (room-level 911 survivability) and Measured Primary (carrier choice). Not on the AVOID lists.
- **Biggest risk:** - Framing near Verizon's own 2026 outages could land badly with Verizon judges (Gen 3's 'Heard' suffered from this); present it as 'extend what Verizon already does for 911 centers'.
- OPRA may withhold alarm logs under security exemptions, or arrive too late.
- Mapping lines to sites is easy for Verizon, so our value is the design, the witness and the data.
- Revenue is small.

## I44: SAME ADDRESS: a lifeline keeps its location when it leaves copper, and Verizon notices when the box moves (lens silent_failures, pillar Pillar 1, AI-Driven Automation & Efficiency: an automated location-parity gate in the migration workflow, plus nightly anomaly detection., self-score 56)

**One-liner:** Verizon's own 911 support page says 'the enterprise customer is the source of truth' for location, and lists 'call routes to a Call Relay Center' and 'civic address is incorrect at the PSAP' as troubles reported after a real call. SAME ADDRESS makes Verizon a second witness. At cutover it diffs the old copper line's 911 location against the new registration and the label the lifeline announces, and blocks the order on a mismatch. Every night after, it compares each fixed cellular box's radio fingerprint against its registered address and flags drift.

- **Problem evidence:** 1) Verizon's Business wireline 911 support page: 'The enterprise customer is the source of truth.' Its listed troubles to report include '9-1-1 call routes to a Call Relay Center instead of the local PSAP' and 'Civic address is incorrect at the PSAP'. Customers gather the 'Full address displayed at PSAP' afterwards, so detection is reactive (page undated, fetched Oct 21 2026). https://www.verizon.com/business/support/product-support/products/wireline-911-location-management/
2) DA 26-1042 (Sept 28 2026): Verizon 'does not have the MLTS customer's location information needed to route 911 calls', so those calls go to a national call center where the caller must state the location. https://docs.fcc.gov/public/attachments/DA-26-1042A1.pdf
3) Verizon's Voice Connect (BPC100) guide: 'Moving the device could impact 911 capabilities and signal strength ... the device is the property of Verizon and must stay within your home.' https://www.verizon.com/content/dam/verizon/support/consumer/documents/phone/voice-connect-user-guide-2nd-gen.pdf
4) Labels drift in the field. In our Chicago reclassification, 75 of 1,721 phone-cited inspections since 2023 concern a missing or wrong car-ID or address message, e.g. 2026-10-09 'NASHVILLE, TN' and 2026-08-12 'PROGRAM EMERGENCY PHONE TO GIVE UNIQUE ELEVATOR IDENTIFICATION'. https://data.cityofchicago.org/resource/22u3-xenr.json
5) The cutover moment: new 47 CFR 51.333(a) (effective Oct 15 2026) requires notice to 911 service providers but not to building or elevator owners. https://www.govinfo.gov/content/pkg/FR-2026-10-01/pdf/2026-20192.pdf
IBC 1009.8 requires 'written identification of the location' posted beside each elevator-landing two-way device, so there is a physical label to compare against.
- **Verizon tech:** 1) At conversion: Verizon's own ALI/service-address record for the copper line, compared with the dispatchable location registered for the One Talk, BPC100 or POTSolve replacement (NG911 PIDF-LO per DA 26-1042 Phase 2) and the lifeline's announced label. Any loss of detail (e.g. 'ELEV 2 FL 3' becoming a street address only) or any changed address holds the cutover order.
2) Nightly: a home-radio fingerprint for each fixed Verizon cellular endpoint, built from serving and neighbour cells, timing advance and RSRP, using Verizon RAN data or ThingSpace Device Diagnostics (cell ID and RF for LwM2M-certified modules). A model flags when a box has moved, or when many boxes registered to one headquarters address attach across several campuses.
https://thingspace.verizon.com/documentation/apis/device-diagnostics/getting-started.html
- **Verizon money:** Mostly risk and cost, not revenue:
- fewer 911 misroute and wrong-address incidents during the WC 26-228 business-line conversions and the NG911 transition, at a time when the FCC is already scrutinising Verizon's transitions (DA 26-1038, DA 26-1042);
- recovery of Verizon-owned Voice Connect boxes that leave the home ($129.99 retail each);
- detection of boxes used off-premise (fraud).
As a Business feature, location parity can be bundled into Verizon's wireline 911 location management for Kari's Law and RAY BAUM'S compliance, a reason to choose Verizon's replacement over a cable VoIP line.
CFO formula, inputs are ASSUMPTIONS: incidents avoided x cost per incident + boxes recovered x $129.99. Honest size: small.
- **Dated anchor:** - DA 26-1042 and DA 26-1043 (Sept 28 2026): missing MLTS location sends calls to a national center.
- FCC 26-19 rules effective Oct 15 2026: notice to 911 providers, not owners.
- Chicago label citations dated Aug 12 2026 and Oct 9 2026.
- WC 26-228 (on or after Oct 23 2026).
